-category of functors from
\mathsf{Mfd}
to
\mathscr{C}
which preserves transverse pullbacks and the terminal object.

Remark 4.2
.

It turns out one does not need to demand that
i
be fully faithful, and that it is automatically satisfied.

4.2.
An algebro-geometric description

Of course, such a definition is not very tractable, however there is the following remarkable connection between derived manifolds and
\mathbf{C}^{\infty}
-algebras:

Theorem 4.3
.

[
11
, Theorem 5.3]

For all
\infty
-categories
\mathscr{C}
with finite limits, composition with the canonical functor
\mathbf{C}^{\infty}\to\mathsf{Mfd}\to\mathsf{DMfd}
induces an equivalence of
\infty
-categories

\operatorname{Fun}^{\mathbf{lex}}\left(\mathsf{DMfd},\mathscr{C}\right)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{-\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu-\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu-\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu-\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu-\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu-\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu\longrightarrow}}\mathbf{Alg}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}\left(\mathscr{C}\right).

In light of
2.28
, there is the following corollary, which provides a concrete model for derived manifolds:

Corollary 4.4
.

There is a canonical equivalence of
\infty
-categories

\mathsf{DMfd}\simeq\left(\mathbf{Alg}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}\left(\mathcal{S}\mskip-3.0mu\operatorname{pc}\right)^{\mathbf{fp}}\right)^{op}

between the
\infty
-category of derived manifolds and the opposite of the
\infty
-category of finitely presented
\mathbf{C}^{\infty}
-algebras in
\mathcal{S}\mskip-3.0mu\operatorname{pc}.

We wish to develop a theory of derived geometry that also incorporates supermanifolds, as such, we want a supergeometric analogue of the above. Upon careful analysis of the proofs in
[
11
]
, such a result will follow essentially verbatim from the even case once two technical facts are established. The first has already been proven, namely Lemma
3.4
. The second fact we need is that the the canonical morphism of algebraic theories

\mathbf{SCom}_{\mathbb{R}}\to{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}

is unramified. We postpone this to Theorem
5.22
. However, we record the result for derived supermanifolds here:

Definition 4.5
.

The
\infty
-category
\mathsf{DSMfd}
of derived supermanifolds is the (essentially unique)
\infty
-category with finite limits, together with a functor
i:\mathsf{SMfd}\to\mathsf{DSMfd}
which preserves transverse pullbacks and the terminal object, such that for all
\infty
-categories
\mathscr{C}
with finite limits, composition along
i
induces an equivalence of
\infty
-categories

\operatorname{Fun}^{\mathbf{lex}}\left(\mathsf{DSMfd},\mathscr{C}\right)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{-\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu-\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu-\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu-\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu-\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu-\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu\longrightarrow}}\operatorname{Fun}^{\pitchfork}\left(\mathsf{SMfd},\mathscr{C}\right)

between the
\infty
-category of finite limit preserving functors from
\mathsf{DSMfd}
to
\mathscr{C}
and the
\infty
-category of functors from
\mathsf{SMfd}
to
\mathscr{C}
which preserves transverse pullbacks and the terminal object.

Theorem 4.6
.

For all
\infty
-categories
\mathscr{C}
with finite limits, composition with the canonical functor
{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}\to\mathsf{SMfd}\to\mathsf{DSMfd}
induces an equivalence of
\infty
-categories

\operatorname{Fun}^{\mathbf{lex}}\left(\mathsf{DSMfd},\mathscr{C}\right)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{-\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu-\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu-\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu-\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu-\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu-\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu\longrightarrow}}\mathbf{Alg}_{{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}}\left(\mathscr{C}\right).

Corollary 4.7
.

There is a canonical equivalence of
\infty
-categories

\mathsf{DSMfd}\simeq\left(\mathbf{Alg}_{{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}}\left(\mathcal{S}\mskip-3.0mu\operatorname{pc}\right)^{\mathbf{fp}}\right)^{op}

between the
\infty
-category of derived supermanifolds and the opposite of the
\infty
-category of finitely presented
{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}
-algebras in
\mathcal{S}\mskip-3.0mu\operatorname{pc}.

The upshot is the universal property of derived (super)manifolds leads us naturally to the study of derived algebraic geometry over
\mathbf{C}^{\infty}
-(super)algebras. Put another way, derived differential (super)geometry is precisely the derived algebraic geometry of
\mathbf{C}^{\infty}
-(super)algebras!

4.3.
A geometric description

There is an alternative more down to earth description of the
\infty
-category of derived manifolds. It turns out this
\infty
-category arises naturally as a localization of the
1
-category of non-positively graded manifolds. More specifically, let
f:\mathcal{M}\to\mathcal{N}
be a map of dg-manifolds (or dg-supermanifolds). We say

Definition 4.8
.

i)

f
is a
fibration
if it is a submersion of underlying graded (super)manifolds, or equivalently, if the induced
\mathbf{End}\left(\mathbb{R}^{0|1}\right)
-equivariant map
\mathfrak{M}\to\mathfrak{N}
is a submersion of supermanifolds.

ii)

f
is a
weak equivalence
if

f^{*}:\mathbf{C}^{\infty}\left(\mathcal{N}\right)\to\mathbf{C}^{\infty}\left(\mathcal{M}\right)

is a weak equivalence of dg-
{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}
-algebras, i.e. of underlying cochain complexes.

Consider the canonical functor of
1
-categories

\displaystyle\mathbf{C}^{\infty}:\mathsf{dg}\mbox{-}\mathsf{Mfd}_{\leq 0}

\displaystyle\to

\displaystyle\left(\underline{\mathbf{dg}\mathbf{C}^{\infty}\mathbf{Alg}}_{\leq 0}\right)^{op}

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}

\displaystyle\mapsto

\displaystyle\mathbf{C}^{\infty}\left(\mathcal{M}\right)

It sends weak equivalences to quasi-isomorphisms, hence there is an induced functor between
\infty
-categories

\mathbf{C}^{\infty}:\mathscr{DgMfd}_{\leq 0}\to\mathsf{dgC}^{\infty}\mathsf{Alg}_{\leq 0}^{op},

where
\mathscr{DgMfd}_{\leq 0}
is the
\infty
-category obtained by formally inverting the weak equivalences of dg-manifolds, up to homotopy.
By
[
8
, Lemma 6.1]
, the essential image lies in the homotopically finite generated
\mathbf{C}^{\infty}
-algebras, and hence by Corollary
4.7
, there an induced functor

\Psi:\mathscr{DgMfd}_{\leq 0}\to\mathsf{DMfd}

from the
\infty
-category of non-positively graded dg-manifolds to the
\infty
-category of derived manifolds.

Theorem 4.9
.

[
8
, Theorem 6.11]

The functor
\Psi:\mathscr{DgMfd}_{\leq 0}\to\mathsf{DMfd}
is an equivalence of
\infty
-categories.

The extension of the proof of this fact to dg-supermanifolds is trivial and the details are left to the reader. We record it here:

There is a well-defined functor

\Psi:\mathscr{DgSMfd}_{\leq 0}\to\mathsf{DSMfd}

from the
\infty
-category of non-positively graded dg-supermanifolds to the
\infty
-category of derived manifolds.

Theorem 4.10
.

The functor
\Psi:\mathscr{DgSMfd}_{\leq 0}\to\mathsf{DSMfd}
is an equivalence of
\infty
-categories.

Given this, much of this manuscript can be rephrased using the language of dg-manifolds. We hope to do so in the future.

5.
Homotopical
\mathbf{C}^{\infty}
-algebras

Definition 5.1
.

We denote the
\infty
-category
\mathbf{Alg}_{{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}}\left(\mathcal{S}\mskip-3.0mu\operatorname{pc}\right)
by
\mathbf{d}{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}\mathbf{Alg}
.

By Theorem
2.29
, any
{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}
-algebra in the
\infty
-category
\mathcal{S}\mskip-3.0mu\operatorname{pc}
of spaces can be modeled by a simplicial
{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}
-algebra in sets. Since any
{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}
-algebra has an underlying
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-graded abelian group, namely its even and odd additive groups, such a simplicial algebra has an underlying simplicial abelian group of even and odd elements respectively. It follows each of the respective underlying simplicial sets are Kan complexes. There is a model structure on the category of
{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}
-algebras in
\mathrm{Set}^{\Delta^{op}}
such that:

i)

f:X\to Y
is a
weak equivalence
if both

f\left(\mathbb{R}\right):X\left(\mathbb{R}\right)\to Y\left(\mathbb{R}\right)

and

f\left(\mathbb{R}^{0|1}\right):X\left(\mathbb{R}^{0|1}\right)\to Y\left(\mathbb{R}^{0|1}\right)

are weak homotopy equivalences of simplicial sets and

ii)

f:X\to Y
is a
fibration
if both maps above are Kan fibrations of simplicial sets.

The
\infty
-category associated to this model category is equivalent to
\mathbf{Alg}_{{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}}\left(\mathcal{S}\mskip-3.0mu\operatorname{pc}\right).
Having this model structure at hand will sometimes be useful for concrete arguments and calculations.

5.1.
Differential Graded
\mathbf{C}^{\infty}
-algebras

Let a
\mathbf{C}^{\infty}
-algebra
\mathcal{A}
(in
\mathcal{S}\mskip-3.0mu\operatorname{pc}
) be represented by a simplicial
\mathbf{C}^{\infty}
-algebra
X.
As discussed,
X
has the underlying structure of a simplicial abelian group. The classical Dold-Kan correspondence associates to this simplicial abelian group a cochain complex. In fact, this abelian group is an
\mathbb{R}
-module, so this becomes a cochain complex over
\mathbb{R}.
One may wonder if one can transfer the
\mathbf{C}^{\infty}
-algebra structure to this cochain complex.

Definition 5.2
.

A
differential graded
\mathbf{C}^{\infty}
-algebra
(dg-
\mathbf{C}^{\infty}
-algebra), is a commutative differential graded
\mathbb{R}
-algebra
\left(\mathcal{A}^{\bullet},d\right),
together with the additional structure of a lift of the induced commutative
\mathbb{R}
-algebra structure on
\mathcal{A}_{0}
to the structure of a
\mathbf{C}^{\infty}
-algebra. A morphism
f:\left(\mathcal{A},d\right)\to\left(\mathcal{A}^{\prime},d^{\prime}\right)
between two such algebras is a morphism of differential graded
\mathbb{R}
-algebras such that the morphism
f_{0}:\mathcal{A}_{0}\to\mathcal{A}^{\prime}_{0}
is a morphism of
\mathbf{C}^{\infty}
-algebras.

Similarly, a
differential graded
\mathbf{C}^{\infty}
-superalgebra
(dg-
\mathbf{C}^{\infty}
-superalgebra), is a commutative differential graded
\mathbb{R}
-superalgebra
\left(\mathcal{A}^{\bullet},d\right),
(here each
\mathcal{A}_{i}
is additionally
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-graded) together with the additional structure of a lift of the induced supercommutative
\mathbb{R}
-algebra structure on
\mathcal{A}_{0}
to the structure of a
{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}
-algebra. A morphism
f:\left(\mathcal{A},d\right)\to\left(\mathcal{A}^{\prime},d^{\prime}\right)
between two such algebras is a morphism of differential graded
\mathbb{R}
-algebras such that the morphism
f_{0}:\mathcal{A}_{0}\to\mathcal{A}^{\prime}_{0}
is a morphism of
{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}
-algebras.

Theorem 5.3
.

[
9
, Theorem 6.5]

There exists a cofibrantly generated, almost simplicial, model category structure on the category of dg-
\mathbf{C}^{\infty}
-algebras, unique with the property that

f:\left(\mathcal{A},d\right)\to\left(\mathcal{A}^{\prime},d^{\prime}\right)

is a weak equivalence (respectively fibration) if and only if the induced map of underlying cochain complexes is, with respect to the projective model structure on
\mathbf{Ch}_{\mathbb{R}},
and similarly for the category of dg-
\mathbf{C}^{\infty}
-superalgebras, but with the category
\mathbf{Ch}^{\bullet}_{\mathbb{R}}
replaced with that of
\mathbf{Ch}^{\bullet}\left(\mathbf{Vect_{\mathbb{R}}^{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}}\right)
— the category of cochain complexes of
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-graded real vector spaces. Moreover, there are induced model structures on non-positively (and non-negatively) graded
\mathbf{C}^{\infty}
-(super)algebras.

Definition 5.4
.

Denote by
\mathbf{dg}\mathbf{C}^{\infty}\mathbf{Alg},

\mathbf{dg}\mathbf{C}^{\infty}\mathbf{Alg}_{\leq 0},

\mathbf{dg}\mathbf{C}^{\infty}\mathbf{Alg}_{\geq 0},
the
\infty
-categories associated to the above model categories of appropriately bounded
dg
-
\mathbf{C}^{\infty}
-algebras. Similarly, denote by
\mathbf{dg}{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}\mathbf{Alg},

\mathbf{dg}{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}\mathbf{Alg}_{\leq 0},

\mathbf{dg}{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}\mathbf{Alg}_{\geq 0},
the analogously defined
\infty
-categories of differential graded
{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}
-algebras.

Remark 5.5
.

A dg-
\mathbf{C}^{\infty}
-superalgbera
\left(\mathcal{A},d\right)
has both a
\mathbb{Z}
-grading and a
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-grading, and they need not be compatible!

Notice that for any dg-
\mathbf{C}^{\infty}
-superalgbera
\left(\mathcal{A}^{\bullet},d\right),
there is an underlying cochain complex of super vector spaces
\mathcal{A}^{\bullet}.
This produces a forgetful functor

\mathbf{dg}\mathbf{C}^{\infty}\mathbf{Alg}\to\mathbf{dg}\mathsf{SVect},

with a left adjoint
\mathsf{Sym}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}.

Recall that if
V^{\bullet}
is a
\mathbb{Z}
-graded vector space, and for each
i,
we have a given basis
\left(e_{i}^{\alpha}\right),

\mathsf{Sym}\left(V^{\bullet}\right)
can be described as the free graded commutative algebra with a generator of degree
i
for each basis element
e_{i}^{\alpha}.
If each
V^{i}
is additionally
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-graded, each of these generators is appropriately even or odd. If
\left(V^{\bullet},d\right)
is then a cochain complex, we can describe each linear map

d_{i}:V_{i}\to V_{i+1}

in terms of the basis elements as a matrix
M^{i},
where, using Einstein’s summation convention,

d_{i}\left(e_{i}^{\alpha}\right)=M^{i}_{\alpha\beta}e^{\beta}_{i+1}.

\mathsf{Sym}\left(\left(V^{\bullet},d\right)\right)
can then be described as
\mathsf{Sym}\left(V^{\bullet}\right)
equipped with the differential

(3)

D:=M^{i}_{\alpha\beta}e^{\beta}_{i+1}\frac{\partial}{\partial e^{\alpha}_{i}}.

Similarly,
\mathsf{Sym}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}\left(V^{\bullet}\right)
is the free dg-
{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}
-algebra on the variables
e^{\alpha}_{i}.
If
V^{\bullet}=V
is concentrated in degree
0,
and has superdimension
n|m,
then

\mathsf{Sym}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}\left(V\right)\cong\mathbf{C}^{\infty}\left(\mathbb{R}^{n|m}\right).

On the other extreme, if
V^{0}=0,
then

\mathsf{Sym}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}\left(V^{\bullet}\right)\simeq\mathsf{Sym}\left(V^{\bullet}\right).

Combining these two facts implies that in general

\mathsf{Sym}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}\left(V^{\bullet}\right)\simeq\mathsf{Sym}\left(\underset{i\neq 0}{\bigoplus}V^{i}\left[-i\right]\right)\underset{S\left(V^{0}\right)}{\otimes}\mathbf{C}^{\infty}\left(V_{0}\right).

For example, if
W^{0}=\mathbb{R}^{n|m},

W^{-1}=\mathbb{R}^{k|l},

W^{-2}=\mathbb{R}^{a|b},
and all other components zero, then

\mathsf{Sym}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}\left(W^{\bullet}\right)\cong\mathbf{C}^{\infty}\left(\mathbb{R}^{n|m}\right)\underset{\mathbb{R}}{\otimes}\mathbb{R}\left[x_{1},x_{2},\ldots,x_{k},y_{1},y_{2},\ldots y_{l},z_{1},\ldots,z_{a},w_{1},\ldots w_{b}\right],

with each
x_{i}
in cohomological degree
-1
and even in its
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-grading, each
y_{i}
in cohomological degree
-1
and odd in its
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-grading, each
z_{i}
in cohomological degree
-2
and even in its
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-grading, and each
w_{i}
in cohomological degree
-2
and odd in its
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-grading.
\mathsf{Sym}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}\left(W^{\bullet}\right)
is the free
\mathbb{Z}
-graded
{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}
-algebra on
n
even generators of degree
0,

m
odd generators of degree
0,

k
even generators of degree
-1
etc.

For a cochain complex
\left(V^{\bullet},d\right),
the underlying graded commutative algebra of
\mathsf{Sym}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}\left(\left(V^{\bullet},d\right)\right)
is
\mathsf{Sym}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}\left(V^{\bullet}\right),
and the differential is given by the same formula (
3
).

Finally, we note that in the projective model structure, every cochain complex is cofibrant, so the left derived functor of
\mathsf{Sym}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}
is itself.

Definition 5.6
.

A morphism
f:\left(\mathcal{A},d\right)\to\left(\mathsf{B},d\right)
between dg-
{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}
-algebras is
quasi-free
if there exists a
\mathbb{Z}
-graded super vector space
W^{\bullet}
such that
f
is isomorphic to the inclusion of
\mathcal{A}
into
\mathcal{A}\underset{\mathbb{R}}{\otimes}\mathsf{Sym}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}\left(W^{\bullet}\right),
after forgetting the differentials.

Proposition 5.7
.

[
9
]

A morphism
f:\left(\mathcal{A},d\right)\to\left(\mathsf{B},d\right)
between non-positively graded differential
{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}
-algebras is a cofibration if and only if it is a retract of a quasi-free extension.

Cofibrations between non-connective dg-
{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}
-algebras are slightly more subtle:

Proposition 5.8
.

[
9
]

A quasi-free extension
f:\left(\mathcal{A},d\right)\to\left(\mathsf{B},d\right)
dg-
{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}
-algebras is a cofibration if and only if it admits an exhaustive filtration

\mathcal{A}=\mathcal{B}_{-1}\subset\mathcal{B}_{0}\subset\mathcal{B}_{1}\subset\cdots\mathcal{B}_{k}\subset\cdots

by sub-algebras such that for all
k\geq 0,

1.

\mathcal{B}_{k}
is obtained from
\mathcal{B}_{k-1}
by adjoining a set of generators
\{x_{k,\alpha}\}_{\alpha\in I_{k}},
i.e. the filtration is by quasi-free extensions, and

2.

d\left(x_{k,\alpha}\right)\in\mathcal{B}_{k-1}
for all
\alpha\in I_{k}.

This implies that the cofibrant objects in the model category
\mathit{dgSC^{\infty}Alg}_{\leq 0}
are retracts of quasi-free dg-
\mathbf{C}^{\infty}
-superalgebras by
[
9
, Corollary 6.20]
. In particular, each algebra
\mathbf{C}^{\infty}\left(\mathbb{R}^{p|q}\right),
regarded as a dg-
{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}
-algebra is cofibrant, and the collection of these algebras is closed under coproducts, and hence is also closed under derived
\mathbf{C}^{\infty}
-tensor products, as they coincide in this case. It follows that the canonical functor

\displaystyle{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\to

\displaystyle\left(\mathbf{dg}\mathbf{C}^{\infty}\mathbf{Alg}_{\leq 0}\right)^{op}

\displaystyle\mathbb{R}^{p|q}

\displaystyle\mapsto

\displaystyle\mathbf{C}^{\infty}\left(\mathbb{R}^{p|q}\right)

preserves finite products. Hence, by Theorem
2.15
, we deduce that there is a unique colimit preserving functor

N_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}:\mathbf{d}{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}\mathbf{Alg}\to\mathbf{dg}\mathbf{C}^{\infty}\mathbf{Alg}_{\leq 0}

which sends each algebra of the form
\mathbf{C}^{\infty}\left(\mathbb{R}^{p|q}\right)
to itself. By the adjoint functor theorem, it follows there exists a right adjoint
\Gamma^{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}
. In fact, it follows from the Yoneda lemma that
\Gamma^{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}
sends a dg-
{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}
-algebra
\mathcal{A}
to

\displaystyle\Gamma^{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}\left(\mathcal{A}\right):{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\to

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}\mskip-3.0mu\operatorname{pc}

\displaystyle\mathbb{R}^{p|q}

\displaystyle\mapsto

\displaystyle\mathbb{M}\mathrm{ap}_{\mathbf{dg}\mathbf{C}^{\infty}\mathbf{Alg}_{\leq 0}}\left(\mathbf{C}^{\infty}\left(\mathbb{R}^{p|q}\right),\mathcal{A}\right).

Theorem 5.9

(The
\mathbf{C}^{\infty}
Dold-Kan Correspondence)
.

[
14
, Corollary 2.2.10]

The above adjunction
is an adjoint equivalence of
\infty
-categories.

The above theorem is a major convenience as it allows us to use techniques from homological algebra in our study of derived differential geometry.

5.2.
Localizations of homotopical
{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}
-algebras

Definition 5.10
.

Let
\mathcal{A}
be a homotopical
\mathbf{C}^{\infty}
-algebra and let
a\in\pi_{0}\left(\mathcal{A}\right)
. We say that a map
f:\mathcal{A}\rightarrow\mathsf{B}
such that
f\left(a\right)\in\pi_{0}\left(B\right)
is invertible is a
localization of
A
with respect to
a
if for each
\mathcal{C}\in\mathbf{Alg}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}\left(\mathcal{S}\mskip-3.0mu\operatorname{pc}\right)
, the map
\mathbb{M}\mathrm{ap}_{\mathbf{Alg}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}\left(\mathcal{S}\mskip-3.0mu\operatorname{pc}\right)}\left(\mathsf{B},\mathcal{C}\right)\rightarrow\mathbb{M}\mathrm{ap}_{\mathbf{Alg}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}\left(\mathcal{S}\mskip-3.0mu\operatorname{pc}\right)}\left(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{C}\right)
given by composition with
f
induces an equivalence

\mathbb{M}\mathrm{ap}_{\mathbf{Alg}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}\left(\mathcal{S}\mskip-3.0mu\operatorname{pc}\right)}\left(\mathsf{B},\mathcal{C}\right)\overset{\simeq}{\longrightarrow}\mathbb{M}\mathrm{ap}^{0}_{\mathbf{Alg}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}\left(\mathcal{S}\mskip-3.0mu\operatorname{pc}\right)}\left(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{C}\right),

where
\mathbb{M}\mathrm{ap}^{0}_{\mathbf{Alg}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}\left(\mathcal{S}\mskip-3.0mu\operatorname{pc}\right)}\left(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{C}\right)
is the union of those connected components of
\mathbb{M}\mathrm{ap}_{\mathbf{Alg}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}\left(\mathcal{S}\mskip-3.0mu\operatorname{pc}\right)}\left(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{C}\right)
spanned by those maps
g
such that
g\left(a\right)
is invertible in
\pi_{0}\left(\mathcal{C}\right)
.

In the case of a classical
\mathbf{C}^{\infty}
-algebra (in sets),
\mathcal{A},
and some
a\in\mathcal{A}
, the above definition reduces to the usual
\mathbf{C}^{\infty}
localization
\mathcal{A}\left[1/a\right]
given up to equivalence by the pushout

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{C}^{\infty}\left(\mathbb{R}\right)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{A}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{C}^{\infty}\left(\mathbb{R}\setminus\{0\}\right)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{A}\left[1/a\right]}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q_{a}}

of
C^{\infty}
-rings. The localization of homotopical
C^{\infty}
-ring admits a similar characterization, for which we will need the following definition.

Definition 5.11
.

(1)

A map
f:\mathcal{A}\rightarrow\mathsf{B}
in
\mathbf{Alg}_{\mathbf{Com}_{\mathbb{R}}}\left(\mathcal{S}\mskip-3.0mu\operatorname{pc}\right)
is
strong
(in the sense of
[
37
, Definition 2.2.2.1]
) if the natural map

\pi_{n}\left(\mathcal{A}\right)\underset{\pi_{0}\left(\mathcal{A}\right)}{\otimes}\pi_{0}\left(\mathsf{B}\right)\rightarrow\pi_{n}\left(\mathsf{B}\right)

is an isomorphism for all
n\geq 0
.

(2)

A map
f:\mathcal{A}\rightarrow\mathsf{B}
of homotopical
C^{\infty}
-algebras is
strong
if
f^{\sharp}:\mathcal{A}^{\sharp}\rightarrow\mathsf{B}^{\sharp}
is strong.

Proposition 5.12
.

[
11
, Proposition 4.16]

Let
\mathcal{A}
be a homotopical
\mathbf{C}^{\infty}
-algebra and let
a\in\pi_{0}\left(\mathcal{A}\right)
, and let
f:A\rightarrow B
a map of simplicial
C^{\infty}
-rings. The following are equivalent:

(1)

The map
f:A\rightarrow B
exhibits
B
as a localization with respect to
a
.

(2)

For every
n\geq 0
, the induced map

\pi_{n}\left(\mathcal{A}^{\sharp}\right)\underset{\pi_{0}\left(A^{alg}\right)}{\otimes}\left(\pi_{0}\left(\mathcal{A}\right)\left[1/a\right]\right)^{alg}\rightarrow\pi_{n}\left(B^{alg}\right)

is an equivalence; that is,
f
is strong and the map of
C^{\infty}
-schemes corresponding to
\pi_{0}\left(A\right)\rightarrow\pi_{0}\left(B\right)
is an open immersion.

(3)

B
fits into a pushout diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{C}^{\infty}\left(\mathbb{R}\right)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{C}^{\infty}\left(\mathbb{R}\setminus\{0\}\right)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q_{a}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}

where
q_{a}
is the unique up to homotopy map associated to
a\in\pi_{0}\left(A\right)
(note that as a consequence, localizations always exist).

We denote the
\mathbf{C}^{\infty}
-localization of a homotopical
\mathbf{C}^{\infty}
-ring
\mathcal{A}
by an element
a\in\pi_{0}\mathcal{A}
by
\mathcal{A}\left[1/a\right].

5.3.
{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}
-Koszul complexes

There is one subtle notion needed to work with homological algebra in the
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-graded setting. For example, in the free superalgebra on odd generators
\eta_{1},\eta_{2},\ldots\eta_{n}
, the sequence of generators is not a regular sequence in the usual sense, since it consists of zero divisors. However, it is an
odd regular sequence
in the sense of
[
28
]
:

Definition 5.13
.

An even element
q
of a supercommutative ring
\mathcal{A}
is
regular
if it is not a zero-divisor.

An odd element
p
of a supercommutative ring
\mathcal{A}
is
regular
if the sequence

\mathcal{A}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\cdot p}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathcal{A}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\cdot p}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathcal{A}

is exact.

A sequence
\left(a_{1},a_{2},...,a_{n}\right)
of (even or odd) elements in a supercommutative ring
\mathcal{A}
will be called a
regular sequence
, if the image of
a_{i+1}
is a regular element of
\mathcal{A}/\left(a_{1},\ldots,a_{i}\right)
for all
i.

If
\mathcal{A}
is a
\mathbb{Z}
-graded commutative superalgebra, a sequence
\left(a_{1},a_{2},...,a_{n}\right)
of elements (possibly of different degrees), is
regular
if it is a regular sequence in the underlying
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-graded commutative algebra.

Remark 5.14
.

Recall that if
a\in\mathcal{A}
is of bidegree
||a||=\left(n,\varepsilon\right),
it is of
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-degree
n+\varepsilon
in the underlying
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-graded algebra.

Definition 5.15
.

Let
\mathcal{A}
be a dg-
{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}
-algebra. Let
a_{1},\ldots,a_{k}
be a set of elements of
\mathcal{A}
of bidegrees
\left(n_{1},\varepsilon_{1}\right),\ldots,\left(n_{k},\varepsilon_{k}\right)
such that each
a_{i}
is closed, i.e.
da_{i}=0.
The
Koszul algebra
of
\mathcal{A}
associated to this set is the (
1
-categorical) pushout

\textstyle{\left(\mathbf{C}^{\infty}\{x_{1},\ldots,x_{k}\},||x_{i}||=||a_{i}||,0\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\left(a_{1},\ldots,a_{k}\right)}
\textstyle{\left(\mathbf{C}^{\infty}\{x_{1},\ldots,x_{k},\xi_{1},\ldots,\xi_{k}\},d=x_{i}\frac{\partial}{\partial\xi_{i}},||\xi_{i}||=\left(n_{i}-1,\varepsilon_{i}\right)\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{A}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{K\left(\mathcal{A},a_{1},\ldots,a_{k}\right),}

in
\mathit{dgSC^{\infty}Alg}_{\leq 0}.

If
\mathcal{A}=\left(\mathcal{A},d\right),
then the underlying
\mathbb{Z}
-graded commutative superalgebra of
K\left(\mathcal{A},a_{1},\ldots,a_{k}\right)
is

K\left(\mathcal{A},a_{1},\ldots,a_{k}\right)\cong\mathcal{A}\{\xi_{1},\ldots,\xi_{k}\},
the free
\mathcal{A}
-algebra (in
{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}
-algebras) on the generators
\xi_{1},\ldots,\xi_{k}.
Since all of these generators are strictly of negative degrees, we have that

\mathcal{A}\{\xi_{1},\ldots,\xi_{k}\}\cong\mathcal{A}\underset{\mathbb{R}}{\otimes}\mathbb{R}\left[\xi_{1},\ldots,\xi_{k}\right],

where
\mathbb{R}\left[\xi_{1},\ldots,\xi_{k}\right]
is the free commutative
\mathbb{R}
-algebra on the (graded) generators
\xi_{1},\ldots,\xi_{k}.

Using Einstein summation conventions, the differential takes the form

D=D_{\mathcal{A}}+a^{i}\frac{\partial}{\partial\xi_{i}}.

Remark 5.16
.

The Koszul algebra of
a_{1},\ldots,a_{k}
can be constructed inductively, so that

K\left(\mathcal{A},a_{1},\ldots,a_{n}\right)=K\left(K\left(\mathcal{A},a_{1}\right),a_{2},\ldots,a_{n}\right)=\ldots=K\left(K\ldots\left(K\left(\mathcal{A},a_{1}\right),a_{2}\right),\ldots,a_{n}\right).

Lemma 5.17
.

Suppose that
\mathcal{A}
is a dg-
\mathbf{C}^{\infty}
-algebra with zero differential, and let
a
be a regular element of
\mathcal{A}
of any degree. Then there is a quasi-isomorphism of dg-
\mathbf{C}^{\infty}
-algebras

K\left(\mathcal{A},a\right)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathcal{A}/\left(a\right),

where
\left(a\right)
is the homogeneous ideal generated by
a.

Proof.

Case 1
:
|a|
is even. Then we have that
a
is not a zero-divisor. Let
||a||=\left(i,\varepsilon\right).
Let
\xi_{i-1}
denote the added generator of bidegree
\left(i-1,\epsilon\right).
Then
\xi_{i-1}
is fermionic so squares to zero. Therefore, as a
\mathbb{Z}
-graded super vector space, we have

K\left(\mathcal{A},a\right)\cong\mathcal{A}^{\bullet}\oplus\xi_{i-1}\cdot\mathcal{A}^{\bullet}\left[1-i\right].

We have

d_{k}:\mathcal{A}^{k}\oplus\xi_{i-1}\cdot\mathcal{A}^{k-i+1}\to\mathcal{A}^{k+1}\oplus\xi_{i-1}\cdot\mathcal{A}^{k-i}

is zero for
k=0,\ldots,i,
and for
k<i,

d_{k}\left(b_{k}+\xi_{i-1}c_{k-i+1}\right)=a\cdot c_{k-i+1}.

Since
a
is not a zero-divisor, we have

d_{k}\left(b_{k}+\xi_{i-1}c_{k-i+1}\right)=0\iff c_{k-1+i}=0.

This means that
\ker\left(d_{k}\right)=\mathcal{A}^{k}.
It’s also clear that
\mathbf{Im}\left(d_{k-1}\right)=\left(a\right)_{k},
that is, the
k^{th}
component of the principal ideal
\left(a\right).
It follows that the canonical map
K\left(\mathcal{A},a\right)\to\mathcal{A}/\left(a\right)
is a quasi-isomorphism.

Case 2
:
||a||
is odd. Then we have that
a\cdot x=0
iff
x=a\cdot b
for some
b.
So we have the underlying graded algebra of
K\left(\mathcal{A},a\right)
is the polynomial algebra
\mathcal{A}\left[\xi_{i-1}\right]
on a generator of
bosonic
degree
\left(i-1,\varepsilon\right).
An element of degree
k
is polynomial of the form

x=c_{k}+\underset{j\geq 1}{\sum}c_{k-j\cdot\left(i-1\right)}\xi^{j}_{i-1},
with each
c_{r}\in\mathcal{A}^{r}.

So

dx=\underset{j\geq 1}{\sum}c_{k-j\cdot\left(i-1\right)}\cdot a\cdot j\xi^{j-1}_{i-1}.

This means that
dx=0
if and only if for all
j\geq 1,

c_{k-j\cdot\left(i-1\right)}\cdot a=0,

but since
a
is regular, this means that each
c_{k-j\cdot\left(i-1\right)}=0
for
j\geq 1,
i.e.
x=c_{k},
so
\ker\left(d_{k}\right)=\mathcal{A}_{k}.
Again, we clearly have that
\mathbf{Im}\left(d_{k-1}\right)=\left(a\right)_{k},
so it follows once more that the canonical map
K\left(\mathcal{A},a\right)\to\mathcal{A}/\left(a\right)
is a quasi-isomorphism.
∎

Proposition 5.18
.

Suppose that
\mathcal{A}
is a dg-
\mathbf{C}^{\infty}
-algebra with zero differential, and let
\left(a_{1},a_{2},\ldots a_{n}\right)
be a regular sequence of elements of
\mathcal{A}.
Then

K\left(\mathcal{A},a_{i}\right)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathcal{A}/\left(a_{1},a_{2},\ldots,a_{n}\right).

Proof.

We will prove this by induction on the number of elements in the sequence. For simplicity, we will assume we are in the
\mathbb{Z}
-graded rather than
\mathbb{Z}\times\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-graded setting. We have already established this for
n=1.
Suppose now that it is true for all sequences of
n-1
regular elements. We wish to show it also holds for those of length
n.

Let
\left(a_{1},a_{2},\ldots a_{n}\right)
be a regular sequence of elements of
\mathcal{A}.
Firstly, notice that since we are not allowing elements of degree
1,
the Koszul construction can never add generators of degree
0.
Therefore, for any dg-
\mathbf{C}^{\infty}
-algebra
\mathcal{B}
one can identify the underlying cdga of the Koszul algebra of an element
b_{i}\in\mathcal{B}
of degree
i
as the pushout

\textstyle{\mathbb{R}\left[x_{i}\right]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{b_{i}}
\textstyle{\mathbb{R}\left[x_{i},\xi_{i-1}\right]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{B}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{K\left(\mathcal{B},b_{i}\right).}

Applying this to the case that
\mathcal{B}=K\left(\mathcal{A},a_{1},\ldots,a_{n-1}\right),
we can consider the stack of pushout diagrams

\textstyle{\mathbb{R}\left[x_{i}\right]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{a_{n}}
\textstyle{\mathbb{R}\left[x_{i},\xi_{i-1}\right]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{K\left(\mathcal{A},a_{1},\ldots,a_{n-1}\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

\sim

\textstyle{K\left(\mathcal{A}/\left(\mathcal{A},a_{1},\ldots,a_{n-1}\right),b_{i}\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{A}/\left(\mathcal{A},a_{1},\ldots,a_{n-1}\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{K\left(\mathcal{A}/\left(a_{1},\ldots,a_{n-1}\right),a_{n}\right),}

where

K\left(A,a_{1},\ldots,a_{n-1}\right)\to\mathcal{A}/\left(\mathcal{A},a_{1},\ldots,a_{n-1}\right)

is a quasi-isomorphism. Since the projective model structure on non-positively graded differential algebras over
\mathbb{R}
is left proper, and the top horizontal map is a cofibration, it follows that the map

K\left(a_{1},\ldots,a_{n}\right)\to K\left(\mathcal{A}/\left(a_{1},\ldots,a_{n-1}\right),a_{n}\right)

is also a quasi-isomorphism. Since the outer square is also a pushout diagram, and
a_{n}
is regular in
\mathcal{A}/\left(a_{1},\ldots,a_{n-1}\right),
Lemma
5.17
implies that
K\left(\mathcal{A}/\left(a_{1},\ldots,a_{n-1}\right),a_{n}\right)\to\mathcal{A}/\left(a_{1},\ldots,a_{n}\right)
is a quasi-isomorphism. This completes the proof.
∎

Lemma 5.19
.

Suppose that
\mathcal{M}
is a supermanifold such that
\mathbf{C}^{\infty}\left(\mathcal{M}\right)
is cofibrant in
\mathit{dgSC^{\infty}Alg}_{\leq 0},
e.g.
\mathbb{R}^{n|m}
for some
n
and
m.
Let
\varphi=\left(f,\alpha\right):\mathcal{M}\to\mathbb{R}^{p|q}
be a submersion locally around the origin. Then the following diagram is a homotopy pushout in
\mathit{dgSC^{\infty}Alg}_{\leq 0}
:

\textstyle{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}\left(\mathbb{R}^{p|q}\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\varphi^{\ast}}
\scriptstyle{ev_{0}}
\textstyle{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}\left(\mathcal{M}\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i^{\ast}}
\textstyle{\mathbb{R}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}\left(\varphi^{-1}\left(0\right)\right),}

where
i:\varphi^{-1}\left(0\right)\hookrightarrow\mathcal{M}
is in the inclusion of the closed sub-supermanifold of zeros of
\varphi.

Proof.

The function
f
corresponds, via its components, to
p
smooth functions

f_{1},f_{2},\ldots f_{p}:\mathcal{M}\to\mathbb{R}

and
q
smooth functions

g_{1},\ldots,q_{q}:\mathcal{M}\to\mathbb{R}^{0|1}.

Hence we have
f_{1},\ldots f_{p}\in\mathbf{C}^{\infty}\left(\mathcal{M}\right)_{0}
and
g_{1},\ldots,g_{q}\in\mathbf{C}^{\infty}\left(\mathcal{M}\right)_{1}.
Consider the super Koszul complex
K\left(\mathbf{C}^{\infty}\left(\mathcal{M}\right),f_{1},\ldots,f_{p},g_{1},\ldots,g_{q}\right),
which is a differential graded commutative superalgebra over
\mathbb{R}.
As a
\mathbb{Z}
-graded superalgebra it is

K\left(\mathbf{C}^{\infty}\left(\mathcal{M}\right),f_{1},\ldots,f_{p},g_{1},\ldots,g_{q}\right)=\mathbf{C}^{\infty}\left(\mathcal{M}\right)\left[\theta_{1},\theta_{2},\ldots,\eta_{1},\eta_{2},\ldots,\eta_{q}\right],

where each
\theta_{i}
is of degree
\left(0,-1\right)
and each
\eta_{j}
is of degree
\left(1,-1\right)
with respect to the
\mathbb{Z}_{2}\times\mathbb{Z}
grading. The differential is given by

d=\sum_{i=1}^{p}f_{i}\frac{\partial}{\partial\theta_{i}}+\sum_{j=1}^{q}g_{j}\frac{\partial}{\partial\eta_{j}}.

In particular, we see that
K\left(\mathbf{C}^{\infty}\left(\mathcal{M}\right),f_{1},\ldots,f_{p},g_{1},\ldots,g_{q}\right)_{0}=\mathbf{C}^{\infty}\left(\mathcal{M}\right)
which is a
{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}
-algebra, so the super Koszul algebra is a dg-
{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}
-algebra.

Consider for a moment the special case
\mathcal{M}=\mathbb{R}^{p|q}
and
\varphi=id.
Choose coordinates
\left(y_{1},y_{2},\ldots,y_{p}|\beta_{1},\beta_{2},\ldots,\beta_{q}\right).
Then these coordinates, regarded as elements of
\mathbf{C}^{\infty}\left(\mathcal{M}\right)=\mathbf{C}^{\infty}\left(\mathbb{R}^{p|q}\right)
form a regular sequence. It follows that the super Koszul complex in this case is acyclic. Thus the we have an equivalence of homotopy pushouts

\mathbb{R}\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu\underset{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}\left(\mathbb{R}^{p|q}\right)}{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\mkern-13.73mu\resizebox{1305270}{540744}{$\phantom{x}{}_{\phantom{.}{}^{\infty}}$}$\crcr$\displaystyle\bigcirc$\crcr}}}}{\mathbin{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\mkern-13.73mu\resizebox{1305270}{540744}{$\phantom{x}{}_{\phantom{.}{}^{\infty}}$}$\crcr$\textstyle\bigcirc$\crcr}}}}{\mathbin{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\mkern-13.73mu\resizebox{1305270}{540744}{$\phantom{x}{}_{\phantom{.}{}^{\infty}}$}$\crcr$\scriptstyle\bigcirc$\crcr}}}}{\mathbin{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-13.73mu\resizebox{1305270}{540744}{$\phantom{x}{}_{\phantom{.}{}^{\infty}}$}$\crcr$\scriptscriptstyle\bigcirc$\crcr}}}}}^{\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu\mathbb{L}}\mspace{2mu}\mathbf{C}^{\infty}\left(\mathcal{M}\right)\simeq K\left(\mathbf{C}^{\infty}\left(\mathbb{R}^{p|q}\right),y_{1},\ldots,y_{p},\beta_{1},\ldots,\beta_{q}\right)\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu\underset{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}\left(\mathbb{R}^{p|q}\right)}{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\mkern-13.73mu\resizebox{1305270}{540744}{$\phantom{x}{}_{\phantom{.}{}^{\infty}}$}$\crcr$\displaystyle\bigcirc$\crcr}}}}{\mathbin{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\mkern-13.73mu\resizebox{1305270}{540744}{$\phantom{x}{}_{\phantom{.}{}^{\infty}}$}$\crcr$\textstyle\bigcirc$\crcr}}}}{\mathbin{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\mkern-13.73mu\resizebox{1305270}{540744}{$\phantom{x}{}_{\phantom{.}{}^{\infty}}$}$\crcr$\scriptstyle\bigcirc$\crcr}}}}{\mathbin{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-13.73mu\resizebox{1305270}{540744}{$\phantom{x}{}_{\phantom{.}{}^{\infty}}$}$\crcr$\scriptscriptstyle\bigcirc$\crcr}}}}}^{\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu\mathbb{L}}\mspace{2mu}\mathbf{C}^{\infty}\left(\mathcal{M}\right).

Moreover, the natural homomorphism

\mathbf{C}^{\infty}\left(\mathbb{R}^{p|q}\right)\to K\left(\mathbf{C}^{\infty}\left(\mathcal{M}\right),y_{1},\ldots,y_{p},\beta_{1},\ldots,\beta_{q}\right)

is a quasi-free extension, hence a cofibration.

Let us now return to the case of a general
\varphi.
Since all dg-
\mathbf{C}^{\infty}
-algebras in the diagram

\textstyle{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}\left(\mathbb{R}^{p|q}\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\varphi^{\ast}}
\textstyle{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}\left(\mathcal{M}\right)}
\textstyle{K\left(\mathbf{C}^{\infty}\left(\mathbb{R}^{p|q}\right),y_{1},\ldots,y_{p},\beta_{1},\ldots,\beta_{q}\right)}

are cofibrant, and the vertical arrow above is a cofibration, the ordinary pushout computes the homotopy pushout. But the ordinary pushout is precisely
K\left(\mathbf{C}^{\infty}\left(\mathcal{M}\right),f_{1},\ldots,f_{p},g_{1},\ldots,g_{q}\right),
whereas for the homotopy pushout we can replace
K\left(\mathbf{C}^{\infty}\left(\mathbb{R}^{p|q}\right),y_{1},\ldots,y_{p},\beta_{1},\ldots,\beta_{q}\right)
with
\mathbb{R}
since it is acyclic. So the desired homotopy pushout is given by the super Koszul complex
K\left(\mathbf{C}^{\infty}\left(\mathcal{M}\right),f_{1},\ldots,f_{p},g_{1},\ldots,g_{q}\right).
Therefore, it will suffice to prove that
K\left(\mathbf{C}^{\infty}\left(\mathcal{M}\right),f_{1},\ldots,f_{p},g_{1},\ldots,g_{q}\right)
is quasi-isomorphic to
\mathbf{C}^{\infty}\left(\varphi^{-1}\left(0\right)\right).

Suppose that as a supermanifold we have
\mathcal{M}=\left(M,\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}}\right).
Define a
1
-categorical sheaf
\mathcal{O}_{\varphi}
on
M
with values in
\mathit{dgSC^{\infty}Alg}_{\leq 0}
by

\mathcal{O}_{\varphi}\left(U\right):=\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}}\left(U\right)\underset{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}\left(\mathcal{M}\right)}{\otimes}K\left(\mathbf{C}^{\infty}\left(\mathcal{M}\right),f_{1},\ldots,f_{p},g_{1},\ldots,g_{q}\right),

where the tensor product is the underived tensor product of commutative differential graded superalgebras over
\mathbb{R}.
In concrete terms this is adding graded coordinates
\theta_{1},\ldots,\theta_{p},\eta_{1},\ldots,\eta_{q}
to
\mathcal{M},
and equipping the result with a vector field
\mathbb{Z}
-degree
+1.
(This gives the structure of a differential graded supermanifold). By Batchelor’s theorem,
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}}
is given by sections of a vector bundle over
M,
and hence
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}}
and
\mathcal{O}_{\varphi}
are soft.

We claim that
\mathcal{O}_{\varphi}
is a homotopy sheaf of dg-
{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}
-algebras. The model structure on dg-
{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}
-algebras is produced via transfer from the projective model structure on non-positively graded cochain complexes on super vector spaces, and hence homotopy limits can be computed as bare cochain complexes. Therefore, it suffices to prove that
\mathcal{O}_{\varphi}
is a homotopy sheaf of cochain complexes (of super vector spaces). Let
\mathcal{V}:=\left(V_{\alpha}\right)_{\alpha}
be an open cover of an open subset
U
of
M.
Let
\mbox{\v{C}}_{\mathcal{V}}:\Delta^{op}\to\mathbf{Op}\left(M\right)
be the Čech nerve of the cover, regarded as a simplicial diagram in the poset of open subsets of
M.
The homotopy sheaf condition demands the homotopy limit of the natural cosimplicial diagram
\mathcal{O}_{\varphi}\circ\mbox{\v{C}}_{\mathcal{V}}
of cochain complexes agrees up to quasi-isomorphism with
\mathcal{O}_{\varphi}\left(U\right).
This homotopy limit can be computed as the totalization of the natural double complex arising by applying cosimplicial Dold-Kan to the cosimplicial direction of
\mathcal{O}_{\varphi}\circ\mbox{\v{C}}_{\mathcal{V}}.
The spectral sequence for the double complex, which computes the cohomology of the totalization, degenerates on the
E_{2}
page since the sheaves of complexes involved are all soft. The result readily follows at this point from the (
1
-categorical) sheaf condition of
\mathcal{O}_{\varphi}.

Since being a submersion is a local property, we can find an open sub-supermanifold
\mathcal{U}
of the closed sub-supermanifold
\mathcal{N}:=\varphi^{-1}\left(0\right)
such that
\varphi|_{\mathcal{U}}
is globally a submersion. By the local form of submersions, we can find local coordinate charts
\left(\mathcal{U}_{\alpha}\subseteq\mathcal{U}\right)_{\alpha},

\mathcal{U}_{\alpha}\cong\mathbb{R}^{n+p|m+q}
with coordinates
\left(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n},y_{1},\ldots y_{p}|\xi_{1},\ldots,\xi_{m},\beta_{1},\ldots,\beta_{q}\right),
such that in these coordinates we have

\varphi\left(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n},y_{1},\ldots y_{p}|\xi_{1},\ldots,\xi_{m},\beta_{1},\ldots,\beta_{q}\right)=\left(y_{1},\ldots,y_{p}|\beta_{1},\ldots,\beta_{q}\right).

In particular,
\mathcal{U_{\alpha}}\cap\varphi^{-1}\left(0\right),
is exactly the space

\{\left(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n},y_{1},\ldots y_{p}|\xi_{1},\ldots,\xi_{m},\beta_{1},\ldots,\beta_{q}\right)\mspace{3mu}|\mspace{3mu}\left(y_{1},\ldots,y_{p}|\beta_{1},\ldots,\beta_{p}\right)=0\}.

Under this change of coordinates, we have that
\mathcal{O}_{\varphi}\left(\mathcal{U}_{\alpha}\right)
is equivalent to the Koszul complex

K\left(\mathbf{C}^{\infty}\{y_{1},\ldots,y_{p}|\beta_{1},\ldots,\beta_{q}\},y_{1},y_{2},\ldots,y_{p},\beta_{1},\beta_{2},\ldots,y_{q}\right).

However,
y_{1}\ldots,y_{p},\beta_{1},\ldots,y_{q}
is a regular sequence, hence

\displaystyle K\left(\mathbf{C}^{\infty}\{y_{1},\ldots,y_{p}|\beta_{1},\ldots,\beta_{q}\},y_{1},y_{2},\ldots,y_{p},\beta_{1},\beta_{2},\ldots,y_{q}\right)

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle\mathbf{C}^{\infty}\{y_{1},\ldots,y_{p}|\beta_{1},\ldots,\beta_{q}\}/\left(y,\beta\right)

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle\mathbf{C}^{\infty}\left(\mathcal{U}_{\alpha}\right)/\left(f\mspace{3mu},f|_{\mathcal{N}\cap\mathcal{U}_{\alpha}}=0\right)

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle\mathbf{C}^{\infty}\left(\mathcal{N}\cap\mathcal{U}_{\alpha}\right).

By the homotopy sheaf property, we conclude that
\mathcal{O}_{\varphi}\left(\mathcal{U}\right)\simeq\mathbf{C}^{\infty}\left(\mathcal{N}\right).

Finally, notice that on one hand since
f_{1},\ldots,f_{p}
are invertible away from
\mathcal{N},

\mathcal{O}_{\varphi}
is acyclic away from
\mathcal{N},
and hence, again by the sheaf property, the restriction map

K\left(\mathbf{C}^{\infty}\left(\mathcal{M}\right),f_{1},\ldots,f_{p},g_{1},\ldots,g_{q}\right)=\mathcal{O}_{\varphi}\left(\mathcal{M}\right)\to\mathcal{O}_{\varphi}\left(\mathcal{U}\right)

is a quasi-isomorphism. On the other hand, we have already proved that
K\left(\mathbf{C}^{\infty}\left(\mathcal{M}\right),f_{1},\ldots,f_{p},g_{1},\ldots,g_{q}\right)
computes the desired homotopy pushout.
∎

In the process of this proof, we have proven the following:

Proposition 5.20
.

Suppose that
\mathcal{M}
is a supermanifold such that
\mathbf{C}^{\infty}\left(\mathcal{M}\right)
is cofibrant in
\mathbf{dg}\mathbf{C}^{\infty}\mathbf{Alg}_{\leq 0},
e.g.
\mathbb{R}^{n|m}
for some
n
and
m.
Let
\varphi=\left(f,\alpha\right):\mathcal{M}\to\mathbb{R}^{p|q}
be any smooth map. Then the following diagram is a homotopy pushout in
\mathit{dgSC^{\infty}Alg}_{\leq 0}
:

\textstyle{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}\left(\mathbb{R}^{p|q}\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\varphi^{\ast}}
\scriptstyle{ev_{0}}
\textstyle{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}\left(\mathcal{M}\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathbb{R}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{K\left(\mathbf{C}^{\infty}\left(\mathcal{M}\right),f_{1},\ldots,f_{p},g_{1},\ldots,g_{q}\right),}

where
\varphi=\left(f_{1},\ldots,f_{p}|g_{1},\ldots,g_{q}\right).

It is worth remarking that the morphism

\mathbf{C}^{\infty}\left(\mathbb{R}^{p|q}\right)\to K\left(\mathbf{C}^{\infty}\left(\mathbb{R}^{p|q}\right),y_{1},\ldots,y_{p},\beta_{1},\ldots,\beta_{q}\right)

used in the proof is also a cofibration in the projective model structure on non-positively graded commutative differential graded superalgebras over
\mathbb{R}.
This model structure is moreover left proper
[
39
, Theorem 4.17]
, hence we also get have the following:

Proposition 5.21
.

Suppose that
\mathcal{M}
is a supermanifold. Let
\varphi=\left(f,\alpha\right):\mathcal{M}\to\mathbb{R}^{p|q}
be any smooth map. Then the following diagram is a homotopy pushout in the projective model structure on non-positively graded commutative differential graded superalgebras over
\mathbb{R}.

\textstyle{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}\left(\mathbb{R}^{p|q}\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\varphi^{\ast}}
\scriptstyle{ev_{0}}
\textstyle{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}\left(\mathcal{M}\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathbb{R}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{K\left(\mathbf{C}^{\infty}\left(\mathcal{M}\right),f_{1},\ldots,f_{p},g_{1},\ldots,g_{q}\right),}

where
\varphi=\left(f_{1},\ldots,f_{p}|g_{1},\ldots,g_{q}\right).

Proof.

This follows from the above discussion together with the fact that the above diagram is a
1
-categorical pushout diagram of differential graded commutative superalgebras.
∎

Theorem 5.22
.

Let
\varphi:\mathbb{R}^{n|m}\to\mathbb{R}^{p|q}
be any smooth map of supermanifolds. Then for any
l,k,
the following diagram is pushout in
\mathbf{Alg}_{\mathbf{SCom}_{\mathbb{R}}}\left(\mathcal{S}\mskip-3.0mu\operatorname{pc}\right)
:

\textstyle{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}\left(\mathbb{R}^{n+p|m+q}\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{pr^{\ast}}
\scriptstyle{\Gamma_{f}^{\ast}}
\textstyle{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}\left(\mathbb{R}^{n+p+l|m+q+k}\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}\left(\mathbb{R}^{n|m}\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}\left(\mathbb{R}^{n+l|m+k}\right)}

where
\Gamma_{\varphi}
is the graph of
\varphi.
I.e. the canonical morphism

\mathbf{SCom}_{\mathbb{R}}\to{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}

of algebraic theories is
unramified
in the sense of
[
11
, Definition 3.31]
.

Proof.

Suppose once again that
\varphi=\left(f_{1},\ldots,f_{p}|g_{1},\ldots,g_{q}\right).
Consider the super Koszul complex
K\left(\mathbf{C}^{\infty}\{t_{1},\ldots,t_{p}|\xi_{1},\ldots,\xi_{q}\},t_{1},\ldots,t_{p},\xi_{1},\ldots,\xi_{q}\right)\simeq\mathbb{R}.
Introduce coordinates

\left(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n},y_{1},\ldots,y_{p}|\alpha_{1},\ldots,\alpha_{m},\beta_{1}\ldots\beta_{q}\right)

on
\mathbb{R}^{n+m|p+q}.
Define the function

h:\mathbb{R}^{n+p|m+q}\to\mathbb{R}^{p|q}

h\left(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n},y_{1},\ldots,y_{p}|\alpha_{1},\ldots,\alpha_{m},\beta_{1}\ldots\beta_{q}\right)=\left(y_{1}-f_{1}\left(x,\alpha\right),\ldots,y_{p}-f_{p}\left(x,\alpha\right)|\beta_{1}-g_{1}\left(x,\alpha\right),\ldots,\beta_{q}-g_{q}\left(x,\alpha\right)\right).

Write
h=\left(r,s\right).

Then
\Gamma_{\varphi}^{*}
factors as

\mathbf{C}^{\infty}\left(\mathbb{R}^{n+p|m+q}\right)\rightarrowtail K\left(\mathbf{C}^{\infty}\left(\mathbb{R}^{n+p|m+q}\right),r_{1},\ldots,r_{p},s_{1},\ldots,s_{q}\right)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle a}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathbf{C}^{\infty}\left(\mathbb{R}^{n|m}\right),

where the first arrow is a cofibration and the second is induced by the identification

H^{0}\left(K\left(\mathbf{C}^{\infty}\left(\mathbb{R}^{n+p|m+q}\right),r_{1},\ldots,r_{p},s_{1},\ldots,s_{q}\right)\right)\cong\mathbf{C}^{\infty}\left(\mathbb{R}^{n|m}\right),

since we have e.g.
\left[y_{i}\right]=\left[f_{i}\right]
in Koszul cohomology. Notice that we also have a
1
-categorical pushout diagram

\textstyle{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}\{t_{1},\ldots,t_{p}|\xi_{1},\ldots,\xi_{q}\}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{h^{\ast}}
\textstyle{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}\left(\mathbb{R}^{n+p|m+q}\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{K\left(\mathbf{C}^{\infty}\{t_{1},\ldots,t_{p}|\xi_{1},\ldots,\xi_{q}\},t_{1},\ldots,t_{p},\xi_{1},\ldots,\xi_{q}\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{K\left(\mathbf{C}^{\infty}\left(\mathbb{R}^{n+p|m+q}\right),r_{1},\ldots,r_{p},s_{1},\ldots,s_{q}\right).}

The vertical map is a cofibration and all the algebras are cofibrant, hence this is also a homotopy pushout. Moreover, since
h
is a submersion, it follows from Lemma
5.19
that
K\left(\mathbf{C}^{\infty}\left(\mathbb{R}^{n+p|m+q}\right),r_{1},\ldots,r_{p},s_{1},\ldots,s_{q}\right)
is quasi-isomorphic to smooth functions on the zero-locus of
h,
which is readily identified with the
0^{th}
cohomology of the super Koszul complex. Hence the morphism
a
above is a quasi-isomorphism. The above pushout diagram is a homotopy pushout both of differential graded commutative superalgbras and of dg-
{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}
-superalgebras. By gluing pushout diagrams, we have the follow diagram

\textstyle{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}\{t_{1},\ldots,t_{p}|\xi_{1},\ldots,\xi_{q}\}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\left(h\circ pr\right)^{\ast}}
\scriptstyle{h^{\ast}}
\textstyle{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}\left(\mathbb{R}^{n+p|m+q}\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{pr^{\ast}}
\textstyle{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}\left(\mathbb{R}^{n+p+l|m+q+k}\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{K\left(\mathbf{C}^{\infty}\{t_{1},\ldots,t_{p}|\xi_{1},\ldots,\xi_{q}\},t_{1},\ldots,t_{p},\xi_{1},\ldots,\xi_{q}\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{K\left(\mathbf{C}^{\infty}\left(\mathbb{R}^{n+p|m+q}\right),r_{1},\ldots,r_{p},s_{1},\ldots,s_{q}\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{K\left(\mathbf{C}^{\infty}\left(\mathbb{R}^{n+p+k|m+q+1}\right),r_{1},\ldots,r_{p},s_{1},\ldots,s_{q}\right).}

all of whose squares are homotopy pushouts of differential graded commutative superalgebras and of dg-
{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}
-superalgebras. Since
pr\circ h
is a submersion, Lemma
5.19
implies that
K\left(\mathbf{C}^{\infty}\left(\mathbb{R}^{n+p+k|m+q+1}\right),r_{1},\ldots,r_{p},s_{1},\ldots,s_{q}\right)
is quasi-isomorphic to smooth functions on the zero locus of
pr\circ h,
which once again is
0^{th}
super Koszul cohomology, and hence
\mathbf{C}^{\infty}\left(\mathbb{R}^{n+l|m+k}\right).
Finally, since
K\left(\mathbf{C}^{\infty}\left(\mathbb{R}^{n+p|m+q}\right),r_{1},\ldots,r_{p},s_{1},\ldots,s_{q}\right)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathbf{C}^{\infty}\left(\mathbb{R}^{n|m}\right),
the right most pushout square is also the homotopy pushout of

\textstyle{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}\left(\mathbb{R}^{n+p|m+q}\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{pr^{\ast}}
\scriptstyle{\Gamma_{f}^{\ast}}
\textstyle{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}\left(\mathbb{R}^{n+p+l|m+q+k}\right)}
\textstyle{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}\left(\mathbb{R}^{n|m}\right)}

and so the result follows.
∎

This finishes the proof of Theorem
4.6
and Corollary
4.7
.

6.
C^{\infty}
-schemes

Definition 6.1
.

A
locally
{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}
-ringed space
is a pair
\left(X,\mathcal{O}_{X}\right)
with
X
a topological space and
\mathcal{O}_{X}
a sheaf with values in the
\infty
-category
\mathbf{d}{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}\mathbf{Alg},
such that for each point
x\in X,
the stalk of
\left(\pi_{0}\left(\mathcal{O}_{X}\right)\right)_{0}
is a local ring with residue field
\mathbb{R}.
Morphisms are pairs

\left(f,\alpha\right):\left(X,\mathcal{O}_{X}\right)\to\left(Y,\mathcal{O}_{Y}\right),

with
f
a continuous map and

\alpha:\mathcal{O}_{Y}\to f_{*}\mathcal{O}_{X}.

More formally, the
\infty
-category
\operatorname{Loc}_{{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}}
of locally
{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}
-ringed space is the full subcategory on such spaces of the total space of the coCartesian fibration associated to to the functor

\operatorname{Shv}\left(\mspace{3mu}\cdot\mspace{3mu},\mathbf{d}{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}\mathbf{Alg}\right):\mathbf{Top}\to\widehat{\mathbf{Cat}}_{\infty}

induced by push-forward of sheaves, where
\widehat{\mathbf{Cat}}_{\infty}
is the
\infty
-category of large
\infty
-categories.

Consider the canonical functor

\Gamma:\operatorname{Loc}_{{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}}\to\mathbf{d}{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}\mathbf{Alg}

induced by taking global sections of each structure sheaf. This functor has a right adjoint

\mathbf{Spec}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}:\mathbf{d}{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}\mathbf{Alg}^{op}\to\operatorname{Loc}_{{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}}

[
14
, Proposition 5.1.8]
which can be described as follows:

Let us first describe the underlined space
\underline{\mathbf{Spec}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}\left(\mathcal{A}\right)}
of
\mathbf{Spec}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}\left(\mathcal{A}\right).
Consider the canonical functor
{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}\to\mathbf{Top}
sending
\mathbb{R}^{p|q}
to
\mathbb{R}^{p}.
This functor preserves finite products, hence, by Theorem
2.15
induces a unique limit-preserving functor

\underline{\mathbf{Spec}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}}:\mathbf{d}{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}\mathbf{Alg}^{op}\to\mathbf{Top}.

Concretely, the underlying set of
\underline{\mathbf{Spec}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}}\left(\mathcal{A}\right)
is the set of homomorphisms

\mathbb{H}\mathrm{om}\left(\pi_{0}\left(\mathcal{A}\right)_{0},\mathbb{R}\right).

Given an element
a\in\pi_{0}\left(\mathcal{A}\right)_{0},
consider the function

\displaystyle f_{a}:\mathbb{H}\mathrm{om}\left(\pi_{0}\left(\mathcal{A}\right)_{0},\mathbb{R}\right)

\displaystyle\to

\displaystyle\mathbb{R}

\displaystyle\varphi

\displaystyle\mapsto

\displaystyle\varphi\left(a\right).

Then the subsets of the form
U_{a}:=f_{a}^{-1}\left(\mathbb{R}\setminus\left\{0\right\}\right)
form a basis for a topology on
\mathbb{H}\mathrm{om}\left(\pi_{0}\left(\mathcal{A}\right)_{0},\mathbb{R}\right)
which is moreover closed under finite intersections— see
[
11
, Section 4.3.1]
. Since this basis is closed under finite intersections, to describe a sheaf on
\underline{\mathbf{Spec}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}}\left(\mathcal{A}\right),
it suffices to define a sheaf on the subcategory of all open subsets of
\underline{\mathbf{Spec}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}}\left(\mathcal{A}\right)
on those of the form
U_{a}.
Hence, there is a unique sheaf
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{A}}
arising as the sheafification of the presheaf

U_{a}\mapsto\mathcal{A}\left[a^{-1}\right],

and we have

\mathbf{Spec}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}\left(\mathcal{A}\right)=\left(\underline{\mathbf{Spec}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}}\left(\mathcal{A}\right),\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{A}}\right).

Remark 6.2
.

By
[
11
, Proposition 4.19]
, if
\mathcal{A}
is finitely presented, then all open subsets of
\mathbf{Spec}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}\left(\mathcal{A}\right)
are of the form
U_{a}
for some
a\in\pi_{0}\left(\mathcal{A}\right).

Definition 6.3
.

An
affine derived
{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}
-schemes
is locally
{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}
-ringed space in the image of
\mathbf{Spec}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}.
Denote the full subcategory of
\operatorname{Loc}_{{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}}
on these objects by
\mathbf{Aff}_{{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}}.

Definition 6.4
.

An affine
{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}
-scheme is
of finite type
if it is equivalent to
\mathbf{Spec}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}\left(\mathcal{A}\right),
for
\mathcal{A}
finitely presented. A
{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}
-scheme is
locally of finite type
if it has a cover by finite type affine
{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}
-schemes.

Remark 6.5
.

Let
\mathcal{A}
be a dg-
{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}
-algebra. By Theorem
4.10
and Corollary
4.7
, it follows that
\mathcal{A}
is a finitely presented
{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}
-ring if and only if it is quasi-isomorphic to
\mathbf{C}^{\infty}\left(\mathcal{M}\right),
for some dg-supermanifold
\mathcal{M}.
By
[
8
, Theorem 5.7]
, we have that

\mathbf{Spec}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}\left(\mathcal{A}\right)\simeq\mathbf{Spec}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}\left(\mathbf{C}^{\infty}\left(\mathcal{M}\right)\right)\simeq\left(\pi_{0}\left(\mathcal{M}\right),\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}}|_{\pi_{0}\left(\mathcal{M}\right)}\right).

The
\mathbf{C}^{\infty}
-spectrum functor enjoys some nice properties:

Proposition 6.6
.

Let
\mathcal{M}
be a supermanifold, then
\mathbf{Spec}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}\left(\mathbf{C}^{\infty}\left(\mathcal{M}\right)\right)\simeq\mathcal{M}.
Moreover, composite

\mathsf{SMfd}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}}{{\lhook\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow}}\mathbf{d}{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}\mathbf{Alg}^{op}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathbf{Spec}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}}}{{-\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu-\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu-\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu-\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu-\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu-\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu-\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu-\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu-\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu-\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu-\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu-\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu\longrightarrow}}\operatorname{Loc}_{{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}}

is fully faithful and preserves transverse pullbacks.

Proof.

It is well known that for an ordinary smooth manifold
M,
the set of
\mathbb{R}
-algebra homomorphisms
\mathbb{H}\mathrm{om}\left(\mathbf{C}^{\infty}\left(M\right),\mathbb{R}\right)
is natural bijection with the points of
M,
and all such homomorphisms are homomorphisms of
\mathbf{C}^{\infty}
-rings. Since any closed subset of a smooth manifold is the zero-set of a smooth function, an open subset is of the form
U_{f},
for
f:M\to\mathbb{R},
a smooth function. Hence, the topology also agrees with that of
M.
It therefore suffices to check that for any open subset
U\subseteq\mathcal{M}_{0}
of the core of
\mathcal{M},
we have

\mathcal{O}_{{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}\left(\mathcal{M}\right)}\left(U\right)\simeq\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}}\left(U\right).

Notice that the following is a transverse pullback diagram in
\mathsf{SMfd}
:

\textstyle{\mathcal{M}|_{U}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathbb{R}\setminus\left\{0\right\}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{M}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f}
\textstyle{\mathbb{R},}

where
U=U_{f}.
By Theorem
3.7
, this implies that

\displaystyle\mathbf{C}^{\infty}\left(\mathcal{M}|_{U}\right)

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle\mathbf{C}^{\infty}\left(\mathcal{M}\right)\underset{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}\left(\mathbb{R}\right)}{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\mkern-13.73mu\resizebox{1305270}{540744}{$\phantom{x}{}_{\phantom{.}{}^{\infty}}$}$\crcr$\displaystyle\bigcirc$\crcr}}}}{\mathbin{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\mkern-13.73mu\resizebox{1305270}{540744}{$\phantom{x}{}_{\phantom{.}{}^{\infty}}$}$\crcr$\textstyle\bigcirc$\crcr}}}}{\mathbin{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\mkern-13.73mu\resizebox{1305270}{540744}{$\phantom{x}{}_{\phantom{.}{}^{\infty}}$}$\crcr$\scriptstyle\bigcirc$\crcr}}}}{\mathbin{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-13.73mu\resizebox{1305270}{540744}{$\phantom{x}{}_{\phantom{.}{}^{\infty}}$}$\crcr$\scriptscriptstyle\bigcirc$\crcr}}}}}\mathbf{C}^{\infty}\left(\mathbb{R}\setminus\left\{0\right\}\right)

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle\mathbf{C}^{\infty}\left(\mathcal{M}\right)\left[f^{-1}\right]

Since
\mathbf{C}^{\infty}\left(\mathcal{M}|_{U}\right)=\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}}\left(U\right),
we can deduce that the presheaf defining
\mathcal{O}_{\mathbf{Spec}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}\left(\mathbf{C}^{\infty}\left(\mathcal{M}\right)\right)}
is already a sheaf and is equivalent to
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}}.

The second statement follows immediately from
[
12
, Theorem 16]
, since
\mathbf{Spec}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}
agrees with Dubuc’s spectrum for
\mathbf{C}^{\infty}
-rings for
0
-truncated algebras.
∎

Despite this, unlike in classical algebraic geometry, the spectrum functor
\mathbf{Spec}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}
is not fully faithful in general. This was already realized for
\mathbf{C}^{\infty}
-rings in
\mathrm{Set}
by Dubuc
[
12
]
. It is fully faithful however when restricted to germ-determined finitely presented
\mathbf{C}^{\infty}
-rings by loc. cit.

Definition 6.7
.

A
{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}
-algebra
\mathcal{A}
is
complete
if the co-unit

\varepsilon:\mathcal{A}\to\Gamma\left(\mathbf{Spec}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}\left(\mathcal{A}\right)\right)

is an equivalence. Denote the full subcategory on the complete algebras by
\mathbf{d}{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}\mathbf{Alg}^{\wedge}.

Proposition 6.8
.

[
14
, Example 5.1.30]
,
[
11
, Corollary 4.45]

If
\mathcal{A}\in\mathbf{d}{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}\mathbf{Alg}
is finitely presented, then it is complete.

Note that the adjunction
\Gamma\dashv\mathbf{Spec}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}
restricts to an adjunction

\textstyle{\mathbf{d}{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}\mathbf{Alg}^{op}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathbf{Spec}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}}
\textstyle{\mathbf{Aff}_{{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}}
\scriptstyle{\Gamma}

Proposition 6.9
.

[
14
, Proposition 5.1.26]

The unit

\eta:id_{\mathbf{Aff}_{{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}}}\Rightarrow\mathbf{Spec}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}\circ\Gamma

is an equivalence.

The following corollary follows immediately:

Corollary 6.10
.

The composite

\left(\mathbf{d}{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}\mathbf{Alg}^{\wedge}\right)^{op}\hookrightarrow\mathbf{d}{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}\mathbf{Alg}^{op}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathbf{Spec}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}}}{{-\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu-\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu-\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu-\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu-\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu-\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu-\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu-\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu-\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu-\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu-\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu-\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu\longrightarrow}}\operatorname{Loc}_{{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}}

is fully faithful, with essential image
\mathbf{Aff}_{{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}}.
In particular

\left(\mathbf{d}{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}\mathbf{Alg}^{\wedge}\right)^{op}\simeq\mathbf{Aff}_{{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}}.

Moreover,
\mathbf{d}{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}\mathbf{Alg}^{\wedge}
is a reflective subcategory of
\mathbf{d}{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}\mathbf{Alg}
with the left adjoint to the inclusion
i^{\wedge}
given by
L^{\wedge}:=\Gamma\circ\mathbf{Spec}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}.

Corollary 6.11
.

The above adjunction restricts to an equivalence

\mathbf{Aff}^{\mathbf{ft}}_{{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}}\simeq\left(\mathbf{Alg}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}\left(\mathcal{S}\mskip-3.0mu\operatorname{pc}\right)^{\mathbf{fp}}\right)^{op}

between affine
{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}
-schemes of finite type and the opposite
\infty
-category of finitely presented
{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}
-algebras. In particular, we have

\mathbf{Aff}^{\mathbf{ft}}_{{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}}\simeq\left(\mathbf{Alg}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}\left(\mathcal{S}\mskip-3.0mu\operatorname{pc}\right)^{\mathbf{fp}}\right)^{op}\simeq\mathsf{DSMfd}\simeq\mathscr{DgSMfd}_{\leq 0}.

Definition 6.12
.

A morphism

\left(f,\alpha\right):\left(X,\mathcal{O}_{X}\right)\to\left(Y,\mathcal{O}_{Y}\right)

is an
open embedding
if
f:X\to Y
is an open embedding of topological spaces, and the map

f^{*}\mathcal{O}_{Y}\to\mathcal{O}_{X}

adjoint to
\alpha
is an equivalence. An
open covering
of a locally
{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}
-ringed space is a collection

\left(\varphi_{i}:\left(X_{i},\mathcal{O}_{X_{i}}\right)\to\left(X,\mathcal{O}_{X}\right)\right)_{i\in I}

of open embeddings such that the underlying maps of topological spaces are jointly surjective. The associated Grothendieck topology is called the
open covering
topology.

Definition 6.13
.

A
derived
{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}
-scheme
is a locally
{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}
-ringed space
\left(X,\mathcal{O}_{X}\right)
such that there exists an open covering

\left(f_{i}:\left(\mathbf{Spec}\,\left(\mathcal{A}_{i}\right)\to\left(X,\mathcal{O}_{X}\right)\right)\right)_{i\in I}

with each
\mathcal{A}_{i}\in\mathbf{d}{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}\mathbf{Alg}.

Definition 6.14
.

A collection of maps
\left(\varphi_{\alpha}:\mathcal{A}\to\mathcal{A}_{\alpha}\right)_{\alpha}
in
\mathbf{d}{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}\mathbf{Alg}
is a
open covering
if the collection
\left(\mathbf{Spec}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}\left(\varphi_{\alpha}\right):\mathbf{Spec}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}\left(\mathcal{A}_{\alpha}\right)\to\mathbf{Spec}\,\left(\mathcal{A}\right)\right)_{\alpha}
is in
\operatorname{Loc}_{{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}}.
It follows immediately from the fact that
\mathbf{Spec}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}
preserves pullbacks, that the collection of open coverings forms a Grothendieck topology on
\mathbf{d}{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}\mathbf{Alg}^{op}.

Remark 6.15
.

Notice also that, since
L^{\wedge}
preserves coproducts, and
\mathbf{Spec}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}\circ L^{\wedge}\circ i^{\wedge},
the open covering topology restricts to
\left(\mathbf{d}{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}\mathbf{Alg}^{\wedge}\right)^{op}.
In fact, since
\mathbf{Spec}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}
induces an equivalence

\left(\mathbf{d}{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}\mathbf{Alg}^{\wedge}\right)^{op}\simeq\mathbf{Aff}_{{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}},

it follows that a collection of maps
\left(\varphi_{\alpha}:\mathcal{A}\to\mathcal{A}_{\alpha}\right)_{\alpha}
in
\mathbf{d}{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}\mathbf{Alg}^{op}
is an open covering if and only if the collection of maps
\left(L^{\wedge}\left(\varphi_{\alpha}\right):L^{\wedge}\mathcal{A}\to L^{\wedge}\mathcal{A}_{\alpha}\right)_{\alpha}
is.

Let
\widehat{\mathcal{S}\mskip-3.0mu\operatorname{pc}}
be the
\infty
-category of large spaces, and for any
\infty
-category let

\widehat{\Psh}\left(\mathscr{C}\right):=\operatorname{Fun}\left(\mathscr{C}^{op},\widehat{\mathcal{S}\mskip-3.0mu\operatorname{pc}}\right),

(and similarly for sheaves). Then we have a composition of adjunctions

\textstyle{\widehat{\operatorname{Shv}}\left(\left(\mathbf{d}{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}\mathbf{Alg}^{\wedge}\right)^{op}\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\widehat{\operatorname{PShv}}\left(\left(\mathbf{d}{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}\mathbf{Alg}^{\wedge}\right)^{op}\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{a}
\scriptstyle{\left(i^{\wedge}\right)_{*}}
\textstyle{\widehat{\operatorname{PShv}}\left(\left(\mathbf{d}{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}\mathbf{Alg}^{\wedge}\right)^{op}\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\left(i^{\wedge}\right)^{*}}

with
a
denoting sheafification with respect to the induced open covering topology, exhibiting
\widehat{\operatorname{Shv}}\left(\left(\mathbf{d}{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}\mathbf{Alg}^{\wedge}\right)^{op}\right)
as a left exact localization of
\widehat{\operatorname{PShv}}\left(\left(\mathbf{d}{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}\mathbf{Alg}^{\wedge}\right)^{op}\right).
Unwinding the definitions, since
i^{\wedge}\simeq\left(L^{\wedge}\right)_{!},
it is in fact the localization with respect to sieves arising from covering families
\left(\varphi_{\alpha}:\mathcal{A}\to\mathcal{A}_{\alpha}\right)_{\alpha}
in
\mathbf{d}{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}\mathbf{Alg}^{op}
such that
\left(L^{\wedge}\left(\varphi_{\alpha}\right):L^{\wedge}\mathcal{A}\to L^{\wedge}\mathcal{A}_{\alpha}\right)_{\alpha}
is an open covering. But by Remark
6.15
, this is if and only if the original family was an open covering. This implies the following:

Corollary 6.16
.

Restriction along
i^{\wedge}
induce an equivalence of
\infty
-categories

\operatorname{Shv}\left(\mathbf{d}{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}\mathbf{Alg}^{op}\right)\simeq\operatorname{Shv}\left(\left(\mathbf{d}{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}\mathbf{Alg}^{\wedge}\right)^{op}\right),

where sheaves are taken with respect to the open covering topology.

Remark 6.17
.

The open covering topology on
\left(\mathbf{d}{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}\mathbf{Alg}^{\wedge}\right)^{op}
is sub-canonical. However, the open covering topology on
\mathbf{d}{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}\mathbf{Alg}^{op}
is
NOT
sub-canonical. Indeed, since the former is sub-canonical, and
\left(i^{\wedge}\right)^{*}\simeq\left(L^{\wedge}\right)_{!},
it follows that the sheafification of the representable presheaf corresponding to
\mathcal{A}
is the representable sheaf corresponding to
L^{\wedge}\mathcal{A}.

Remark 6.18
.

By Proposition
6.8
, every finitely presented
{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}
-algebra is complete. Moreover, by
[
11
, Remark 4.17]
, these are closed under localizations. So, it follows from Remark
6.2
that if
\mathcal{A}
is finitely presented, all open subsets of
\mathbf{Spec}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}\left(\mathcal{A}\right)
are of the form
\mathbf{Spec}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}\left(\mathcal{A}\left[a^{-1}\right]\right),
for some
a\in\pi_{0}\left(\mathcal{M}\right),
and are thus finitely presented
{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}
-schemes. It follows that the open cover pre-topology restricts to
\left(\mathbf{Alg}_{{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}}\left(\mathcal{S}\mskip-3.0mu\operatorname{pc}\right)^{\mathbf{fp}}\right)^{op}
and is sub-canonical.

Proposition 6.19
.

For
\mathcal{F}:\mathbf{d}{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}\mathbf{Alg}^{op}\to\mathcal{S}\mskip-3.0mu\operatorname{pc}
a sheaf with respect to the open covering topology, for any
\mathcal{A}\in\mathbf{d}{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}\mathbf{Alg},

\mathcal{F}\left(\mathcal{A}\right)\simeq\mathcal{F}\left(L^{\wedge}\mathcal{A}\right).

Proof.

By the above composite of adjunctions, it follows that any sheaf
\mathcal{F}
with respect to the open covering topology is in the essential image of
\left(i^{\wedge}\right)_{*}.
Suppose that

\mathcal{F}=\left(i^{\wedge}\right)_{*}\mathcal{G}.

Then for any
\mathcal{A},

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}\left(\mathcal{A}\right)

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle\mathbb{M}\mathrm{ap}\left(y\left(\mathcal{A}\right),\left(i^{\wedge}\right)_{*}\mathcal{G}\right)

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle\mathbb{M}\mathrm{ap}\left(\left(i^{\wedge}\right)^{*}\left(y\left(\mathcal{A}\right)\right),\mathcal{G}\right)

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle\mathbb{M}\mathrm{ap}\left(\left(L^{\wedge}\right)_{!}\left(y\left(\mathcal{A}\right)\right),\mathcal{G}\right)

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle\mathbb{M}\mathrm{ap}\left(y\left(L^{\wedge}\mathcal{A}\right),\mathcal{G}\right)

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}\left(L^{\wedge}\mathcal{A}\right),

The result now follows by replacing
\mathcal{A}
with
i^{\wedge}L^{\wedge}\mathcal{A}.

∎

In light of this, it is permissible to write
\mathcal{F}\left(\mathbf{Spec}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}\left(\mathcal{A}\right)\right)
instead of
\mathcal{F}\left(\mathcal{A}\right),
even when
\mathcal{A}
is not complete, and we shall do so freely. One is free to interpret the occurrence of
\mathbf{Spec}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}\left(\mathcal{A}\right)
in the above expression as a purely formal placeholder for
\mathcal{A}
to remind one that they are working in the opposite category, or for
\mathcal{F}
to really mean
\mathbf{Spec}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}\mathcal{F}
— since
\mathbf{Spec}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}
yields an equivalence between
\mathbf{Aff}_{{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}}
and
\mathbf{d}{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}\mathbf{Alg}^{op}.

Finally, we make the following observation:

Remark 6.20
.

A presheaf
\mathcal{F}:\mathbf{d}{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}\mathbf{Alg}^{op}\to\mathcal{S}\mskip-3.0mu\operatorname{pc}
is a sheaf for the open covering topology if and only if for each
\mathcal{A}\in\mathbf{d}{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}\mathbf{Alg},

\mathcal{F}|_{\underline{\mathbf{Spec}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}\left(\mathcal{A}\right)}}
is, where the latter is the composite

\displaystyle\mathbf{Bas}\left(\mathbf{Spec}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}\left(\mathcal{A}\right)\right)^{op}

\displaystyle\to

\displaystyle\mathbf{d}{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}\mathbf{Alg}^{op}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathcal{F}}}{{\longrightarrow}}

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}\mskip-3.0mu\operatorname{pc}

\displaystyle U_{a}

\displaystyle\mapsto

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}\left[a^{-1}\right]

\displaystyle\mapsto

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}\left(\mathcal{A}\left[a^{-1}\right]\right),

where
\mathbf{Bas}\left(\mathbf{Spec}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}\left(\mathcal{A}\right)\right)
is the natural basis.

6.1.
Infinite dimensional manifolds as
\mathbf{C}^{\infty}
-schemes

We will show that a very large class of infinite dimensional manifolds embed fully faithfully into
\mathbf{C}^{\infty}
-schemes. We start by discussing their model spaces, which are infinite dimensional vector spaces.

Recall that one of many equivalent ways of defining a locally convex vector space is as follows:

Definition 6.21
.

A
locally convex (real) vector space
(LCVS) is real vector space
V
for which there exists a set
N_{V}=\left\{\rho_{\alpha}:V\to\mathbb{R}\right\}_{\alpha\in A}
of semi-norms such that the addition

+:V\times V\to V

and scalar multiplication

m:\mathbb{R}\times V\to V

maps are continuous when
V
is equipped with the initial topology with respect to the family
N_{V}
of functions to
\mathbb{R}.
If
N_{V}
can be chosen to be countable,
V
is called a
Frechét space
.

Remark 6.22
.

Explicitly, given a family of semi-norms
N_{V}
as above, the topology of
V
is generated by the subbasis

\left\{\rho_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(U\right)\mspace{3mu}:\mspace{3mu}U\subseteq\mathbb{R}\mbox{ open, }\alpha\in A\right\}.

Definition 6.23
.

Suppose that
E
and
F
are LCV spaces. Let
U\subseteq E
be an open subset and let
f:U\to F
be a continuous function, and let
x\in U
and
v\in E.
The
Gâteaux derivative
of
f
at
x
in the direction
v
is

Df_{x}\left(v\right):=\underset{t\to 0}{\mathsf{lim}}\frac{f\left(x+t\cdot v\right)-f\left(x\right)}{t}\in F,

provided this limit exists.

We will say that such an
f
is
\mathbf{C}_{G}^{1}
if
Df_{x}
exists for all
x\in U
and the function

\displaystyle Df:U\times E

\displaystyle\to

\displaystyle F

\displaystyle\left(x,v\right)

\displaystyle\mapsto

\displaystyle Df_{x}\left(v\right)

is continuous. This is essentially what is called
Michal-Bastiani differentiable
in the literature. For
k\geq 1,
We will say that
f
is
\mathbf{C}_{G}^{k+1}
if
f
is continuous and
Df
is
\mathbf{C}^{k}.
Finally, we will say that
f
is
\mathbf{C}^{\infty}_{G}
or Gâteaux smooth if it is
\mathbf{C}_{G}^{k}
for all
k\geq 0.
Denote the set of Gâteaux smooth functions from
U
to
\mathbb{R}
by
\mathbf{C}^{\infty}_{G}\left(U\right).
This has a canonical structure of a
\mathbf{C}^{\infty}
-algebra in an obvious way.

Remark 6.24
.

For Frechét or Banach spaces, this notion of smoothness agrees with Frechét smoothness (although for finite
n,
the classes of
C^{n}
-functions can differ).

Remark 6.25
.

The assignment
U\mapsto\mathbf{C}^{\infty}_{G}\left(U\right)
is clearly a sheaf of
\mathbf{C}^{\infty}
-algebras on the underlying topological space of
E
. Moreover, for all open subsets
U,

\mathbf{C}^{\infty}_{G}\left(U\right)
is closed under locally finite sums.

Besides this notion of smoothness, there exists the notion of
convenient smoothness
of Frölicher and Kriegl:

Definition 6.26
.

A LCVS
E
is a
convenient vector space
if a curve
\gamma:\mathbb{R}\to E
is in
\mathbf{C}^{\infty}_{G}
if and only if for all continuous linear functions
\ell:E\to\mathbb{R},

\ell\circ\gamma\in\mathbf{C}^{\infty}\left(\mathbb{R}\right).

For
E
a convenient vector space, the
c^{\infty}
-topology
on
E
is the final topology with respect to all smooth curves, i.e.
U\subseteq E
is
c^{\infty}
-open if and only if for all smooth curves
\gamma,

\gamma^{-1}\left(U\right)\subseteq\mathbb{R}
is open. We denote
E
equipped with the
c^{\infty}
-topology by
E_{c^{\infty}}
.

For a
c^{\infty}
-open subset
U,
a function
f:U\to\mathbb{R}
is
conveniently smooth
if for all
\gamma:\mathbb{R}\to E
smooth with
\gamma\left(\mathbb{R}\right)\subseteq U,

f\circ\gamma\in\mathbf{C}^{\infty}\left(\mathbb{R}\right).
Denote the set of conveniently smooth functions on
U
by
\mathbf{C}^{\infty}_{C}\left(U\right).
More generally, a set-theoretic function
f:E\to F
between convenient vector spaces is
conveniently smooth
if for all
\mathbf{C}^{\infty}_{G}
-smooth curves
\gamma:\mathbb{R}\to E,

f\circ\gamma
is a
\mathbf{C}^{\infty}_{G}
-smooth curve in
F,
equivalently, for all continuous linear functionals
\ell:E\to\mathbb{R},

\ell\circ f\circ\gamma\in\mathbf{C}^{\infty}\left(\mathbb{R}\right).

Remark 6.27
.

The
c^{\infty}
-topology on a convenient vector space
E
is clearly finer than the locally convex topology, and may not agree with it, that is there can exists subsets
B\subseteq E
such that for all smooth curves
\gamma,

\gamma^{-1}\left(B\right)
is open, but
B
is not open (for the topology generated by the semi-norms). In fact,
E_{c^{\infty}}
may fail to be a topological vector space with the
c^{\infty}
-topology. Nonetheless, it is easily checked by using the sheaf condition on
\mathbb{R},
that the assignment to each
c^{\infty}
-open subset
U
the set of conveniently smooth functions
\mathbf{C}^{\infty}_{C}\left(U\right)
is a sheaf for the
c^{\infty}
-topology. It should be pointed out that a function
f:E\to F
which is conveniently smooth may fail to be continuous for the given locally convex topologies, even when
F=\mathbb{R},
(but is of course continuous for their respective
c^{\infty}
-topologies).

There is a large class of convenient vector spaces for which the
c^{\infty}
-topology
does
coincide with the given locally convex topology
[
19
, Theorem 4.11]
. These include any
E
which is:

•

a sequential space (equivalently the quotient of a metric space), e.g. any Frechét space.

•

the strong dual of a Frechét-Schwartz space.

Proposition 6.28
.

[
19
, Section 16.21]
