For any convenient vector space
E,
there is a basis for the
c^{\infty}
-topology which consists of open subsets which are conveniently diffeomorphic to
E_{c^{\infty}}
itself.

Definition 6.29
.

Let
\left(X,\mathcal{O}_{X}\right)
be a
\mathbf{C}^{\infty}
-ringed space with
\mathcal{O}_{X}
a subsheaf of the sheaf of continuous functions. We say that it is
smoothly regular
if
X
carries the final topology with respect to all functions of the form

\displaystyle X

\displaystyle\longrightarrow

\displaystyle\mathbb{R}

\displaystyle x

\displaystyle\mapsto

\displaystyle a\left(x\right),

for some
a\in\Gamma\left(\mathcal{O}_{X}\right).

Proposition 6.30
.

[
19
, Proposition 14.4]

Let
E
be a LCVS. Then
\left(E,\mathbf{C}^{\infty}_{G}\right)
is smoothly regular if and only if the locally convex topology of
E
is generated by semi-norms
\rho_{\alpha}
for which

\rho_{\alpha}:\rho_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(\mathbb{R}\setminus\{0\}\right)\to\mathbb{R}

is in
\mathbf{C}^{\infty}_{G}
for all
\alpha.
(These could be different than the original semi-norms chosen, so this is a question of the existence of smooth ones.) Similarly, if
E
is convenient,
\left(E_{c^{\infty}},\mathbf{C}^{\infty}_{C}\right)
is smoothly regular if the
c^{\infty}
-topology on
E
is generated by semi-norms
\rho_{\alpha}
for which

\rho_{\alpha}:\rho_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(\mathbb{R}\setminus\{0\}\right)\to\mathbb{R}

is in
\mathbf{C}^{\infty}_{C}
for all
\alpha.

Remark 6.31
.

The above holds for Nuclear Frechét spaces, and in this case, the
c^{\infty}
-topology coincides with the original locally convex one, and
C^{\infty}_{G}=C^{\infty}_{C}.
On the other extreme, there are Banach spaces which do not even admit a
C^{1}
-norm.

Lemma 6.32
.

Let
E
be a convenient vector space, and let
x\in E.
Then any germ of a smooth function
\mathsf{germ}_{x}f
defined on a
c^{\infty}
-open neighborhood
U
of
x
has a representation as a conveniently smooth function
h:E\to\mathbb{R}
.

Proof.

This follows since
x
has a neighborhood basis of
c^{\infty}
-open subsets which are
c^{\infty}
-diffeomorphic to
E_{c^{\infty}}
by Proposition
6.28
.
∎

Definition 6.33
.

A locally convex space
E
or convenient vector space is
realcompact
if the function of sets

\displaystyle E

\displaystyle\longrightarrow

\displaystyle\mathbb{H}\mathrm{om}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}\mathbf{Alg}}\left(\mathbf{C}^{\infty}\left(E\right),\mathbb{R}\right)

\displaystyle e

\displaystyle\mapsto

\displaystyle ev_{e},

is a bijection, where
ev_{e}\left(f\right)=f\left(e\right).

Proposition 6.34
.

For a locally convex vector space
E
or convenient vector space,
\left(E,\mathbf{C}^{\infty}_{G}\right)
or respectively
\left(E_{c^{\infty}},\mathbf{C}^{\infty}_{C}\right)
is an affine
\mathbf{C}^{\infty}
-scheme if and only if
E
smoothly regular, realcompact, and every germ of a
\mathbf{C}^{\infty}
-function has a global representative.

Proof.

One direction is obvious since any affine
\mathbf{C}^{\infty}
-scheme has these properties. Let us prove the converse. We will be agnostic as to whether
E
is convenient or not, and
E
will denote either
E
with the locally convex topology, or the
c^{\infty}
-topology.

Since
E
is realcompact, we can identify
E=\mathbb{H}\mathrm{om}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}\mathbf{Alg}}\left(\mathbf{C}^{\infty}\left(E\right),\mathbb{R}\right)
as a set. Since
E
is smoothly regular, the topology of
E
is generated by subsets of the form
f^{-1}\left(\mathbb{R}\setminus\{0\}\right),
for
f\in\mathbf{C}^{\infty}\left(E\right),
but this is the same topology as that on
\mathbb{H}\mathrm{om}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}\mathbf{Alg}}\left(\mathbf{C}^{\infty}\left(E\right),\mathbb{R}\right)
coming from the
\mathbf{Spec}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}
-construction. It suffices to prove that the structure sheaves agree. For this, we will show that their stalks agree at each point
x\in E.
Notice that for each
f\in\mathbf{C}^{\infty}\left(E\right),
letting
U_{f}=f^{-1}\left(\mathbb{R}\setminus\{0\}\right),

f|_{U_{f}}
is a unit, and hence there is an induced homomorphism

\mathbf{C}^{\infty}\left(E\right)\left[f^{-1}\right]\to\mathbf{C}^{\infty}\left(U\right).

Recall that the sheaf
\mathcal{O}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}\left(E\right)}
is defined as the sheafification of a presheaf
\tilde{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}\left(E\right)},
and the left hand side is precisely what the presheaf
\tilde{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}\left(E\right)}
assigns
U_{f}.
It follows that we get an induced map of sheaves

\mathcal{O}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}\left(E\right)}\to\mathbf{C}^{\infty}_{E}.

It suffices to show that this morphism induces an isomorphism on stalks. The stalk at
x\in E
of the right hand side is simply the
\mathbf{C}^{\infty}
-ring of germs of smooth functions at
x.
The stalks on the left hand side can be computed directly from the presheaf. There is a neighborhood basis
\mathcal{U}_{x}
of
x
consisting of open subsets of the form
U_{f}=f^{-1}\left(\mathbb{R}\setminus\{0\}\right),
ranging over all
f
for which
f\left(x\right)\neq 0.
Note that these are precise those
f
such that
f\in\mathbf{C}^{\infty}\left(E\right)\setminus\mathfrak{m}_{x}.
The stalk of
\tilde{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}\left(E\right)}
at
x
is therefore the filtered colimit over all such
f
of
\mathbf{C}^{\infty}\left(E\right)\left[f^{-1}\right],
but this is just the
\mathbf{C}^{\infty}
-localization at the compliment of
\mathfrak{m}_{x}.
We claim that the
\mathbf{C}^{\infty}
-localization of
\mathbf{C}^{\infty}\left(E\right)
at this muliplicatively closed subset coincides with the algebraic localization. For this, it suffices to prove that the algebraic localization is a
\mathbf{C}^{\infty}
-algebra, but since
\mathfrak{m}_{x}
is maximal, the algebraic localization is a local ring, so this is obvious. Elements of the algebraic localization are generalized fractions of the form
\frac{g}{s},
with
s
a smooth function such that
s\left(x\right)\neq 0.
We have that
\frac{g}{s}=0
in the localization if and only if there exists
s^{\prime}
smooth such that
s^{\prime}\left(x\right)\neq 0
and such that
s^{\prime}\cdot g=0.
The induced map on stalks sends this generalized fraction to the quotient of the germ of
g
at
x
by the germ of
s
at
x.
So,
\frac{g}{s}
is in the kernel if and only if
\mathsf{germ}_{x}\left(g\right)=0.
This means that there exists an open neighborhood
U
of
x
such that
g|_{U}=0.
Since
E
is smoothly regular, we can shrink
U
so that
U=f^{-1}\left(\mathbb{R}\setminus\{0\}\right),
for some
f\in\mathbf{C}^{\infty}\left(E\right).
But now
f\cdot g=0
, and
\mathbf{germ}_{x}\left(f\right)\neq 0.
It follows that the homomorphism is injective at the level of stalks. It suffices to prove that it is surjective, but this is precisely the statement that every germ of a smooth function has a global representative, so we are done.
∎

Corollary 6.35
.

The following classes of LCVS and convenient vector spaces are affine
\mathbf{C}^{\infty}
-schemes when equipped with their appropriate sheaf of smooth functions:

1.

Any smoothly regular LCVS which admits smooth partitions of unity. This includes:

a)

Any metrizable LCVS for which smooth functions separate disjoint closed sets.

b)

All Hilbert spaces.

c)

All Nuclear Frechét spaces.

2.

All realcompact smoothly regular convenient vector spaces (with the
c^{\infty}
-topology).

3.

Any smoothly regular LCVS which is Lindelöf. Any smoothly regular convenient vector space which is Lindelöf for its
c^{\infty}
-topology.

Proof.

1.
follows since admitting smooth partitions of unity allows one to construct global representatives for germs.
2.
follows since by Lemma
6.32
, any such space admits global representatives for germs.
3.
follows from
[
15
, Theorem 4.41]
. Alternatively, by
[
19
, Theorem 16.10]
, any such space has smooth partitions of unity, so the result follows from 1.

∎

Remark 6.36
.

Let
\left(E,\mathbf{C}^{\infty}_{E}\right)
and
\left(F,\mathbf{C}^{\infty}_{F}\right)
be locally convex vector spaces as above, regarded as affine
\mathbf{C}^{\infty}
-schemes. Since each structure sheaf is a subsheaf of the sheaf of continuous functions, a morphism

\varphi:\left(E,\mathbf{C}^{\infty}_{E}\right)\to\left(F,\mathbf{C}^{\infty}_{F}\right)

of
\mathbf{C}^{\infty}
-ringed spaces is the same as a function of underlying sets
\varphi:E\to F
such that
f\circ\varphi\in\mathbf{C}^{\infty}_{G}\left(E\right)
for all
f\in\mathbf{C}^{\infty}_{G}\left(F\right).
Denoting
\mathsf{LCVS_{s}}
the full subcategory of locally convex real vector spaces and
\mathbf{C}^{\infty}_{G}
-smooth maps on those which are smoothly regular (i.e. there exists a family of semi-norms generating the topology which are
C^{\infty}_{G}
away from their zero-set), realcompact, and such that germs of smooth functions have global representatives, there is a functor

\displaystyle\mathsf{LCVS}_{s}

\displaystyle\to

\displaystyle\mathbf{Aff}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}

\displaystyle E

\displaystyle\mapsto

\displaystyle\left(E,\mathbf{C}^{\infty}_{E}\right).

This functor is faithful, but it is not full.

Consider now instead, the full subcategory of convenient vector spaces and conveniently smooth maps on those which are smoothly regular and realcompact, and denote it by
\mathsf{Conv}_{s}.
Then the corresponding functor

\displaystyle\mathsf{Conv}_{s}

\displaystyle\to

\displaystyle\mathbf{Aff}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}

\displaystyle E

\displaystyle\mapsto

\displaystyle\left(E_{c^{\infty}},\mathbf{C}^{\infty}_{E}\right)

is
fully faithful. To see this, we need to show that a function
\varphi:E\to F
is conveniently smooth if and only if
f\circ\varphi\in\mathbf{C}^{\infty}_{C}\left(E\right)
for all
f\in\mathbf{C}^{\infty}_{C}\left(F\right).
If
\varphi
is conveniently smooth, so is
f\circ\varphi,
so one direction is clear. Conversely, suppose that
f\circ\varphi\in\mathbf{C}^{\infty}_{C}\left(E\right)
for all
f\in\mathbf{C}^{\infty}_{C}\left(F\right).
Let
\gamma:\mathbb{R}\to E
be a smooth curve. We need to show that
f\circ\gamma:\mathbb{R}\to F
is a smooth curve. But since
F
is convenient, this is if and only if for all
\ell:F\to\mathbb{R}
linear and continuous,
\ell\circ\left(f\circ\gamma\right)\in\mathbf{C}^{\infty}\left(\mathbb{R}\right).
Since
\ell\in\mathbf{C}^{\infty}_{C}\left(F\right),
we have by hypothesis that
\ell\circ f\in\mathbf{C}^{\infty}_{C}\left(E\right),
and thus
\gamma\circ\ell\circ f
is also smooth.

Thus, if
E
is not convenient, then even when
\left(E,\mathbf{C}^{\infty}_{E}\right)
is an affine
\mathbf{C}^{\infty}
-scheme, it may not have the same functor of points as
E,
regarding
E
as an infinite-dimensional manifold.

Definition 6.37
.

A
convenient manifold
is a topological space
M,
together with charts with values in convenient vector spaces whose transition maps are conveniently smooth. We call a convenient manifold
admissible
if it has a chart with values in
\mathsf{Conv}_{s}
.

Corollary 6.38
.

The category of admissible convenient manifolds embeds fully faithfully into
\mathbf{C}^{\infty}
-schemes. In particular, so does the full subcategory of Frechét manifolds locally modeled on nuclear Frechét spaces. We denote by

S_{\mathsf{Conv}}:\mathsf{Mfd}_{\mathsf{Conv}_{s}}\hookrightarrow\mathbf{C}^{\infty}\mathsf{Sch}

the fully faithful embedding.

Remark 6.39
.

By
[
15
, Theorem 4.41]
, if
M
is an admissible convenient manifold which is moreover Lindelöf,
S_{\mathsf{Conv}}\left(M\right)
is moreover affine.

Since
\mathbf{C}^{\infty}
-schemes embed fully faithfully into
\operatorname{Shv}\left(\mathbf{d}{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}\mathbf{Alg}^{op}\right),
this gives a fully faithful embedding of admissible convenient manifolds into sheaves via their functor of points. There is another way to fully faithful embed convenient manifolds into sheaves that works even without the admissibility assumption, but the embedding is different. Namely, the category of smoothly regular convenient manifolds embeds fully faithfully into the category of Frölicher spaces
[
19
, Lemma 27.5]
, and these in turn embed fully faithfully into the category of sheaves on (finite dimensional) manifolds
\operatorname{Shv}\left(\mathsf{Mfd}\right)
(and moreover, factors through the inclusion of diffeological spaces). Finally, if

sm:\mathsf{Mfd}\hookrightarrow\mathbf{d}{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}\mathbf{Alg}^{op}

denotes the fully faithful inclusion, there is an induced restriction functor

sm^{*}:\operatorname{Shv}\left(\mathbf{d}{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}\mathbf{Alg}^{op}\right)\to\operatorname{Shv}\left(\mathsf{Mfd}\right)

which admits a left adjoint
sm_{!},
since the covers for a finite dimensional manifold in either site are the same. Since
sm
is fully faithful, so is
sm_{!},
and by composition we get a fully faithful embedding of the category of smoothly regular convenient manifolds into the topos
\operatorname{Shv}\left(\mathbf{d}{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}\mathbf{Alg}^{op}\right).

Now suppose that
M
is an
admissible
convenient manifold. Then
S_{\mathsf{Conv}}\left(M\right)
is a
\mathbf{C}^{\infty}
-scheme, and moreover, its functor of points on manifolds is
sm^{*}S_{\mathsf{Conv}}\left(M\right).
So we wish to compare

S_{\mathsf{Conv}}\left(M\right)
with
sm_{!}sm^{*}S_{\mathsf{Conv}}\left(M\right),
i.e. we wish to determine if the co-unit

\epsilon_{M}:sm_{!}sm^{*}S_{\mathsf{Conv}}\left(M\right)\to S_{\mathsf{Conv}}\left(M\right)

is an equivalence. Let
F\in\operatorname{Shv}\left(\mathsf{Mfd}\right)
be arbitrary. Since we could equivalently take sheaves on
\mathbf{C}^{\infty}
—i.e. the full subcategory of manifolds on those which are of the form
\mathbb{R}^{n}
for some
n,
the canonical map

\underset{f:\mathbb{R}^{n}\to M,n\in\mathbb{N}}{\coprod}\mathbb{R}^{n}\to\tilde{y}\left(M\right)

is an effective epimorphism. Since
sm_{!}
is a left-adjoint, we get that

\pi:\underset{f:\mathbb{R}^{n}\to M,n\in\mathbb{N}}{\coprod}\mathbb{R}^{n}\to sm_{!}sm^{*}S_{\mathsf{Conv}}\left(M\right)

is an effective epimorphism in
\operatorname{Shv}\left(\mathbf{d}{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}\mathbf{Alg}^{op}\right).
If
\epsilon_{M}
were an equivalence, since
S_{\mathsf{Conv}}\left(M\right)
is a
\mathbf{C}^{\infty}
-scheme,
\pi
would need to admit local sections. That means around every
x\in M,
there exists an open subset
U
such the inclusion
U\hookrightarrow M
factors through a map
f:\mathbb{R}^{n}\to M
via a smooth map
\lambda:U\to\mathbb{R}^{n}.
This would imply in particular that

Tf_{\lambda\left(x\right)}:T_{\lambda\left(x\right)}\mathbb{R}^{n}\to T_{x}M

is surjective, which would imply that
M
is finite dimensional.

Corollary 6.40
.

For an admissible convenient manifold,

\epsilon_{M}:sm_{!}sm^{*}S_{\mathsf{Conv}}\left(M\right)\to S_{\mathsf{Conv}}\left(M\right)

is an equivalence if and only if
M
is finite dimensional.

There are two main ways that infinite dimensional manifolds naturally arise. One is via the mapping space between two finite dimensional manifolds, or more generally, as the space of sections of some fiber bundle. Another, is by a pro-system of finite dimensional manifolds. Depending on which of the ways the manifold shows up, one may prefer to use one embedding over the other. On one hand, for the former case,
[
35
, Proposition 3.2.6]
implies that for
E\to M
a proper submersion of finite dimensional manifolds, the relative mapping stack
\Gamma_{M}\left(E\right)
is represented by the image under
sm_{!}
of the nuclear Frechét manifold of sections. On the other hand, for the latter case, consider the following two examples:

Example 6.41
.

The projective limit
\mathbb{R}^{\infty}
with its standard Frechét structure is the projective limit
\underset{k}{\varprojlim\mspace{3mu}}\mathbb{R}^{k}
in the category of nuclear Frechét manifolds.

Example 6.42
.

More generally, for any surjective submersion

\pi:E\to M

of finite dimensional manifolds, its collection of jet bundles
\mathcal{J}^{k}\left(E,M\right)
forms a projective system of finite dimensional manifolds, and the infinite jet bundle
\mathcal{J}^{\infty}\left(E,M\right)
with its standard Frechét structure is the projective limit in the category of nuclear Frechét manifolds. It is locally diffeomorphic to
\mathbb{R}^{\infty}.

Lemma 6.43
.

Let
\mathbb{R}^{\infty}
be equipped with its standard Frechét structure. Then

\mathbf{C}^{\infty}\left(\mathbb{R}^{\infty}\right)\simeq\Gamma\left(\mathbf{Spec}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}\left(\underrightarrow{\mbox{colim}\vskip 0.5pt}\mspace{4mu}\mathbf{C}^{\infty}\left(\mathbb{R}^{k}\right)\right)\right).

Proof.

Since
\underline{\mathbf{Spec}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}}:\mathbf{d}{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}\mathbf{Alg}^{op}\to\mathbf{Top}
preserves limits, we have an identification of topological spaces between
\mathbb{R}^{\infty}
and
\underline{\mathbf{Spec}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}}\left(\underrightarrow{\mbox{colim}\vskip 0.5pt}\mspace{4mu}\mathbf{C}^{\infty}\left(\mathbb{R}^{k}\right)\right).
The structures sheaf of
\mathbf{Spec}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}\left(\underrightarrow{\mbox{colim}\vskip 0.5pt}\mspace{4mu}\mathbf{C}^{\infty}\left(\mathbb{R}^{k}\right)\right)
is the sheafification of the presheaf that assigns an open subset
U
of
\mathbb{R}^{\infty}
the set of set-theoretic functions

U\to\mathbb{R}

which factor as

U\to p_{k}\left(U\right)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle f}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathbb{R},

with
p_{k}:\mathbb{R}^{\infty}\to\mathbb{R}^{k}
the projection, and with
f
smooth. The sheafification is then all functions that are
locally
of this form. But these are precisely the smooth functions from the inherited Frechét manifold structure on
U\subset\mathbb{R}^{\infty}.

∎

Corollary 6.44
.

For any surjective submersion

\pi:E\to M

of finite dimensional manifolds,

\mathbf{C}^{\infty}\left(\mathcal{J}^{\infty}\left(E,M\right)\right)\simeq\Gamma\left(\mathbf{Spec}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}\left(\underrightarrow{\mbox{colim}\vskip 0.5pt}\mspace{4mu}\mathbf{C}^{\infty}\left(\mathcal{J}^{k}\left(E,M\right)\right)\right)\right).

7.
The derived topos

7.1.
The derived site

Let
\mathscr{D}
be a small subcategory of the the opposite category of
\mathbf{d}{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}\mathbf{Alg}
such that
1
1

1

We will also tacitly assume each algebra
\mathcal{A}
in
\mathscr{D}
is complete. Otherwise, after choosing a subcategory
\mathscr{D}
satisfying
1)
-
4)
, one can replace each algebra with its completion.

1)

\mathscr{D}
contains all the compact objects— i.e. (homotopically) finitely presented algebras

2)

For all
\mathcal{A}\in\mathscr{D},
and
n\in\mathbb{N}\cup\left\{-1\right\},

\tau_{\leq n}\left(\mathcal{A}\right)\in\mathscr{D},
where
\tau_{\leq-1}\mathcal{A}
is the reduction of
\pi_{0}\left(\mathcal{A}\right).

3)

\mathscr{D}
is closed under open coverings

4)

\mathscr{D}
is closed under finite limits and retracts.

Definition 7.1
.

The
\infty
-category
\mathscr{D}
together with the open covering Grothendieck topology is called the
derived site
. Moreover, the associated
\infty
-topos

\operatorname{Shv}\left(\mathscr{D}\right)=:\mathcal{E}_{\mathfrak{Der}}

will be called the
derived topos
. Objects of this topos will be called
derived stacks
.

Proposition 7.2
.

\mathcal{E}_{\mathfrak{Der}}
is hypercomplete.

Proof.

It suffices to prove that for each
D\in\mathscr{D},

\mathcal{E}_{\mathfrak{Der}}/D
is hypercomplete. To this end, it suffices to show that each representable
D\in\mathcal{E}_{\mathfrak{Der}}
is a hypersheaf. Let
\mathbf{Spec}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}\left(B\right)
be a representable and let
\mathbf{Spec}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}\left(\mathcal{A}\right)
be another. By Remark
6.20
, it suffices to prove that the restriction of
\mathbf{Spec}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}\left(\mathsf{B}\right)
to
\mathbf{Bas}\left(\mathbf{Spec}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}\left(\mathcal{A}\right)\right)
is a hypersheaf. Without loss of generality, assume that
\mathcal{B}
is complete, so that this restriction can be identified with
\mathbb{M}\mathrm{ap}_{\mathbf{d}{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}\mathbf{Alg}}\left(\mathcal{B},\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{A}}\left(\mspace{3mu}\cdot\mspace{3mu}\right)\right).
It follows from
[
14
, Lemma 5.1.25]
that
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{A}}
is a hypersheaf. The result now follows.
∎

7.2.
Adjunctions and Monads

Denote by

i:\mathscr{D}^{0}\hookrightarrow\mathscr{D}

the full subcategory of classical
{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}
-algebras, and denote by

l:\mathscr{D}_{red}\hookrightarrow\mathscr{D}_{0}

the full subcategory thereof on the reduced algebras. Finally, denote by
k:=i\circ l.
Note that
i
has a right adjoint
\pi_{0}
, which sends
\mathbf{Spec}_{C^{\infty}}\left(A\right)
to spec of its
0
-truncation
\mathbf{Spec}_{C^{\infty}}\left(\pi_{0}\left(A\right)\right),
and
l
has a right adjoint
R
which sends
\mathbf{Spec}_{C^{\infty}}\left(A\right)
to
\mathbf{Spec}_{C^{\infty}}\left(A/\mathbf{Nil}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}\left(A\right)\right)
. Denote the composite
R\circ\pi_{0}
by
p
; it is right adjoint to
k.
Note that
p
sends
\mathbf{Spec}_{C^{\infty}}\left(\mathcal{A}\right)
to
\mathbf{Spec}_{C^{\infty}}\left(\mathcal{A}_{red}\right).

Note that all
6
of these functors preserve covers, consequently there are are many induced adjunctions induced by Kan extensions:

\textstyle{\operatorname{Shv}\left(\mathscr{D}^{0}\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i_{!}}
\scriptstyle{i^{\ast}\simeq\left(\pi_{0}\right)_{!}}
\scriptstyle{i_{\ast}\simeq\left(\pi_{0}\right)^{\ast}}
\scriptstyle{\left(\pi_{0}\right)_{\ast}}
\textstyle{\;\operatorname{Shv}\left(\mathscr{D}\right)}

\textstyle{\operatorname{Shv}\left(\mathscr{D}_{red}\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{l_{!}}
\scriptstyle{l^{\ast}\simeq R_{!}}
\scriptstyle{l_{\ast}\simeq R^{\ast}}
\scriptstyle{R_{\ast}}
\textstyle{\;\operatorname{Shv}\left(\mathscr{D}_{0}\right)}

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 24.95654pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-24.95654pt\raise 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\;\operatorname{Shv}\left(\mathscr{D}\right)}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\,.

Definition 7.3
.

An derived stack
\mathcal{X}
is
classical
if it is in the essential image of
i_{!}
. Denote the full subcategory on the classical stacks by
\mathcal{E}_{\mathfrak{Cl}}.

Since
i
is fully faithful so is
i_{!},
and hence
\mathcal{E}_{\mathfrak{Cl}}
is a coreflective subcategory of
\mathcal{E}_{\mathfrak{Der}}.
In particular, it is closed under colimits.

Proposition 7.4
.

The subcategory
\mathcal{E}_{\mathfrak{Cl}}
is closed under finite products.

Proof.

\mathcal{E}_{\mathfrak{Cl}}
clearly contains the terminal object, so it suffices to check it is closed under binary products. Let
i_{!}\mathcal{X}
and
i_{!}\mathcal{Y}
be classical stacks. Then

i_{!}\mathcal{X}\simeq\underset{\mathbf{Spec}_{C^{\infty}}\left(A\right)\to\mathcal{X}}{\underrightarrow{\mbox{colim}\vskip 0.5pt}\mspace{4mu}}i_{!}\mathbf{Spec}_{C^{\infty}}\left(A\right)

and

i_{!}\mathcal{Y}\simeq\underset{\mathbf{Spec}_{C^{\infty}}\left(B\right)\to\mathcal{Y}}{\underrightarrow{\mbox{colim}\vskip 0.5pt}\mspace{4mu}}i_{!}\mathbf{Spec}_{C^{\infty}}\left(B\right)

where in both colimits,
A
and
B
range over objects in
\mathcal{D}_{0}.
By abuse of notation,

i_{!}\mathbf{Spec}_{C^{\infty}}\left(A\right)=\mathbf{Spec}_{C^{\infty}}\left(A\right)

(more accurately,
i_{!}y_{\mathcal{D}_{0}}\left(\mathbf{Spec}_{C^{\infty}}\left(A\right)\right)\simeq y_{\mathscr{D}}\left(i\left(\mathbf{Spec}_{C^{\infty}}\left(A\right)\right)\right)
). Hence

\displaystyle\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{Y}

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle\left(\underset{\mathbf{Spec}_{C^{\infty}}\left(A\right)\to\mathcal{X}}{\underrightarrow{\mbox{colim}\vskip 0.5pt}\mspace{4mu}}\mathbf{Spec}_{C^{\infty}}\left(A\right)\right)\times\left(\underset{\mathbf{Spec}_{C^{\infty}}\left(B\right)\to\mathcal{X}}{\underrightarrow{\mbox{colim}\vskip 0.5pt}\mspace{4mu}}\mathbf{Spec}_{C^{\infty}}\left(B\right)\right)

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle\underset{\mathbf{Spec}_{C^{\infty}}\left(A\right)\to\mathcal{X}}{\underrightarrow{\mbox{colim}\vskip 0.5pt}\mspace{4mu}}\mspace{5mu}\underset{\mathbf{Spec}_{C^{\infty}}\left(B\right)\to\mathcal{Y}}{\underrightarrow{\mbox{colim}\vskip 0.5pt}\mspace{4mu}}\left(\mathbf{Spec}_{C^{\infty}}\left(A\right)\times\mathbf{Spec}_{C^{\infty}}\left(B\right)\right)

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle\underset{\mathbf{Spec}_{C^{\infty}}\left(A\right)\to\mathcal{X}}{\underrightarrow{\mbox{colim}\vskip 0.5pt}\mspace{4mu}}\mspace{5mu}\underset{\mathbf{Spec}_{C^{\infty}}\left(B\right)\to\mathcal{Y}}{\underrightarrow{\mbox{colim}\vskip 0.5pt}\mspace{4mu}}\left(\mathbf{Spec}_{C^{\infty}}\left(A\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\mkern-13.73mu\resizebox{1305270}{540744}{$\phantom{x}{}_{\phantom{.}{}^{\infty}}$}$\crcr$\displaystyle\bigcirc$\crcr}}}}{\mathbin{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\mkern-13.73mu\resizebox{1305270}{540744}{$\phantom{x}{}_{\phantom{.}{}^{\infty}}$}$\crcr$\textstyle\bigcirc$\crcr}}}}{\mathbin{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\mkern-13.73mu\resizebox{1305270}{540744}{$\phantom{x}{}_{\phantom{.}{}^{\infty}}$}$\crcr$\scriptstyle\bigcirc$\crcr}}}}{\mathbin{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-13.73mu\resizebox{1305270}{540744}{$\phantom{x}{}_{\phantom{.}{}^{\infty}}$}$\crcr$\scriptscriptstyle\bigcirc$\crcr}}}}B\right)\right)

which is in
\mathcal{E}_{\mathfrak{Cl}},
since each
\mathbf{Spec}_{C^{\infty}}\left(A\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\mkern-13.73mu\resizebox{1305270}{540744}{$\phantom{x}{}_{\phantom{.}{}^{\infty}}$}$\crcr$\displaystyle\bigcirc$\crcr}}}}{\mathbin{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\mkern-13.73mu\resizebox{1305270}{540744}{$\phantom{x}{}_{\phantom{.}{}^{\infty}}$}$\crcr$\textstyle\bigcirc$\crcr}}}}{\mathbin{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\mkern-13.73mu\resizebox{1305270}{540744}{$\phantom{x}{}_{\phantom{.}{}^{\infty}}$}$\crcr$\scriptstyle\bigcirc$\crcr}}}}{\mathbin{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-13.73mu\resizebox{1305270}{540744}{$\phantom{x}{}_{\phantom{.}{}^{\infty}}$}$\crcr$\scriptscriptstyle\bigcirc$\crcr}}}}B\right)
is and
\mathcal{E}_{\mathfrak{Cl}}
is closed under colimits.
∎

7.3.
Spaces of sections

Recall that for any
\infty
-topos
\mathcal{E},
and an object
E\in\mathcal{E},
there is a canonical geometric morphism

\pi_{E}:\mathcal{E}/E\to\mathcal{E}.

The inverse image functor is given by pullback:

\displaystyle\pi_{E}^{*}:\mathcal{E}

\displaystyle\to

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}/E

\displaystyle F

\displaystyle\mapsto

\displaystyle F\times E\to E.

This functor has both a left and a right adjoint:

\left(\pi_{E}\right)_{!}\dashv\pi_{E}^{*}\dashv\left(\pi_{E}\right)_{*}.

The left adjoint is the functor:

\displaystyle\left(\pi_{E}\right)_{!}:\mathcal{E}/E\to\mathcal{E}

\displaystyle f:F\to E

\displaystyle\mapsto

\displaystyle F.

Notice that if
M\in\mathscr{D},
that by the Yoneda lemma it follows that for all
N\in\mathscr{D}
and
f:E\to M,

\displaystyle\left(\pi_{M}\right)_{*}\left(f\right)\left(N\right)

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle\mathbb{M}\mathrm{ap}_{\mathcal{E}_{\mathfrak{Der}}}\left(N,\left(\pi_{M}\right)_{*}\left(f\right)\right)

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle\mathbb{M}\mathrm{ap}_{\mathcal{E}_{\mathfrak{Der}}/M}\left(\pi^{*}_{M}\left(N\right),f\right)

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle\mathbb{M}\mathrm{ap}_{\mathcal{E}_{\mathfrak{Der}}/M}\left(N\times M\to M,f\right),

where the latter is the space of sections

\textstyle{E\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f}
\textstyle{N\times M\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{pr_{N}}
\scriptstyle{\sigma}
\textstyle{M.}

Definition 7.5
.

Given
f:E\to M
in
\mathcal{E}_{\mathfrak{Der}},
the
space of sections of
f
is the stack

\mathbf{\Gamma}_{M}\left(f\right):=\left(\pi_{M}\right)_{*}\left(f\right)

Remark 7.6
.

Notice that for
E,F\in\mathcal{E}_{\mathfrak{Der}},
the internal Hom

F^{E}=\underline{\mathbb{M}\mathrm{ap}}_{\mathcal{E}_{\mathfrak{Der}}}\left(E,F\right)=\mathbf{\Gamma}\left(\pi_{F}^{*}E\right),

and conversely, if
f:E\to F,
there is a pullback diagram

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\left\{id_{F}\right\}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}{\hbox{\kern-25.29797pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\line@@}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces==\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0625pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces==\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0625pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern-11.68509pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0625pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0625pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{F^{F}.}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces

8.
Derived Orbifolds

This section will follow
[
7
]
. (See also
[
21
, Chapter 20]
, where these ideas are expanded upon from a different point of view.)

Definition 8.1
.

A
{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}
-ringed
\infty
-topos
is a pair
\left(\mathcal{E},\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E}}\right)
with
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E}}
a
{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}
-algebra in
\mathcal{E},
i.e. a finite product preserving functor

\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E}}:\mathbf{C}^{\infty}\to\mathcal{E}.

Equivalently, by abuse of notation, this is an
\infty
-topos
\mathcal{E}
equipped with a geometric morphism

\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E}}:\mathcal{E}\to\mathcal{B}{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}},

where
\mathcal{B}{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}
is the classifying
\infty
-topos of the algebraic theory
{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}.

The
\infty
-category of
{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}
-ringed
\infty
-topoi, denoted
\mathfrak{Top}_{\infty}^{{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}},
may be defined informally as the
\infty
-category whose objects are
{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}
-ringed
\infty
-topoi, and whose morphisms

\left(\mathcal{E},\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E}}\right)\to\left(\mathcal{F},\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{F}}\right)

consists of a geometric morphism

f:\mathcal{E}\to\mathcal{F},

together with a morphism of sheaves of
{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}
-algebras over
\mathcal{F}

\alpha:\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{F}}\to f_{*}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E}}.

This can be rigorously constructed as a Cartesian fibration over
\mathfrak{Top}_{\infty},
c.f.
[
7
, Section 2.4]
.

Remark 8.2
.

By Theorem
2.15
, specifying a finite product preserving functor

{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}\to\mathcal{E}

is the same as specifying a limit preserving functor

\mathbf{Alg}_{{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}}\left(\mathcal{S}\mskip-3.0mu\operatorname{pc}\right)^{op}\to\mathcal{E},

which is the same as a colimit preserving functor

\mathbf{Alg}_{{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}}\left(\mathcal{S}\mskip-3.0mu\operatorname{pc}\right)\to\mathcal{E}^{op},

which is completely determined by its right adjoint (which must exist) which is a limit preserving functor

\mathcal{E}^{op}\to\mathbf{Alg}_{{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}}\left(\mathcal{S}\mskip-3.0mu\operatorname{pc}\right),

i.e. a sheaf on
\mathcal{E}
with values in
\mathbf{Alg}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}\left(\mathcal{S}\mskip-3.0mu\operatorname{pc}\right).
If
\mathcal{E}\simeq\operatorname{Shv}\left(\mathscr{C},J\right)
for a Grothendieck site
\left(\mathscr{C},J\right),
then this is the same data as a sheaf on
\left(\mathscr{C},J\right)
with values in
\mathbf{Alg}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}\left(\mathcal{S}\mskip-3.0mu\operatorname{pc}\right).
Applying this to the case that the Grothendieck site is the poset of open subsets of a topological space
X
with the standard open covered topology, a finite product preserving functor

{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}\to\operatorname{Shv}\left(X\right)

is the same as a sheaf of
{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}
-rings on
X.
So if
\left(X,\mathcal{O}_{X}\right)
is any
{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}
-ringed space, then
\left(\operatorname{Shv}\left(X\right),\mathcal{O}_{X}\right)
is an
{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}
-ringed
\infty
-topos.

Definition 8.3
.

Given a
{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}
-ringed
\infty
-topos
\left(\mathcal{E},\mathcal{O}\right)
and an object
E
of
\mathcal{E},
the slice topos
\mathcal{E}/E
inherits a canonical structure of a
{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}
-ringed topos as
\left(\mathcal{E}/E,\mathcal{O}_{E}:=\mathcal{O}|_{E}\right),
where more formally
\mathcal{O}|_{E}:=e^{*}\mathcal{O},
where
e:\mathcal{E}/E\to\mathcal{E}
is the canonical (étale) geometric morphism.
We denote
\mathcal{O}|_{E}
by
\mathcal{O}_{E}
and call it the
structure sheaf
of
E.

Definition 8.4
.

An
{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}
-Deligne-Mumford stack
is a
{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}
-ringed
\infty
-topos
\left(\mathcal{X},\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}\right)
such that there exists a collection of objects
X_{\alpha}\in\mathcal{X}
for which the unique map

\underset{\alpha}{\coprod}X_{\alpha}\to 1

is an effective epimorphism, together with equivalences

\left(\mathcal{X}/X_{\alpha},\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}|_{X_{\alpha}}\right)\simeq\left(\operatorname{Shv}\left(\mathbf{Spec}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}\left(\mathcal{A}_{\alpha}\right)\right),\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{A}_{\alpha}}\right)

for
\mathcal{A}_{\alpha}\in\mathscr{D}.
If the
\mathcal{A}_{\alpha}
can be chosen to be of finite presentation (i.e.
\mathbf{Spec}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}\left(\mathcal{A}_{\alpha}\right)
is a derived manifold), then
\left(\mathcal{X},\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}\right)
is called a
derived orbifold
. Denote the full subcategory of
\mathfrak{Top}_{\infty}^{{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}}
spanned by the
{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}
-Deligne-Mumford stacks by
\mathfrak{DM}_{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}.

Remark 8.5
.

The canonical functor

\displaystyle\mathscr{D}

\displaystyle\longrightarrow

\displaystyle\mathfrak{Top}_{\infty}^{{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}}

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}

\displaystyle\mapsto

\displaystyle\left(\operatorname{Shv}\left(\mathbf{Spec}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}\left(\mathcal{A}\right)\right)\right)

is fully faithful. Moreover, the induced functor

\mathfrak{DM}_{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}\to\operatorname{Shv}\left(\mathscr{D}\right)=\mathcal{E}_{\mathfrak{Der}}

is fully faithful by
[
7
, Theorem 2.2]
. We hence can identify
\mathfrak{DM}_{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}
with its image in
\mathcal{E}_{\mathfrak{Der}}.

Definition 8.6
.

Let
\left(\mathcal{X},\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}\right)
be an
{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}
-ringed
\infty
-topos and let
X\in\mathcal{X}.
Then there is a canonical morphism

\left(\mathcal{X}/X,\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}|_{X}\right)\to\left(\mathcal{X},\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}\right).

Any morphism
\left(\mathcal{Y},\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{Y}}\right)\to\left(\mathcal{X},\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}\right)
equivalent to one of the above form is called
étale
.

Denote by
\mathfrak{DM}_{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}^{\acute{e}t}
the subcategory of
\mathfrak{DM}_{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}
of
{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}
-Deligne-Mumford stacks and étale morphisms.

Proposition 8.7
.

[
7
, Remark 1.7]

For any
{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}
-Deligne-Mumford stack
\left(\mathcal{X},\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}\right),
there is a canonical equivalence of
\infty
-categories

\displaystyle\mathcal{X}

\displaystyle\longrightarrow

\displaystyle\mathfrak{DM}_{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}^{\acute{e}t}/\left(\mathcal{X},\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}\right)

\displaystyle X

\displaystyle\mapsto

\displaystyle\left(\mathcal{X}/X,\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}|_{X}\right)\to\left(\mathcal{X},\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}\right)

Remark 8.8
.

It follows from
[
22
, Corollary 6.3.5.9]
that for
\mathcal{X}
any
{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}
-Deligne-Mumford stack,
\mathfrak{DM}_{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}^{\acute{e}t}/\left(\mathcal{X},\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}\right)
can be identified with a full subcategory of
\mathcal{E}_{\mathfrak{Der}}/\mathcal{X},
namely by those morphisms
\mathcal{Y}\to\mathcal{X}
which are representable by an étale map of
{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}
-Deligne-Mumford stacks. In particular, for any
\mathcal{A}\in\mathscr{D},
the
\infty
-topos
\operatorname{Shv}\left(\mathbf{Spec}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}\left(\mathcal{A}\right)\right)
can be identified with the full subcategory of
\mathcal{E}_{\mathfrak{Der}}/\mathbf{Spec}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}\left(\mathcal{A}\right)
spanned by étale maps from an
{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}
-Deligne-Mumford stack.

Consider the canonical inclusion

\mathscr{D}^{op}\hookrightarrow\mathbf{d}{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}\mathbf{Alg}.

Since the open covering topology is sub-canonical on
\mathscr{D}
(as we are assuming all algebras in
\mathscr{D}
are complete), this inclusion is a sheaf
\mathcal{O}
on
\mathscr{D}
with values in
\mathbf{d}{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}\mathbf{Alg}.
Thus
\left(\mathcal{E}_{\mathfrak{Der}},\mathcal{O}\right)
is a
{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}
-ringed
\infty
-topos.

Remark 8.9
.

The above sheaf
\mathcal{O}
is represented by
\mathbb{R}
(and
\mathbb{R}^{0|1}
as a sheaf of
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-graded sets in the super setting).

Remark 8.10
.

Let
\widetilde{\mathcal{X}}\in\mathcal{E}_{\mathfrak{Der}}
be an
{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}
-Deligne-Mumford stack (which is the functor of points of say
\left(\mathcal{X},\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}\right).
) Then, identifying
\mathcal{X}
with the full subcategory
\mathfrak{DM}_{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}^{\acute{e}t}/\mathcal{X}
of
\mathcal{E}_{\mathfrak{Der}}/\mathcal{X},
we have that

\left(\mathcal{X},\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}\right)\simeq\left(\mathfrak{DM}_{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}^{\acute{e}t}/\mathcal{X},\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E}_{\mathfrak{Der}}}|_{\mathfrak{DM}_{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}^{\acute{e}t}/\mathcal{X}}\right).

Denote by
\mathscr{D}^{\acute{e}t}
the subcategory of
\mathscr{D}
spanned by the étale maps. The open covered topology on
\mathscr{D}
restricts to
\mathscr{D}^{\acute{e}t}.
Denote by

j:\mathscr{D}^{\acute{e}t}\to\mathscr{D}

the canonical inclusion. Then the restriction functor

j^{*}:\mathcal{E}_{\mathfrak{Der}}=\operatorname{Shv}\left(\mathscr{D}\right)\to\operatorname{Shv}\left(\mathscr{D}^{\acute{e}t}\right)

admits a left adjoint
j_{!}

[
7
, Definition 3.4]
called the
étale prolongation functor
.

Theorem 8.11
.

[
7
, Definition 3.9]

The essential image of
j_{!}
is precisely (the functor of points of) the
{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}
-Deligne-Mumford stacks.

Definition 8.12
.

Denote by
\mathbb{U}=j_{!}\left(1\right).
This is the
universal
{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}
-Deligne-Mumford stack
.

Theorem 8.13
.

[
7
, Theorems 3.6,3.7]

The
\infty
-category
\mathfrak{DM}_{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}^{\acute{e}t}
itself is (equivalent to) an
\infty
-topos and it is in fact the underlying
\infty
-topos of the universal
{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}
-Deligne-Mumford stack. Moreover, this topos is canonically equivalent to
\operatorname{Shv}\left(\mathscr{D}^{\acute{e}t}\right).

Remark 8.14
.

[
7
, Proposition 3.2]

The equivalence

\mathfrak{DM}_{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}^{\acute{e}t}\simeq\operatorname{Shv}\left(\mathscr{D}^{\acute{e}t}\right)

sends an
{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}
-Deligne-Mumford stack
\mathcal{X}
to the sheaf
y^{\acute{e}t}\left(\mathcal{X}\right)
on
\mathscr{D}^{\acute{e}t}
which sends an object
D
to the space of étale morphisms from
D
to
\mathcal{X}.
Moreover,
j_{!}y^{\acute{e}t}\left(\mathcal{X}\right)\simeq\mathcal{X}.

Proposition 8.15
.

For any
{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}
-Deligne-Mumford stack
\left(\mathcal{X},\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}\right),
there is a canonical morphism of
{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}
-ringed
\infty
-topoi

\left(\mathcal{E}_{\mathfrak{Der}}/\mathcal{X},\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E}_{\mathfrak{Der}}}|_{\mathcal{X}}\right)\to\left(\mathcal{X},\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}\right)

Proof.

By
[
7
, Proposition 2.4]
, there is a canonical geometric morphism

\Lambda:\mathcal{E}_{\mathfrak{Der}}/\mathbb{U}\to\operatorname{Shv}\left(\mathscr{D}^{\acute{e}t}\right)

whose inverse image functor is

\displaystyle\Lambda^{*}:\operatorname{Shv}\left(\mathscr{D}^{\acute{e}t}\right)

\displaystyle\longrightarrow

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{\mathfrak{Der}}/\mathbb{U}

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}

\displaystyle\mapsto

\displaystyle j_{!}\left(\mathcal{F}\to 1\right).

So, by
[
22
, Proposition 6.3.5.8]
, for any
\mathcal{F}\in\operatorname{Shv}\left(\mathscr{D}^{\acute{e}t}\right),
there is a pullback diagram of
\infty
-topoi

\textstyle{\left(\mathcal{E}_{\mathfrak{Der}}/\mathbb{U}\right)/\Lambda^{*}\left(\mathcal{F}\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Shv}\left(\mathscr{D}^{\acute{e}t}\right)/\mathcal{F}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{E}_{\mathfrak{Der}}/\mathbb{U}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Lambda}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Shv}\left(\mathscr{D}^{\acute{e}t}\right).}

Now,
\Lambda^{*}\left(\mathcal{F}\right)=j_{!}\mathcal{F}\to j_{!}1=\mathbb{U},
so
\left(\mathcal{E}_{\mathfrak{Der}}/\mathbb{U}\right)/\Lambda^{*}\left(\mathcal{F}\right)\simeq\mathcal{E}_{\mathfrak{Der}}/j_{!}\left(\mathcal{F}\right).
So, if
\mathcal{F}=y^{\acute{e}t}\left(\mathcal{X}\right),
then

\left(\mathcal{E}_{\mathfrak{Der}}/\mathbb{U}\right)/\Lambda^{*}\left(\mathcal{F}\right)\simeq\mathcal{E}_{\mathfrak{Der}}/\mathcal{X}.

Notice moreover that

\operatorname{Shv}\left(\mathscr{D}^{\acute{e}t}\right)/y^{\acute{e}t}\left(\mathcal{X}\right)\simeq\mathfrak{DM}_{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}^{\acute{e}t}/\left(\mathcal{X},\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}\right)\simeq\mathcal{X}.

The result now follows.
∎

Remark 8.16
.

Denote by
\Lambda_{\mathcal{X}}:\mathcal{E}_{\mathfrak{Der}}/\mathcal{X}\to\mathcal{X}
the geometric morphism from the previous proposition. Then,
\Lambda^{\mathcal{X}}_{*}
is the restriction functor from the large topos
\mathcal{E}_{\mathfrak{Der}}/\mathcal{X}
to the small topos
\mathcal{X}.
In particular,
\Lambda^{\mathcal{X}}_{*}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E}_{\mathfrak{Der}}}|_{\mathcal{X}}=\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}.
Hence the above geometric morphism has a canonical upgrade to a morphism of
{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}
-ringed
\infty
-topoi.

Lemma 8.17
.

There exists a unique colimit preserving functor

\mathcal{E}_{\mathfrak{Der}}\to\mathfrak{Top}_{\infty}^{{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}}

which sends each
{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}
-Deligne-Mumford stack to itself.

Proof.

Firstly, by
[
7
, Lemma 3.1]
, there is a canonical equivalence

\mathcal{E}_{\mathfrak{Der}}\simeq\operatorname{Shv}\left(\mathfrak{DM}_{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}\right),

where a functor
\mathcal{F}:\mathfrak{DM}_{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}^{op}\to\mathcal{S}\mskip-3.0mu\operatorname{pc}
is a sheaf if and only if for each
{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}
-Deligne-Mumford stack
\left(\mathcal{X},\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}\right),
the composite

\mathcal{X}^{op}\simeq\left(\mathfrak{DM}_{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}^{\acute{e}t}/\left(\mathcal{X},\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}\right)\right)^{op}\longrightarrow\left(\mathfrak{DM}_{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}\right)^{op}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathcal{F}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathcal{S}\mskip-3.0mu\operatorname{pc}

preserves small limits. A colimit preserving functor

\mathcal{E}_{\mathfrak{Der}}\to\mathfrak{Top}_{\infty}^{{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}}

is the same as a limit preserving functor

\mathcal{E}_{\mathfrak{Der}}^{op}\to\left(\mathfrak{Top}_{\infty}^{{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}}\right)^{op},

i.e. a sheaf with values in
\left(\mathfrak{Top}_{\infty}^{{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}}\right)^{op}
, which can then be identified with a functor

\mathfrak{DM}_{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}\to\mathfrak{Top}_{\infty}^{{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}}

such that each composite

\mathcal{X}\simeq\mathfrak{DM}_{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}^{\acute{e}t}/\left(\mathcal{X},\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}\right)\longrightarrow\mathfrak{Top}_{\infty}^{{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}}

preserves colimits. Now, observe that for each
{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}
-Deligne-Mumford stack
\left(\mathcal{X},\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}\right),

the composite

\mathcal{X}\simeq\left(\mathfrak{Top}_{\infty}^{{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}}\right)^{\acute{e}t}/\left(\mathcal{X},\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}\right)\to\mathfrak{Top}_{\infty}^{{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}}

preserves colimits, by
[
7
, Proposition 3.1]
.
∎

Proposition 8.18
.

There is a unique colimit-preserving functor

\mathcal{E}_{\mathfrak{Der}}\to\operatorname{Fun}\left(\Delta\left[1\right],\mathfrak{Top}_{\infty}^{{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}}\right)

which sends each
{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}
-Deligne-Mumford stack to the map of
{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}
-ringed
\infty
-topoi

\Lambda_{\mathcal{X}}:\left(\mathcal{E}_{\mathfrak{Der}}/\mathcal{X},\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E}_{\mathfrak{Der}}}|_{\mathcal{X}}\right)\to\left(\mathcal{X},\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}\right)

of Remark
8.16
.

Proof.

Firstly, let us show that there is a functor

\displaystyle\mathfrak{DM}_{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\longrightarrow

\displaystyle\operatorname{Fun}\left(\Delta\left[1\right],\mathfrak{Top}_{\infty}^{{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}}\right)

\displaystyle\left(\mathcal{X},O_{\mathcal{X}}\right)

\displaystyle\mapsto

\displaystyle\Lambda_{\mathcal{X}}:\left(\mathcal{E}_{\mathfrak{Der}}/\mathcal{X},\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E}_{\mathfrak{Der}}}|_{\mathcal{X}}\right)\to\left(\mathcal{X},\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}\right).

Consider the functor

\mathfrak{DM}_{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}\to\mathfrak{Top}_{\infty}^{{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}}

from Lemma
8.17
.

By
[
22
, Theorem 3.6]
, for each such
{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}
-Deligne-Mumford stack, there is a unique étale map
\left(\mathcal{X},\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}\right)\to\left(\mathbb{U},\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{U}}\right).
It follows that there is a canonical extension of the above functor to a functor

\mathfrak{DM}_{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}\to\mathfrak{Top}_{\infty}^{{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}}/\left(\mathbb{U},\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{U}}\right).

Furthermore, base change along the map

\Lambda:\left(\mathcal{E}_{\mathfrak{Der}}/\mathbb{U},\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E}_{\mathfrak{Der}}}|_{\mathbb{U}}\right)\to\left(\mathbb{U},\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{U}}\right)

induces a functor

\displaystyle\mathfrak{Top}_{\infty}^{{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}}/\left(\mathbb{U},\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{U}}\right)

\displaystyle\longrightarrow

\displaystyle\operatorname{Fun}\left(\Delta\left[1\right],\mathfrak{Top}_{\infty}^{{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}}\right)

\displaystyle\left(\mathcal{B},\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{B}}\right)\to\left(\mathbb{U},\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{U}}\right)

\displaystyle\mapsto

\displaystyle\left(\mathcal{B},\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{B}}\right)\times_{\left(\mathbb{U},\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{U}}\right)}\left(\mathcal{E}_{\mathfrak{Der}}/\mathbb{U},\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E}_{\mathfrak{Der}}}|_{\mathbb{U}}\right)\to\left(\mathcal{B},\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{B}}\right).

By composition, we get a functor

F:\mathfrak{DM}_{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}\to\operatorname{Fun}\left(\Delta\left[1\right],\mathfrak{Top}_{\infty}^{{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}}\right).

Unwinding the definitions, it can be identified with the desired one.

To finish the proof, we need to verify that this functor is a cosheaf, or, in other words, the restriction to each
\mathcal{X}
preserves colimits. This will be the case if and only if both
\mathbf{ev}_{0}\circ F
and
\mathbf{ev}_{1}\circ F
preserve colimits.
\mathbf{ev}_{1}\circ F
can be identified with the canonical functor

\mathcal{X}\simeq\left(\mathfrak{Top}_{\infty}^{{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}}\right)^{\acute{e}t}/\left(\mathcal{X},\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}\right)\to\mathfrak{Top}_{\infty}^{{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}}

which we already saw preserves colimits. The second can be identified with the composite

\mathcal{X}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\Lambda_{\mathcal{X}}^{*}}}{{-\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu-\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu-\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu-\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu-\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu-\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu\longrightarrow}}\mathcal{E}_{\mathfrak{Der}}/\mathcal{X}\simeq\left(\mathfrak{Top}_{\infty}^{{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}}\right)^{\acute{e}t}/\left(\mathcal{E}_{\mathfrak{Der}}/\mathcal{X}\right)\to\mathfrak{Top}_{\infty}^{{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}}

which also preserves colimits by
[
7
, Proposition 3.1]
.
∎

9.
Modules and Quasi-coherent sheaves

9.1.
Modules and square-zero extensions

Recall that for a commutative ring
R,
the canonical functor

\displaystyle\mathrm{Ab}\left(\mathbf{Com}\mathbf{Alg}/R\right)

\displaystyle\to

\displaystyle\mathbf{Mod}_{R}

\displaystyle\left(\pi_{B}:B\to R\right)

\displaystyle\mapsto

\displaystyle\ker\left(\pi\right)

from the category of abelian group objects in the slice category to the category of
R
-modules is an equivalence of categories.
Consider the forgetful/free monadic adjunction

\textstyle{\mathrm{Ab}\left(\mathbf{Com}\mathbf{Alg}/R\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{U}
\textstyle{\mathbf{Com}/R\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{F}

Under the equivalence
\mathbf{Mod}_{R}\simeq\mathrm{Ab}\left(\mathbf{Com}\mathbf{Alg}/R\right)\simeq\mathbf{Mod}_{R},

for a module
M,

U\left(M\right)
is the square-zero extension

R\oplus\epsilon M\to R.

Also,
F\left(id_{R}\right)
is isomorphic to the module of Kähler differentials
\Omega^{1}\left(R\right),
since for any module
M,

\displaystyle\mathbb{H}\mathrm{om}\left(F\left(id_{R}\right),M\right)

\displaystyle\cong

\displaystyle\mathbb{H}\mathrm{om}\left(id_{R},R\oplus\epsilon M\to R\right)

\displaystyle\cong

\displaystyle\mathfrak{Der}\left(A,M\right),

which is precisely the universal property of the module
\Omega^{1}\left(R\right).

We now generalize this story for algebras in
\mathbf{d}{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}\mathbf{Alg}.

Definition 9.1
.

Let
\mathcal{A}\in\mathbf{d}{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}\mathbf{Alg}.
An
\mathcal{A}
-module
is by definition a module for the underlying supercommutative dg-algebra
\mathcal{A}_{\sharp}
in unbounded cochain complexes of
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-graded real vector spaces. Denote this
\infty
-category by
\mathbf{Mod}_{\mathcal{A}}.
Denote the subcategory on algebras whose underlying cochain complex is concentrated in non-positive degrees by
\mathbf{Mod}^{\leq 0}_{\mathcal{A}}.

Recall the following theorem:

Lemma 9.2
.

[
31
, Theorem 1.1]

For
\mathcal{A}
any
dg
-algebra, corresponding to a simplicial ring
\tilde{\mathcal{A}},
there is an equivalence

\mathbf{Mod}_{\mathcal{A}}^{\leq 0}\simeq\mathbf{Mod}_{\tilde{\mathcal{A}}}.

Theorem 9.3
.

For any
\mathcal{A}\in\mathbf{d}{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}\mathbf{Alg},
there is a canonical equivalence of
\infty
-categories

\mathbf{Stab}\left(\mathbf{d}{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}\mathbf{Alg}/\mathcal{A}\right)\simeq\mathbf{Mod}_{\mathcal{A}}.

Proof.

Let
\mathcal{B}
be an
{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}
-algebra with underlying supercommutative
\mathbb{R}
-algebra
\mathcal{B}_{\sharp}.
Since
{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}
-completion preserves the terminal algebra
\mathbb{R},
the forgetful functor

\left(\mspace{3mu}\cdot\mspace{3mu}\right)_{\sharp}:\left({\mathbf{d}{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}\mathbf{Alg}}_{\mathcal{B}}\right)_{\ast}\to\left({\mathsf{SCAlg}}_{\mathcal{B}}\right)_{\ast}

from augmented
\mathcal{B}
-algebras in
{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}
-algebras, to augmented
\mathcal{B}
-algebras in
\mathsf{SCAlg}_{\mathbb{R}}
has a left adjoint, sending

\textstyle{\mathcal{B}_{\sharp}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{a}
\textstyle{\mathcal{A}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi}

to

\textstyle{\mathcal{B}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\eta_{\mathcal{A}}\circ a}
\textstyle{\widehat{\mathcal{A}},\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\widehat{\pi}}

where
\eta
is the unit of the adjunction between the
\infty
-categories of un-augmented objects. As both of these
\infty
-categories are presentable, there is an induced adjunction between their stabilizations

\textstyle{\left({\mathsf{SCAlg}}_{\mathcal{B}}\right)_{\ast}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Sigma^{\infty}_{+,{alg}}}
\textstyle{\mathbf{Stab}\left(\left({\mathsf{SCAlg}}_{\mathcal{B}}\right)_{\ast}\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Omega^{\infty}_{alg}}
\scriptstyle{\widehat{\left(\mspace{3mu}\cdot\mspace{3mu}\right)}^{\infty}}
\textstyle{\mathbf{Stab}\left(\left({\mathbf{d}{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}\mathbf{Alg}}_{\mathcal{B}}\right)_{\ast}\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\left(\mspace{3mu}\cdot\mspace{3mu}\right)_{\sharp}}^{\infty}}
\scriptstyle{{\Omega}^{\infty}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}}
\textstyle{\left({\mathbf{d}{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}\mathbf{Alg}}_{\mathcal{B}}\right)_{\ast}.\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Sigma^{\infty}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}}

Consider the adjunction

\textstyle{\mathbf{Mod}_{\mathcal{B}}^{\leq 0}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathsf{Sym}}
\textstyle{\left({\mathsf{SCAlg}}_{\mathcal{B}}\right)_{\ast}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{I}

where

\textstyle{I:\mathcal{A}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi}
\textstyle{\mathcal{B}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{a}
\textstyle{0\times_{\mathcal{B}}\mathcal{A}}

is the right derived functor of the augmentation ideal functor.
By
[
30
, Theorem 3.2.3]
, the induced adjunction
\mathsf{Sym}^{\infty}\dashv I^{\infty}
between their stabilizations is an equivalence. Since
\mathbf{Stab}\left(\mathbf{Mod}^{\leq 0}_{\mathcal{B}}\right)\simeq\mathbf{Mod}_{\mathcal{B}},
this implies

\mathbf{Stab}\left(\left({\mathsf{SCAlg}}_{\mathcal{B}}\right)_{\ast}\right)\simeq\mathbf{Mod}_{\mathcal{B}}.

It therefore suffices to prove that
\widehat{\left(\mspace{3mu}\cdot\mspace{3mu}\right)}^{\infty}\dashv\left(\mspace{3mu}\cdot\mspace{3mu}\right)_{\sharp}
is an adjoint equivalence.

Notice that
[
30
, Corollary 3.1.4, Theorem 3.2.3]
imply that the essential image of

\mathsf{Sym}^{\infty}\Sigma^{\infty}_{+}:\mathbf{Mod}^{\leq 0}_{\mathcal{B}}\to\mathbf{Stab}\left(\left({\mathsf{SCAlg}}_{\mathcal{B}}\right)_{\ast}\right)

is equivalent to the connective
\left({\mathsf{SCAlg}}_{\mathcal{B}}\right)_{\ast}
-spectra objects. Let
M
be a connective
\mathcal{B}
-module in
\mathbf{Mod}^{\leq 0}_{\mathcal{B}}.
To ease notation, let
F:=\widehat{\left(\mspace{3mu}\cdot\mspace{3mu}\right)}^{\infty}
and
G:=\left(\mspace{3mu}\cdot\mspace{3mu}\right)_{\sharp}^{\infty}.

We claim that the unit

\Sigma\mathsf{Sym}^{\infty}\Sigma^{\infty}_{+}M\to GF\Sigma\mathsf{Sym}^{\infty}\Sigma^{\infty}_{+}M,

is an equivalence. Since
F
and
\mathsf{Sym}^{\infty}
preserve colimits, we have

\displaystyle\Sigma\mathsf{Sym}^{\infty}\Sigma^{\infty}_{+}M

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle\mathsf{Sym}^{\infty}\Sigma^{\infty}_{+}M\left[1\right].

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle\Sigma^{\infty}_{+,alg}\mathsf{Sym}M\left[1\right].

It follows that

F\Sigma\mathsf{Sym}^{\infty}\Sigma^{\infty}_{+}M\simeq\Sigma^{\infty}_{+,\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}\mathsf{Sym}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}M\left[1\right].

For a general
\mathcal{B}
-module
N,

\mathsf{Sym}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}\left(N\right)=\mathsf{Sym}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}\left(N_{0}\right)\underset{\mathsf{Sym}\left(N_{0}\right)}{\otimes}\mathsf{Sym}\left(N\right).

Since
M\left[1\right]_{0}=\mathbb{R},
we therefore have that
\left(\mathsf{Sym}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}M\left[1\right]\right)_{\sharp}=\mathsf{Sym}M\left[1\right].
It follows that the unit is an equivalence, as claimed. So the component of the unit is an equivalence for all
\left({\mathsf{SCAlg}}_{\mathcal{B}}\right)_{\ast}
-spectra objects of the form
\Sigma N,
for
N
connective. By
[
20
, Corollary 6.2.2.16]
, since
\left(\mspace{3mu}\cdot\mspace{3mu}\right)_{\sharp}^{\infty}
preserves sequential colimits, we can identify
G
with the pointwise application of
\left(\mspace{3mu}\cdot\mspace{3mu}\right)_{\sharp}^{\infty}
to spectra objects, and can conclude that
G
preserves sequential colimits as well. Since every spectrum object is a sequential colimit of such objects of the form
\Sigma N
with
N
connective, and
F
and
G
preserve sequential colimits, it follows that the unit is an equivalence everywhere.

We now wish to show that that counit
\beta
is also an equivalence. Notice that
G
is conservative since
\left(\mspace{3mu}\cdot\mspace{3mu}\right)_{\sharp}
is. Therefore, is suffices to show that for any
\left(\mathbf{d}{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}\mathbf{Alg}_{\mathcal{B}}\right)_{\ast}
-spectrum object
X,

G\left(\beta_{X}\right)
is an equivalence. By the triangle identity for adjunctions, we have that the following diagram commutes up to homotopy

\textstyle{GX}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces GFGX}
\scriptstyle{G\beta_{X}}
\textstyle{GX\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{id}

where the vertical arrow is the unit of
GX,
hence an equivalence. Therefore,
G\left(\beta_{X}\right)
is an equivalence.
∎

Since
\mathbf{d}{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}\mathbf{Alg}/\mathcal{A}
has finite limits, there is an adjunction

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 39.03465pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-39.03465pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\left(\mathbf{d}{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}\mathbf{Alg}/\mathcal{A}\right)_{*}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 41.52765pt\raise-7.73805pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.5853pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\Sigma^{\infty}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 63.03465pt\raise-2.15277pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 63.03465pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathbf{Stab}\left(\mathbf{d}{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}\mathbf{Alg}/\mathcal{A}\right)\simeq\mathbf{Mod}_{\mathcal{A}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 41.52766pt\raise 7.73807pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.5853pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\Omega^{\infty}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 39.03467pt\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces,

with
\Sigma^{\infty}\dashv\Omega^{\infty}.

Concretely, given an
\mathcal{A}
-module
M,
under the identification above,
\Omega^{\infty}\left(M\right)
is the square zero extension of
\mathcal{A}
by
\tau_{\leq 0}M.
Consequently, we denote the composite

\mathbf{Mod}^{\leq 0}_{\mathcal{A}}\hookrightarrow\mathbf{Mod}_{\mathcal{A}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathbf{Stab}\left(\mathbf{d}{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}\mathbf{Alg}/\mathcal{A}\right)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\Omega^{\infty}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\left(\mathbf{d}{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}\mathbf{Alg}/\mathcal{A}\right)_{*}\to\mathbf{d}{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}\mathbf{Alg}/\mathcal{A}

by
\mathcal{A}\left[\mspace{3mu}\cdot\mspace{3mu}\right].

For a given
\mathcal{A}
-module
M,

\mathcal{A}\left[M\right]\simeq\mathcal{A}\oplus M,
as an
\mathcal{A}
-modules, and the projection map is the map making
\mathcal{A}\left[M\right]
an object in
\mathbf{d}{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}\mathbf{Alg}/A.
Moreover, the canonical section

a\mapsto\left(a,0\right)

is the map making it a pointed object. We use the following notation

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 14.92361pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-14.92361pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathcal{A}\left[M\right]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 17.8528pt\raise 5.00972pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.00417pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\pi_{M}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 38.92361pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 38.92361pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathcal{A}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{}{}{{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}}{}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{}{}{}{{}{}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 17.84377pt\raise-25.05548pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.25555pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{0}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{}{}{}{{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}}{\hbox{\kern 5.20749pt\raise-8.00037pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}\lx@xy@spline@}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces.

Notice that the canonical functor

\left(\mathbf{d}{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}\mathbf{Alg}/\mathcal{A}\right)_{*}\to\mathbf{d}{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}\mathbf{Alg}/\mathcal{A}

has a left adjoint

\displaystyle\left(\mspace{3mu}\cdot\mspace{3mu}\right)_{+}:\mathbf{d}{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}\mathbf{Alg}/\mathcal{A}

\displaystyle\to

\displaystyle\left(\mathbf{d}{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}\mathbf{Alg}/\mathcal{A}\right)_{*}

\displaystyle\left(\mathsf{B}\to\mathcal{A}\right)

\displaystyle\to

\displaystyle\left(\mathcal{A}\to\mathsf{B}\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\mkern-13.73mu\resizebox{1305270}{540744}{$\phantom{x}{}_{\phantom{.}{}^{\infty}}$}$\crcr$\displaystyle\bigcirc$\crcr}}}}{\mathbin{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\mkern-13.73mu\resizebox{1305270}{540744}{$\phantom{x}{}_{\phantom{.}{}^{\infty}}$}$\crcr$\textstyle\bigcirc$\crcr}}}}{\mathbin{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\mkern-13.73mu\resizebox{1305270}{540744}{$\phantom{x}{}_{\phantom{.}{}^{\infty}}$}$\crcr$\scriptstyle\bigcirc$\crcr}}}}{\mathbin{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-13.73mu\resizebox{1305270}{540744}{$\phantom{x}{}_{\phantom{.}{}^{\infty}}$}$\crcr$\scriptscriptstyle\bigcirc$\crcr}}}}\mathcal{A}\to\mathcal{A}\right).

Denote the composite

\mathbf{d}{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}\mathbf{Alg}/\mathcal{A}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\left(\mspace{3mu}\cdot\mspace{3mu}\right)_{+}}}{{-\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu-\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu-\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu-\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu-\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu-\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu\longrightarrow}}\left(\mathbf{d}{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}\mathbf{Alg}/\mathcal{A}\right)_{*}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\Sigma^{\infty}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathbf{Stab}\left(\left(\mathbf{d}{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}\mathbf{Alg}/\mathcal{A}\right)_{*}\right)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathbf{Mod}_{\mathcal{A}}

by
\Omega^{1}_{\left(\mspace{3mu}\cdot\mspace{3mu}\right)}\left(\mathcal{A}\right).
We will return to this functor in Section
9.2
. For now, we just observe that by the composition of adjoints, we have

(7)

\Omega^{1}_{\left(\mspace{3mu}\cdot\mspace{3mu}\right)}\left(\mathcal{A}\right)\dashv\mathcal{A}\left[\mspace{3mu}\cdot\mspace{3mu}\right].

Remark 9.4
.

Since the functor
\mathcal{A}\left[\mspace{3mu}\cdot\mspace{3mu}\right]
is a right adjoint, it preserves limits. In particular, we have that for
M_{1},M_{2},\ldots,M_{n}

\mathcal{A}
-modules

\mathcal{A}\left[\bigoplus\limits^{n}_{i=1}M_{i}\right]\simeq\mathcal{A}\left[M_{1}\right]\times_{\mathcal{A}}\mathcal{A}\left[M_{2}\right]\ldots\times_{\mathcal{A}}\mathcal{A}\left[M_{n}\right].

9.2.
Derivations and
1
-forms

Definition 9.5
.

Given an
\mathcal{A}
-module
M,
the
space of
\mathbf{C}^{\infty}
-derivations of
\mathcal{A}
with values in
M
is the mapping space

\mathfrak{Der}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu\left(\mathcal{A},M\right):=\mathbb{M}\mathrm{ap}_{\mathbf{d}{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}\mathbf{Alg}/A}\left(id_{\mathcal{A}},\mathcal{A}\left[M\right]\right).

More generally, given a map
f:\mathsf{B}\to\mathcal{A},
the
space of
\mathbf{C}^{\infty}
-derivations of
\mathcal{A}
along
f
with values in
M
is the space

\mathfrak{Der}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu\left(\mathcal{A},M\right)_{f}:=\mathbb{M}\mathrm{ap}_{\mathbf{d}{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}\mathbf{Alg}/A}\left(f,\mathcal{A}\left[M\right]\right).

Remark 9.6
.

Given a derivation
\varphi
of
\mathcal{A}
with values in
M,

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 14.92361pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-14.92361pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathcal{A}\left[M\right]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 17.8528pt\raise 5.00972pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.00417pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\pi_{M}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 38.92361pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 38.92361pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathcal{A}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{}{}{{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}}{}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{}{}{}{{}{}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 17.20695pt\raise-24.98741pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-0.8264pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\varphi}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{}{}{}{{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}}{\hbox{\kern 5.20749pt\raise-8.00037pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}\lx@xy@spline@}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces,

as a map of
\mathcal{A}
-modules,

\displaystyle\varphi:\mathcal{A}

\displaystyle\to

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}\left[M\right]\simeq\mathcal{A}\oplus M

\displaystyle a

\displaystyle\mapsto

\displaystyle\left(a,\mathcal{D}_{\varphi}\left(a\right)\right),

for a unique map of
\mathcal{A}
-modules
\mathcal{D}_{\varphi}:\mathcal{A}\to M.
The map
\mathcal{D}_{\varphi}
is a derivation in the classical sense, but the converse need not be true; a derivation of
\mathcal{A}
as a
\mathbf{C}^{\infty}
-algebra needs to satisfy more conditions than simply being a derivation of the underlying
\mathbb{R}
-algebra
\mathcal{A}_{\sharp}.
See
[
9
, Proposition 2.11]
. However for nice enough
{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}
-rings (such as the algebra of functions on a supermanifold) any derivation on the sense of
\mathbb{R}
-algebras is a
\mathbf{C}^{\infty}
-derivation
[
9
, Corollary 2.18]
.

Definition 9.7
.

For
n\in\mathbb{Z},
let
\mathbb{R}\left[n\right]
denote the graded vector space with
\mathbb{R}
in degree
-n,
and otherwise zero, regarded as a cochain complex with zero differential. Let
\mathbb{E}\in\mathbf{Mod}_{\mathcal{A}},
and let

\mathbb{E}\left[n\right]:=\mathbb{E}\underset{\mathbb{R}}{\otimes}\mathbb{R}\left[n\right].

Remark 9.8
.

In concrete terms, we have
\left(\mathbb{E}\left[n\right]\right)^{k}=\mathbb{E}^{n+k}
with the same differential as before, i.e. you shift the entire complex to the left by
n.

Remark 9.9
.

Since
\mathbf{Mod}_{\mathcal{A}}
is a stable
\infty
-category, it comes equipped with a suspension functor

\Sigma:\mathbf{Mod}_{\mathcal{A}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathbf{Mod}_{\mathcal{A}}.

Strictly speaking, there is a difference between
\Sigma\mathbb{E}
and
\mathbb{E}\left[1\right].
The functor
\Sigma
is given by
\mathbb{R}\left[1\right]\underset{\mathbb{R}}{\otimes}\left(\mspace{3mu}\cdot\mspace{3mu}\right).
For a given
\mathcal{A}
-module
\mathbb{E}^{\bullet}
, and any integer
k,
we have

\left(\Sigma\mathbb{E}^{\bullet}\right)^{k}=\mathbb{E}^{k+1}=\left(\mathbb{E}^{\bullet}\left[1\right]\right)^{k},

but the differentials are different. The differential on
\mathbb{E}^{\bullet}\left[1\right]^{k}=\mathbb{E}^{k+1}
is simply
d_{k+1},
whereas the differential for
\Sigma\mathbb{E}^{\bullet}
is
-d_{k+1}.
Clearly, these two
\mathcal{A}
-modules are canonically equivalent. Also, of course, if
\mathbb{E}
has zero differential (for example is
\mathbb{R}\left[m\right]
, for some
m
), then we do have
\Sigma^{n}\mathbb{E}=\mathbb{E}\left[n\right].

The functor
\Omega:\mathbf{Mod}_{\mathcal{A}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathbf{Mod}_{\mathcal{A}},
is defined to be

\Sigma^{-1}=\mathbb{R}\left[-1\right]\underset{\mathbb{R}}{\otimes}\left(\mspace{3mu}\cdot\mspace{3mu}\right)

and is inverse to
\Sigma.
For any cochain complex, the following is a (homotopy) pullback diagram

\textstyle{\Omega\left(\mathbb{E}\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathbb{E},}

hence the notation.

Definition 9.10
.

For all
n\in\mathbb{Z},
let

\mathcal{A}\left[\xi_{n}\right]:=\mathcal{A}\left[\mathcal{A}\left[-n\right]\right],

i.e. the square zero extension associated to the
\mathcal{A}
-module
\mathcal{A}\left[-n\right].
Concretely,

\mathcal{A}\left[\xi_{n}\right]=\mathcal{A}\left[\mathbf{x}\right]/\mathbf{x}^{2},\mspace{10mu}|\mathbf{x}|=n.

In the supercommutative case,
\mathcal{A}
-modules are
\mathbb{Z}\times\mathbb{Z}_{2},
graded, so we mean that
\mathbf{x}
is in degree
\left(n,0\right).
We also define
\Pi\mathcal{A}\left[\xi_{n}\right]:=\mathcal{A}\left[\Pi\mathcal{A}\left[-n\right]\right],
where
\Pi
is the parity-reversal operator. Concretely,

\Pi\mathcal{A}\left[\xi_{n}\right]=\mathcal{A}\left[\mathbf{x}\right],\mspace{10mu}|\mathbf{x}|=\left(n,1\right).

(We do not need to impose that
\mathbf{x}^{2}=0
; this is automatic since
\mathbf{x}
has odd
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-parity)

Remark 9.11
.

Since
\mathcal{A}\left[\mspace{3mu}\cdot\mspace{3mu}\right]
is a right adjoint, it follows that for any
n\in Z,
we have a pullback diagram

\textstyle{\mathcal{A}\left[\xi_{n+1}\right]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{A}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{0}
\textstyle{\mathcal{A}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{0}
\textstyle{\mathcal{A}\left[\xi_{n}\right],}

and similarly for the odd versions.

Definition 9.12
.

Let
n\in\mathbb{N}.
Define the
n
-shifted first order
1
-disk
be the
\mathbf{C}^{\infty}
-scheme

\mathbb{D}_{\left(1\right)}\left[n\right]:=\mathbf{Spec}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}\left(\mathbb{R}\left[\xi_{n}\right]\right).

In the setting of supergeometry, we also denote the above by

\mathbb{D}_{\left(1\right)}\left[n\right]^{\left(1,0\right)}:=\mathbf{Spec}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}\left(\mathbb{R}\left[\xi_{n}\right]\right)

for clarity, and call it the
n
-shifted first order
even

1
-disk
, and we denote

\mathbb{D}_{\left(1\right)}\left[n\right]^{\left(0,1\right)}:=\mathbf{Spec}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}\left(\Pi\mathbb{R}\left[\xi_{n}\right]\right)

and call it the
n
-shifted first order
odd

1
-disk

Remark 9.13
.

The first order odd
1
disk is the same as the odd line

\mathbb{D}_{\left(1\right)}^{\left(1,0\right)}=\mathbb{R}^{\left(0|1\right)}.

Notice that the canonical functor

\left(\mathbf{d}{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}\mathbf{Alg}/\mathcal{A}\right)_{*}\to\mathbf{d}{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}\mathbf{Alg}/\mathcal{A}

has a left adjoint

\displaystyle\left(\mspace{3mu}\cdot\mspace{3mu}\right)_{+}:\mathbf{d}{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}\mathbf{Alg}/\mathcal{A}

\displaystyle\to

\displaystyle\left(\mathbf{d}{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}\mathbf{Alg}/\mathcal{A}\right)_{*}

\displaystyle\left(\mathsf{B}\to\mathcal{A}\right)

\displaystyle\to

\displaystyle\left(\mathcal{A}\to\mathsf{B}\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\mkern-13.73mu\resizebox{1305270}{540744}{$\phantom{x}{}_{\phantom{.}{}^{\infty}}$}$\crcr$\displaystyle\bigcirc$\crcr}}}}{\mathbin{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\mkern-13.73mu\resizebox{1305270}{540744}{$\phantom{x}{}_{\phantom{.}{}^{\infty}}$}$\crcr$\textstyle\bigcirc$\crcr}}}}{\mathbin{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\mkern-13.73mu\resizebox{1305270}{540744}{$\phantom{x}{}_{\phantom{.}{}^{\infty}}$}$\crcr$\scriptstyle\bigcirc$\crcr}}}}{\mathbin{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-13.73mu\resizebox{1305270}{540744}{$\phantom{x}{}_{\phantom{.}{}^{\infty}}$}$\crcr$\scriptscriptstyle\bigcirc$\crcr}}}}\mathcal{A}\to\mathcal{A}\right).

Definition 9.14
.

Recall the adjunction (
7
). For
f:\mathsf{B}\to\mathcal{A}
any map in
\mathbf{d}{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}\mathbf{Alg},
we say that the module
\Omega^{1}_{f}\left(\mathcal{A}\right)
is the
module of differential
1
-forms relative to
f
. When
f=id_{\mathcal{A}},
we denote
\Omega^{1}_{f}\left(\mathcal{A}\right)
simply by
\Omega^{1}\left(\mathcal{A}\right),
and call it the
module of differential
1
-forms of
\mathcal{A}.

Remark 9.15
.

\Omega^{1}\left(\mathcal{A}\right)
is the
cotangent complex

\mathbb{L}_{X}
of
X=\mathbf{Spec}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}\left(\mathcal{A}\right).
More generally,
\Omega^{1}_{f}\left(\mathcal{A}\right)
is the
relative cotangent complex
.

Proposition 9.16
.

[
14
, Example 2.2.18]
,
Let
\mathcal{M}
be a supermanifold, then

\Omega^{1}\left(\mathbf{C}^{\infty}\left(\mathcal{M}\right)\right)\simeq\Omega^{1}_{dR}\left(\mathcal{M}\right).

Due to the adjunction (
7
), we have that

\mathfrak{Der}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu\left(A,M\right)_{f}\simeq\mathbb{M}\mathrm{ap}_{\mathbf{Mod}_{\mathcal{A}}}\left(\Omega^{1}_{f}\left(\mathcal{A}\right),M\right).

Note that the stable
\infty
-category
\mathbf{Mod}_{\mathcal{A}}
is canonically enriched in itself. For
N
and
M
two
\mathcal{A}
-modules, denote their mapping module by
\mathbf{Mod}_{\mathcal{A}}\left(N,M\right).
In light of this, and the above, we make the following definition:

Definition 9.17
.

Let
M
be an
\mathcal{A}
-module and let
f:\mathsf{B}\to\mathcal{A}.
The
\mathcal{A}
-module of derivations of
\mathcal{A}
along
f
with values in
M
is

\underline{\mathfrak{Der}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu}\left(\mathcal{A},M\right)_{f}:=\mathbf{Mod}_{\mathcal{A}}\left(\Omega^{1}_{f}\left(\mathcal{A}\right),M\right),

and the
\mathcal{A}
-module of derivations of
\mathcal{A}
is

\underline{\mathfrak{Der}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu}\left(\mathcal{A}\right):=\mathbf{Mod}_{\mathcal{A}}\left(\Omega^{1}\left(\mathcal{A}\right),\mathcal{A}\right).

We also denote
\underline{\mathfrak{Der}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu}\left(\mathcal{A}\right)
by
\mathfrak{X}\left(\mathcal{A}\right)
and refer to it as the
\mathcal{A}
-module of vector fields on
\mathcal{A}.

Remark 9.18
.

The space of derivations is related to the module of derivations in the following way

\mathfrak{Der}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu\left(\mathcal{A},M\right)_{f}\simeq\mathcal{DK}\left(\tau_{\leq 0}\underline{\mathfrak{Der}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu}\left(\mathcal{A},M\right)_{f}\right),

where
\mathcal{DK}
of a connective
\mathcal{A}
-module is the corresponding simplicial
\mathcal{A}
-module via the Dold-Kan correspondence.

Remark 9.19
.

If
\mathcal{M}
is a
\mathbb{N}
-graded supermanifold,
\mathfrak{X}\left(\mathbf{C}^{\infty}\left(\mathcal{M}\right)\right)\simeq\mathfrak{X}\left(\mathcal{M}\right),
that is the
\mathbf{C}^{\infty}\left(\mathcal{M}\right)
-module of derivations of
\mathbf{C}^{\infty}\left(\mathcal{M}\right)
is equivalent to the
\mathbf{C}^{\infty}\left(\mathcal{M}\right)
-module of graded vector fields on
\mathcal{M},
in the usual sense.

9.3.
Tangent categories

Definition 9.20
.

Let
\mathscr{C}
be an
\infty
-category with finite limits. The
tangent category
to
\mathscr{C}
is the
\infty
-category of excisive functors

\mathcal{T}\mathscr{C}:=\mathbf{Exc}\left(\mathcal{S}\mskip-3.0mu\operatorname{pc}^{\mathsf{fin}}_{*},\mathscr{C}\right)

from finite pointed spaces to
\mathscr{C}.

Let
F:\mathcal{S}\mskip-3.0mu\operatorname{pc}^{\mathsf{fin}}\to\mathscr{C}
be any functor. Then it is the same data as the induced functor

\mathcal{S}\mskip-3.0mu\operatorname{pc}^{\mathsf{fin}}\to\mathscr{C}/F\left(*\right),

which sends a space
X
to
F\left(X\right)\to F\left(*\right).
Now, suppose instead that

F:\mathcal{S}\mskip-3.0mu\operatorname{pc}^{\mathsf{fin}}_{*}\to\mathscr{C}.

Consider the induced functor

\tilde{F}:\mathcal{S}\mskip-3.0mu\operatorname{pc}^{\mathsf{fin}}_{*}\to\mathscr{C}/F\left(*\right).

If
*\to X
is a pointed space, then as we have
*\to X\to*
is the identify of
*,
we have that
\tilde{F}\left(X\right)
is canonically pointed by

\tilde{F}\left(*\to X\right):id_{F\left(*\right)}\to\tilde{F}\left(X\right).

So we have an induced functor

\mathcal{S}\mskip-3.0mu\operatorname{pc}^{\mathsf{fin}}_{*}\to\left(\mathscr{C}/F\left(*\right)\right)_{*}

which is reduced. This functor moreover is excisive if and only if
F
is, in which case we get a reduced excisive functor

\mathcal{S}\mskip-3.0mu\operatorname{pc}^{\mathsf{fin}}_{*}\to\left(\mathscr{C}/F\left(*\right)\right)_{*},

i.e. an object of
\mathbf{Stab}\left(\mathscr{C}/F\left(*\right)\right).
Now suppose that
f:C\to D,
then since
\mathscr{C}
has finite limits, there exists a functor

f^{*}:\mathscr{C}/D\to\mathscr{C}/C

given by pulling back along
f,
right adjoint to the functor induced by composition with
f.
Hence
f^{*}
preserves limits and thus induces a functor

\mathbf{Stab}\left(f\right):\mathbf{Stab}\left(\mathscr{C}/D\right)\to\mathbf{Stab}\left(\mathscr{C}/C\right).

Now suppose that
\alpha:F\Rightarrow G
is an arrow in
\mathcal{T}\mathscr{C},
and
Z
is an object in
\mathcal{S}\mskip-3.0mu\operatorname{pc}^{\mathsf{fin}}_{*}.
Then consider the naturality square for
\alpha
at
Z
:

\textstyle{F\left(Z\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
