\scriptstyle{\alpha\left(Z\right)}
\textstyle{G\left(Z\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\alpha\left(\ast\right)^{*}\left(G\left(Z\right)\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{F\left(\ast\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\alpha\left(\ast\right)}
\textstyle{G\left(\ast\right)}

This part of an explicit unwinding of a canonical equivalence

\mathcal{T}\mathscr{C}\simeq\int_{\mathscr{C}}\mathbf{Stab}\left(\mathscr{C}/\mspace{3mu}\cdot\mspace{3mu}\right).

In particular, evaluation at the terminal object
\ast,

(9)

q:=\mathbf{ev}_{\ast}:\mathcal{T}\mathscr{C}\to\mathscr{C}

is a Cartesian fibration.

Corollary 9.21
.

\mathcal{T}\left(\mathbf{d}{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}\mathbf{Alg}\right)\simeq\int_{\mathbf{d}{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}\mathbf{Alg}}\mathbf{Mod}^{\ast}_{\left(\mspace{3mu}\cdot\mspace{3mu}\right)},

where
\mathbf{Mod}^{\ast}_{\left(\mspace{3mu}\cdot\mspace{3mu}\right)}:\left(\mathbf{d}{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}\mathbf{Alg}\right)^{op}\to\widehat{\mathbf{Cat}}_{\infty}

is the functor induced by restriction of scalars.

Remark 9.22
.

Note that, since taking the induced module is left adjoint to restriction of scalars, it follows that there is a canonical equivalence of
\infty
-categories

\mathcal{T}\left(\mathbf{d}{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}\mathbf{Alg}\right)\simeq\int_{\mathbf{d}{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}\mathbf{Alg}}\mathbf{Mod}^{!}_{\left(\mspace{3mu}\cdot\mspace{3mu}\right)},

where for each
\varphi:\mathcal{A}\to\mathsf{B}

\displaystyle\mathbf{Mod}^{!}_{\left(\varphi\right)}:\mathbf{Mod}_{\mathcal{A}}

\displaystyle\to

\displaystyle\mathbf{Mod}_{\mathsf{B}}

\displaystyle M

\displaystyle\mapsto

\displaystyle\mathsf{B}\underset{\mathcal{A}}{\otimes}M.

In particular,
q
is also a coCartesian fibration, and hence a bifibration.

Let us consider also the canonical functor induced by evaluation at
S^{0}

\pi_{\mathscr{C}}:=\mathbf{ev_{S^{0}}}:\mathcal{T}\mathscr{C}\to\mathscr{C}.

Note that for each
C,

\mathbf{ev_{\ast}}^{-1}\left(C\right)\simeq\mathbf{Stab}\left(\mathscr{C}/C\right),
and thus
\pi_{\mathscr{C}}
restricts to a functor

\mathbf{Stab}\left(\mathscr{C}/C\right)\to\mathscr{C}/C,

which can be identified with
\Omega_{\mathscr{C}}^{\infty}.

Remark 9.23
.

If follows that when
\mathscr{C}=\mathbf{d}{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}\mathbf{Alg},
for each
\mathcal{A},
the restriction of
\pi_{\mathscr{C}}
can be identified with

\mathcal{A}\left[\mspace{3mu}\cdot\mspace{3mu}\right]:\mathbf{Mod}_{\mathcal{A}}\to\mathbf{d}{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}\mathbf{Alg}/A.

Note that the
1^{st}
Taylor approximation
P_{1}
(in the sense of Goodwillie calculus) shows that
\mathcal{T}\mathscr{C}=\mathbf{Exc}\left(\mathcal{S}\mskip-3.0mu\operatorname{pc}^{\mathsf{fin}}_{*},\mathscr{C}\right)
is a reflective subcategory of
\operatorname{Fun}\left(\mathcal{S}\mskip-3.0mu\operatorname{pc}^{\mathsf{fin}}_{*},\mathscr{C}\right),
and hence presentable and closed under limits in
\operatorname{Fun}\left(\mathcal{S}\mskip-3.0mu\operatorname{pc}^{\mathsf{fin}}_{*},\mathscr{C}\right)
. Moreover, the functor
\pi_{\mathscr{C}}
is given by evaluation at
S^{0},
and hence preserves limits. By the adjoint functor theorem, this implies that there exist a left adjoint
\mathbb{L}\dashv\pi_{\mathscr{C}}.

Remark 9.24
.

It is well known that if
\mathcal{E}
is an
\infty
-topos, then
\mathcal{T}\mathcal{E}
is also. By the above, this follows from the observation that the Taylor approximation functor
P_{1}
is left exact.

Definition 9.25
.

For
\mathscr{C}=\mathbf{d}{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}\mathbf{Alg},

\mathbb{L}
is the absolute cotangent complex functor.

Proposition 9.26
.

For
\mathcal{A}\in\mathbf{d}{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}\mathbf{Alg},

\mathbb{L}\left(\mathcal{A}\right)=\left(\mathcal{A},\Omega^{1}\left(\mathcal{A}\right)\right).

Proof.

Let
\mathcal{A}\in\mathbf{d}{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}\mathbf{Alg},
and consider
\mathbb{L}\left(\mathcal{A}\right)\in\mathcal{T}\mathbf{d}{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}\mathbf{Alg}.
Notice that
\mathbb{L}\left(\mathcal{A}\right)
is in the fiber of

\pi:\tau\left(\mathbf{d}{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}\mathbf{Alg}\right)\to\mathbf{d}{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}\mathbf{Alg}

over
\mathcal{A},
which can be identified with
\mathbf{Mod}_{\mathcal{A}}.
Now, let
M\in\mathbf{Mod}_{\mathcal{A}}.
We have a pullback diagram

\textstyle{\mathbb{M}\mathrm{ap}_{\mathbf{Mod}_{\mathcal{A}}}\left(\mathbb{L}\left(\mathcal{A}\right),M\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathbb{M}\mathrm{ap}_{\mathcal{T}\mathbf{d}{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}\mathbf{Alg}}\left(\mathbb{L}\left(\mathcal{A}\right),M\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\ast\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{id_{\mathcal{A}}}
\textstyle{\mathbb{M}\mathrm{ap}_{\mathbf{d}{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}\mathbf{Alg}}\left(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{A}\right).}

But since

\mathbb{M}\mathrm{ap}_{\mathcal{T}\mathbf{d}{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}\mathbf{Alg}}\left(\mathbb{L}\left(\mathcal{A}\right),M\right)\simeq\mathbb{M}\mathrm{ap}_{\mathbf{d}{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}\mathbf{Alg}}\left(\mathcal{A},\pi_{\mathscr{C}}\left(M\right)\right),

and
\pi_{\mathscr{C}}\left(M\right)\simeq\mathcal{A}\left[\mspace{3mu}\cdot\mspace{3mu}\right],
we can identify
\mathbb{M}\mathrm{ap}_{\mathbf{Mod}_{\mathcal{A}}}\left(\mathbb{L}\left(\mathcal{A}\right),M\right)
with

\mathbb{M}\mathrm{ap}_{\mathbf{d}{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}\mathbf{Alg}/A}\left(id_{\mathcal{A}},\mathcal{A}\left[\mspace{3mu}\cdot\mspace{3mu}\right]\right)\simeq\mathbb{M}\mathrm{ap}_{\mathbf{Mod}_{\mathcal{A}}}\left(\Omega^{1}\left(\mathcal{A}\right),M\right).

∎

9.4.
Sheaves of modules

9.4.1.
{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}
-ringed topoi

Recall (Definition
8.1
) that an
{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}
-ringed
\infty
-topos is a pair
\left(\mathcal{E},\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E}}\right)
of an
\infty
-topos
\mathcal{E}
with a sheaf
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E}}
on
\mathcal{E}
with values in
\mathbf{d}{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}\mathbf{Alg}.

Remark 9.27
.

If
\mathcal{E}=\mathcal{S}\mskip-3.0mu\operatorname{pc},

\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E}}
may be identified with a
{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}
-ring object in spaces, i.e. an object of
\mathbf{d}{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}\mathbf{Alg}.

Definition 9.28
.

(Recall the coCartesian fibration
q
(
9
).) Let
\left(\mathcal{E},\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E}}\right)
be an
{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}
-ringed
\infty
-topos. An
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E}}
-module
is a sheaf
\mathscr{M}
on
\mathcal{E},
with values in
\mathcal{T}\mathbf{d}{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}\mathbf{Alg},
which is a lift of the structure sheaf:

\textstyle{\mathcal{T}\mathbf{d}{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}\mathbf{Alg}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{q}
\textstyle{\mathcal{E}^{op}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E}}}
\scriptstyle{\mathscr{M}}
\textstyle{\mathbf{d}{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}\mathbf{Alg}.}

I.e., the
\infty
-category fits into a pullback diagram

\textstyle{\mathbf{Mod}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Shv}\left(\mathcal{E},\mathcal{T}\mathbf{d}{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}\mathbf{Alg}\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{q_{!}}
\textstyle{\ast\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E}}}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Shv}\left(\mathcal{E},\mathbf{d}{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}\mathbf{Alg}\right).}

Less formally, an
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E}}
-module is a sheaf
\mathscr{M}
of
\mathbb{R}
-modules on
\mathcal{E}
together with the structure of an
\mathcal{E}\left(E\right)
-module on
\mathscr{M}\left(E\right)
for each
E\in\mathcal{E}
, such that for each
f:E\to F
in
\mathcal{E},
the canonical map

\mathscr{M}\left(f\right):\mathscr{M}\left(F\right)\to\mathscr{M}\left(E\right)

is a map of
\mathcal{E}\left(F\right)
-modules.

Remark 9.29
.

Notice that we have a pullback diagram

\textstyle{\mathcal{T}\mathbf{d}{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}\mathbf{Alg}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{T}\mathsf{ComAlg}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathbf{d}{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}\mathbf{Alg}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathsf{ComAlg},}

where
\mathsf{ComAlg}
is the
\infty
-category of
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-ring spectra. As such, an
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E}}
-module sheaf is precisely the same as a module sheaf for the underlying sheaf of
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-rings. Such a sheaf is the same as an
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-ring object in
\mathcal{E}.
A module for it is a module for this
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-ring object. This point of view is useful in establishing functoriality results.

Remark 9.30
.

For any
{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}
-ringed
\infty
-topos
\left(\mathcal{E},\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E}}\right),
there is a canonical limit-preserving functor

U_{\mathcal{E}}:\mathbf{Mod}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E}}}\to\mathbf{Stab}\left(\mathcal{E}\right)\simeq\operatorname{Shv}\left(\mathcal{E},\mathcal{S}\mskip-3.0mu\operatorname{pt}\right),

which object-wise assigns to
E\in\mathcal{E},
the underlying spectrum of
\mathscr{M}\left(E\right),
i.e. the underlying
\mathbb{S}
-module of the underlying
H\mathbb{R}
-module (equivalently chain complex in
\mathbb{R}
-modules) of
\mathscr{M}\left(E\right),
where
\mathbb{S}
is the sphere spectrum. To be more precise, if the structure sheaf is
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-graded, we have

U_{\mathcal{E}}:\mathbf{Mod}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E}}}\to\mathbf{Stab}\left(\mathcal{E}\right)\simeq\operatorname{Shv}\left(\mathcal{E},\mathcal{S}\mskip-3.0mu\operatorname{pt}\right)^{2},

associating a spectrum object to both the even and odd components of the module sheaf, but we will not dwell on this.

Definition 9.31
.

Let
\mathcal{X}
be an object in an
{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}
-ringed
\infty
-topos
\left(\mathcal{E},\mathcal{O}\right).
Then the functor

\mathbb{TOT}_{\mathcal{X}}:=\Omega_{\mathcal{E}/\mathcal{X}}^{\infty}\circ U_{\mathcal{E}/\mathcal{X}}:\mathbf{Mod}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}}\to\mathcal{E}/\mathcal{X},

where
\Omega_{\mathcal{E}/\mathcal{X}}^{\infty}:\mathcal{S}\mskip-3.0mu\operatorname{pt}\left(\mathcal{E}\right)\to\mathcal{E}
is the right adjoint to
\Sigma^{\infty}_{\mathcal{E}/\mathcal{X},+},

is called the
total space
functor.

Definition 9.32
.

Given a derived stack
\mathcal{X}\in\mathcal{E}_{\mathfrak{Der}},
an
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}
-module
is a module sheaf for the
{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}
-ringed
\infty
-topos
\left(\mathcal{E}_{\mathfrak{Der}}/\mathcal{X},\mathcal{O}|_{\mathcal{X}}\right).

9.5.
Functoriality of module sheaves

The
\infty
-category of
{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}
-ringed
\infty
-topoi may be defined informally as the
\infty
-category whose objects are
{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}
-ringed
\infty
-topoi, and whose morphisms
\left(\mathcal{E},\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E}}\right)\to\left(\mathcal{F},\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{F}}\right)
consists of a geometric morphism

f:\mathcal{E}\to\mathcal{F},

together with a morphism of sheaves of
{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}
-algebras over
\mathcal{F}

\alpha:\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{F}}\to f_{*}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E}}.

This can be rigorously constructed as a Cartesian fibration over
\mathfrak{Top}_{\infty},
c.f.
[
7
, Section 2.4]
.

Given such a morphism, if
\mathscr{M}
is an
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E}}
-module, then

f_{\star}\left(\mathscr{M}\right):=\alpha^{\star}\left(f_{*}\mathscr{M}\right)

is an
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{F}}
-module, where
\alpha^{\star}
is the functor given by restriction of scalars. This assembles into a functor

f_{\star}:\mathbf{Mod}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E}}}\to\mathbf{Mod}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{F}}}.

Notice also that if
\mathscr{N}
is an
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{F}}
-module, then
\mathscr{N}
has the canonical structure of a
f^{*}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{F}}
-module, and hence

f^{\star}\left(\mathscr{N}\right):=\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E}}\underset{f^{*}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{F}}}{\otimes}f^{*}\mathscr{N}

has the canonical structure of a
\mathcal{E}
-module. This assembles into a functor

f^{\star}:\mathbf{Mod}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{F}}}\to\mathbf{Mod}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E}}},

which is readily checked to be left adjoint to
f_{\star}

[
21
, Section 2.5]
.

9.5.1.
Working internally

Note that this is not special for for
{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}
-rings. There is a completely analogous story where
\mathcal{E}
is allowed to take values in not-necessarily commutative algebras. We will make the convention that in the non-commutative case,
\mathbf{Mod}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E}}}
is the
\infty
-category of left
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E}}
-modules. The
\infty
-category of right
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E}}
-modules is thus
\mathbf{Mod}_{\left(\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E}}\right)^{op}}.
This will be important later when we discuss
\mathcal{D}
-modules.

Here is a special case. Suppose that
\left(\mathcal{E},\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E}}\right)
is an
{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}
-ringed
\infty
-topos, and that
\mathcal{R}
and
\mathcal{S}
are sheaves of not necessarily commutative or bounded
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E}}
-algebras. An
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E}}
-algebra homomorphism
\varphi:\mathcal{S}\to\mathcal{R}
is the same data as a morphism of ringed
\infty
-topoi

f:\left(\mathcal{E},\mathcal{R}\right)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\left(id,\alpha\right)}}{{-\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu-\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu-\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu-\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu-\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu-\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu-\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu-\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu-\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu-\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu-\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu-\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu\longrightarrow}}\left(\mathcal{E},\mathcal{S}\right).

A pertinent example to have in mind is
\mathcal{E}=\mathbf{Sh}\left(M\right)
for a manifold
M,

\mathcal{E}=\Delta\left(\mathbb{R}\right)
—the constant sheaf with value
\mathbb{R},

\mathcal{S}=\mathbf{C}^{\infty}_{M}
—smooth functions on
M,
and
\mathcal{R}=\mathcal{D}_{M}
—the sheaf of linear differential operators on
M,
locally of finite degree. In this case, there are the standard change of ring functors

\alpha_{!}\dashv\alpha^{\star}\dashv\alpha_{\star},

with
\alpha_{!}
the induced module construction
\mathcal{S}\underset{\mathcal{R}}{\otimes}\left(\mspace{3mu}\cdot\mspace{3mu}\right),

\alpha^{\star}
restriction of scalars, and
\alpha_{\star}
the co-induced module functor. Given a
\mathcal{S}
-module
N,
the underlying
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E}}
-module of
\alpha_{\star}N
is the same as
\mathbf{Mod}_{\mathcal{S}}\left(\mathcal{R},N\right),
but where the left
\mathcal{R}
-module structure is induced from that on
\mathcal{S}
via
\alpha.
Notice that we have

f^{\star}=\alpha_{!}\mbox{, }f_{\star}=\alpha^{\star}.

As such, we will make the definition

f^{!}:=\alpha_{\star}.

9.5.2.
Module Spec

Consider the special case when
\left(\mathcal{F},\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{F}}\right)=\left(\mathcal{S}\mskip-3.0mu\operatorname{pc},\Gamma\left(\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}\right)\right),
where we are using the canonical identification

\operatorname{Shv}\left(\mathcal{S}\mskip-3.0mu\operatorname{pc},\mathbf{d}{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}\mathbf{Alg}\right)\simeq\mathbf{d}{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}\mathbf{Alg},

and where
f:\mathcal{E}\to\mathcal{S}\mskip-3.0mu\operatorname{pc}
is the (homotopically) unique map, and
\alpha
is the identity (since
f_{*}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E}}=\Gamma\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E}}
). In this case

f_{\star}=\Gamma:\mathbf{Mod}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E}}}\to\mathbf{Mod}_{\Gamma\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E}}}

is the global sections functor.

Definition 9.33
.

With
f
as in the preceding paragraph, we call
f^{\star}
the
module spec
functor, and denote it

\mathbf{MSpec}_{\mathcal{E}}:\mathbf{Mod}_{\Gamma\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E}}}\to\mathbf{Mod}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E}}}.

Lemma 9.34
.

Suppose that

\textstyle{\mathcal{E}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{g}
\scriptstyle{f}
\textstyle{\mathcal{F}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{h}
\textstyle{\mathcal{G}}

is a commutative diagram of
{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}
-ringed
\infty
-topoi. Then

g_{\star}\simeq h_{\star}\circ f_{\star}\mbox{ and }g^{\star}\simeq f^{\star}\circ h^{\star}.

Proof.

The former follows immediately from the fact that for the direct image functors for the corresponding geometric morphisms satisfies
g_{*}\simeq h_{*}\circ f_{*}.
The latter then follows from uniqueness of adjoints.
∎

Corollary 9.35
.

For any morphism
f:\mathcal{E}\to\mathcal{F}
of
{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}
-ringed
\infty
-topoi, the following diagram commutes up to homotopy:

\textstyle{\mathbf{Mod}_{\Gamma\left(\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{F}}\right)}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathbf{MSpec}_{\mathcal{F}}}
\scriptstyle{\Gamma\left(f\right)_{!}}
\textstyle{\mathbf{Mod}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{F}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f^{\star}}
\textstyle{\mathbf{Mod}_{\Gamma\left(\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E}}\right)}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathbf{MSpec}_{\mathcal{E}}}
\textstyle{\mathbf{Mod}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E}}},}

where

\Gamma\left(f\right)_{!}\left(M\right)=\Gamma\left(\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E}}\right)\underset{\Gamma\left(\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{F}}\right)}{\otimes}M.

9.6.
Quasi-coherent sheaves

Definition 9.36
.

Let
\mathcal{A}\in\mathbf{d}{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}\mathbf{Alg}
be a
\mathbf{C}^{\infty}
-algebra. We can regard
\mathbf{Spec}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}\left(\mathcal{A}\right)
as a
\mathbf{C}^{\infty}
-ringed
\infty
-topos, rather than simply a topological space, i.e. we can consider the pair

\left(\operatorname{Shv}\left(\underline{\mathbf{Spec}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}\left(\mathcal{A}\right)}\right),\mathcal{O}_{A}\right).

Denote by

\mathbf{MSpec}_{\mathcal{A}}:\mathbf{Mod}_{\mathcal{A}}\to\mathbf{Mod}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{A}}}

the functor
\mathbf{MSpec}_{\mathcal{E}}
for
\left(\mathcal{E},\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E}}\right)
the above
\mathbf{C}^{\infty}
-ringed
\infty
-topos. An
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{A}}
-sheaf over
\underline{\mathbf{Spec}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}\left(\mathcal{A}\right)}
is
quasi-coherent
if is in the essential image of
\mathbf{MSpec}_{\mathcal{A}}.
Denote the full subcategory of
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{A}}
-modules on the quasi-coherent sheaves by
\mathbf{QC}\left(\mathbf{Spec}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}\left(\mathcal{A}\right)\right).

Proposition 9.37
.

For a complete
\mathbf{C}^{\infty}
-ring
\mathcal{A}
and a given
\mathcal{A}
-module
M,

\mathbf{MSpec}\left(M\right)
is the sheafification of the presheaf
\tilde{M}
which assigns to an open subset
U
the module

\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{A}}\left(U\right)\underset{\mathcal{A}}{\otimes}M.

Proof.

Since
\mathcal{A}
is complete,
\mathcal{A}\simeq\Gamma\left(\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{A}}\right),
so the unique geometric morphism

\operatorname{Shv}\left(\underline{\mathbf{Spec}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}\left(\mathcal{A}\right)}\right)\to\mathcal{S}\mskip-3.0mu\operatorname{pc}

canonically gives rise to a morphism of
{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}
-ringed
\infty
-topoi

t:\left(\operatorname{Shv}\left(\underline{\mathbf{Spec}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}\left(\mathcal{A}\right)}\right),\mathcal{O}_{A}\right)\to\left(\mathcal{S}\mskip-3.0mu\operatorname{pc},\mathcal{A}\right).

Denoting by
i
the inclusion of the sheaf
\infty
-category

\operatorname{Shv}\left(\underline{\mathbf{Spec}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}\left(\mathcal{A}\right)}\right)
into the presheaf
\infty
-category
\Psh\left(\mathsf{Op}\left(\underline{\mathbf{Spec}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}\left(\mathcal{A}\right)}\right)\right),

and denoting by
a
its left adjoint (sheafification), the pair
\left(i,a\right)
constitutes a geometric morphism

\operatorname{Shv}\left(\underline{\mathbf{Spec}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}\left(\mathcal{A}\right)}\right)\to\Psh\left(\mathsf{Op}\left(\underline{\mathbf{Spec}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}\left(\mathcal{A}\right)}\right)\right).

By abuse of notation, denote the induced map of
{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}
-ringed
\infty
-topoi

i:\left(\operatorname{Shv}\left(\underline{\mathbf{Spec}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}\left(\mathcal{A}\right)}\right),O_{\mathcal{A}}\right)\to\left(\Psh\left(\mathsf{Op}\left(\underline{\mathbf{Spec}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}\left(\mathcal{A}\right)}\right)\right),i_{*}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{A}}\right).

Since
\mathcal{S}\mskip-3.0mu\operatorname{pc}
is the terminal
\infty
-topos, there is a unique factorization of
t
as

\textstyle{\left(\operatorname{Shv}\left(\underline{\mathbf{Spec}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}\left(\mathcal{A}\right)}\right),O_{\mathcal{A}}\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i}
\scriptstyle{t}
\textstyle{\left(\mathcal{S}\mskip-3.0mu\operatorname{pc},\mathcal{A}\right)}
\textstyle{\left(\Psh\left(\mathsf{Op}\left(\underline{\mathbf{Spec}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}\left(\mathcal{A}\right)}\right)\right),i_{*}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{A}}\right).\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{s}

Note that
s^{*}
is the constant presheaf functor, so for
M
any
\mathcal{A}
-module and
U
any open subset,

s^{\star}\left(M\right)\left(U\right)=\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{A}}\left(U\right)\underset{\mathcal{A}}{\otimes}M.

Now, for
\mathcal{F}
any
i_{*}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{A}}
-module, since
i^{*}i_{*}\mathcal{O}\mathcal{A}\simeq\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{A}}
since
i^{*}=a
and
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{A}}
is a sheaf, it follows that
i^{\star}\mathcal{F}\simeq a\left(\mathcal{F}\right),
i.e.
i^{\star}\mathcal{F}
is simply the sheafification of
\mathcal{F}.
Combining these two observations with the fact that

\mathbf{MSpec}\left(M\right)=t^{\star}M\simeq i^{\star}\left(s^{\star}M\right),

yields the desired result.
∎

Theorem 9.38
.

[
14
, Lemma 5.1.33]
,
Let
\mathcal{A}
be a complete
{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}
-ring. Then every
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{A}}
-module is quasi-coherent, and hence

\mathbf{QC}\left(\mathbf{Spec}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}\left(\mathcal{A}\right)\right)=\mathbf{Mod}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{A}}}.

Moreover, the right adjoint
\Gamma_{\mathcal{A}}\vdash\mathbf{MSpec}_{\mathcal{A}}
is fully faithful, and the adjunction
\mathbf{MSpec}_{\mathcal{A}}\dashv\Gamma_{\mathcal{A}}
restricts to an equivalence of
\infty
-categories between
\mathbf{Mod}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{A}}}
and the full subcategory of
\mathbf{Mod}_{\mathcal{A}}
on those
\mathcal{A}
-modules
M
such that the unit

\eta_{M}:M\to\Gamma_{\mathcal{A}}\left(\mathbf{MSpec}\left(M\right)\right)

is an equivalence.

Definition 9.39
.

The full subcategory of
\mathbf{Mod}_{\mathcal{A}}
on those sheaves for which the unit is an equivalence is called the subcategory of
complete

\mathcal{A}
-modules, which we denote by
\widehat{\mathbf{Mod}}_{\mathcal{A}}
. We also denote the reflector
\Gamma\circ\mathbf{MSpec}
by
\widehat{\left(\mspace{3mu}\cdot\mspace{3mu}\right)}.

Remark 9.40
.

Since
\widehat{\mathbf{Mod}}_{\mathcal{A}}
is a reflective localization of the presentable
\infty
-category
\mathbf{Mod}_{\mathcal{A}},

\widehat{\mathbf{Mod}}_{\mathcal{A}}
is also presentable.

Remark 9.41
.

[
14
, Example 5.1.36]

Since the subcategory
\widehat{\mathbf{Mod}}_{\mathcal{A}},
is reflective, it is closed under limits and retracts Moreover, for complete
{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}
-algebras
\mathcal{A},

\displaystyle\Gamma\mathcal{O}_{\mathbf{Spec}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}\left(\mathcal{A}\right)}

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle\Gamma\mathbf{MSpec}\mathcal{A},

so
\mathcal{A}\in\widehat{\mathbf{Mod}}_{\mathcal{A}}.
It follows that
\widehat{\mathbf{Mod}}_{\mathcal{A}}
contains all
finitely presented
\mathcal{A}
-modules.

Remark 9.42
.

As
\widehat{\mathbf{Mod}}_{\mathcal{A}}\simeq\mathbf{Mod}_{\mathbf{Spec}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}\left(\mathcal{A}\right)},
it follows that it is a symmetric monoidal with an inherited tensor product
\widehat{\otimes}
from that of
\mathbf{Mod}_{\mathbf{Spec}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}\left(\mathcal{A}\right)}
. By checking on stalks, one can see that in fact

M\widehat{\otimes}N\simeq\widehat{M\otimes N}.

Let
\varphi:\mathcal{A}\to\mathcal{B}
be a morphism of
{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}
-rings. Then we have the induced morphism of
{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}
-ringed
\infty
-topoi

\mathbf{Spec}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}\left(\varphi\right):\left(\operatorname{Shv}\left(\underline{\mathbf{Spec}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}\left(\mathcal{B}\right)}\right),\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{B}}\right)\to\left(\operatorname{Shv}\left(\underline{\mathbf{Spec}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}\left(\mathcal{A}\right)}\right),\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{A}}\right).

Consider the functor

\mathbf{Spec}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}\left(\varphi\right)^{\star}:\mathbf{Mod}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{A}}}\to\mathbf{Mod}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{B}}}.

Under the identifications

\mathbf{Mod}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{A}}}\simeq\widehat{\mathbf{Mod}}_{\mathcal{A}},
and
\mathbf{Mod}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{B}}}\simeq\widehat{\mathbf{Mod}}_{\mathcal{B}}
, this induces a functor

\widehat{\mathbf{Mod}}_{\mathcal{A}}\to\widehat{\mathbf{Mod}}_{\mathcal{B}}

Definition 9.43
.

For a complete
\mathcal{A}
-module
M,
we denote the image of
M
under the above functor by

\mathcal{B}\underset{\mathcal{A}}{\widehat{\otimes}}M,

and call it the
completed induced module of
M
.

Proposition 9.44
.

There is a canonical identification

\widehat{\mathcal{B}\underset{\mathcal{A}}{\otimes}M}\simeq\mathcal{B}\underset{\mathcal{A}}{\widehat{\otimes}}M.

Proof.

On one hand,
\widehat{\mathcal{B}\underset{\mathcal{A}}{\otimes}M}
is global sections of the sheafification of the presheaf on
\mathbf{Bas}\left(\mathbf{Spec}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}\left(\mathcal{B}\right)\right)
which assigns to
U_{b}=\mathbf{Spec}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}\left(\mathcal{B}\left[b^{-1}\right]\right)
the module

\mathcal{B}\left[b^{-1}\right]\underset{\mathcal{B}}{\otimes}\left[\mathcal{B}\underset{A}{\otimes}M\right].

On the other hand,
\mathcal{B}\underset{\mathcal{A}}{\widehat{\otimes}}M
is global sections of the sheafification of the presheaf which assigns to
U_{b}
the module

\mathcal{B}\left[b^{-1}\right]\underset{\mathcal{A}}{\otimes}M,

but these presheaves are canonically equivalent.
∎

Lemma 9.45
.

Let
\left(\mathcal{E},\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E}}\right)
be an
{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}
-ringed
\infty
-topos. Then the composite functor

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}^{op}

\displaystyle\to

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathbf{Cat}}_{\infty}

\displaystyle E

\displaystyle\mapsto

\displaystyle\mathbf{Mod}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E}}|_{E}}

is a sheaf (i.e. preserves small limits).

Proof.

This follows almost verbatim by the proof of
[
23
, Remark 2.1.11]
.
∎

Denote by
\mathbf{QC}
the composite functor

\mathscr{D}^{op}\hookrightarrow\left(\mathbf{d}{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}\mathbf{Alg}\right)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathbf{Spec}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}}}{{-\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu-\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu-\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu-\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu-\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu-\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu\longrightarrow}}\left(\mathfrak{Top}_{\infty}^{{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}}\right)^{op}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathbf{Mod}}}{{-\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu-\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu-\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu-\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu-\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu-\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu\longrightarrow}}\widehat{\mathbf{Cat}}_{\infty}

sending an algebra
\mathcal{A}
to the
\infty
-category
\mathbf{Mod}_{\mathcal{O}_{A}}\simeq\widehat{\mathbf{Mod}}_{\mathcal{A}}.

Proposition 9.46
.

The above presheaf of large
\infty
-categories is a sheaf.

Proof.

Let
\mathscr{C}
be an
\infty
-category with small limits. Define a functor
G:\left(\mathfrak{Top}_{\infty}^{{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}}\right)^{op}\to\mathscr{C}
to be a sheaf if and only if for each
\left(\mathcal{E},\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E}}\right),

\chi_{\mathcal{E}}^{*}G:\mathcal{E}^{op}\to\mathscr{C}
preserves small limits, where

\displaystyle\chi_{\mathcal{E}}:\mathcal{E}

\displaystyle\to

\displaystyle\mathfrak{Top}_{\infty}^{{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}}

\displaystyle E

\displaystyle\mapsto

\displaystyle\left(\mathcal{E}/E,\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E}}|_{E}\right).

For each
\left(\mathcal{E},\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E}}\right),
the sheaf from Lemma
9.45
is precisely
\chi_{\mathcal{E}}^{*}\mathbf{Mod},
hence
\mathbf{Mod}
is a sheaf on
\mathfrak{Top}_{\infty}^{{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}}.
Now let
D
be an element of
\mathscr{D},
and let
\left(U_{\alpha}\hookrightarrow D\right)
be an open cover by elements of
\mathscr{D}.
Then in the topos of sheaves
\operatorname{Shv}\left(\underline{\mathbf{Spec}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}}\left(\mathcal{A}\right)\right)
on the underlying space of
D,
the colimit of the associated Čech nerve of the cover is the terminal object (the whole space
D
). This means that
D
is the
limit
in the opposite category, and since the restriction of
\mathbf{Mod}
preserves limits, we have that
\mathbf{QC}\left(D\right)
is the limit of the cosimplicial diagram of
\mathbf{QC}\left(U_{\alpha}\right).
Thus
\mathbf{QC}
is a sheaf.
∎

The sheaf
\mathbf{QC}
of
\infty
-categories on
\mathscr{D}
is equivalent to a sheaf of
\infty
-categories on
\mathcal{E}_{\mathfrak{Der}}.
Concretely, one may take the left Kan extension of
\mathbf{QC}
along the Yoneda embedding of
\mathscr{D}
into
\mathcal{E}_{\mathfrak{Der}}
of
\mathbf{QC}.
We will abuse notation and write this also as

\mathbf{QC}:\mathcal{E}_{\mathfrak{Der}}^{op}\to\widehat{\operatorname{Cat}}_{\infty}.

Definition 9.47
.

Let
E\in\mathcal{E}_{\mathfrak{Der}}
be a derived stack. Then the
\infty
-category of
quasi-coherent sheaves
on
E
is the
\infty
-category
\mathbf{QC}\left(E\right).
For
f:Y\to E
any morphism in
\mathcal{E}_{\mathfrak{Der}}
, we denote by
f^{\star}
the functor

\mathbf{QC}\left(f\right):\mathbf{QC}\left(E\right)\to\mathbf{QC}\left(F\right)

and call it the
inverse image
functor.

Remark 9.48
.

Let
f:\mathbf{Spec}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}\left(\mathcal{B}\right)\to\mathbf{Spec}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}\left(\mathcal{A}\right)
be a morphism in
\mathscr{D}.
Then, upon the identifications of quasi-coherent sheaves with complete modules:

\mathbf{QC}\left(\mathbf{Spec}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}\left(\mathcal{B}\right)\right)\simeq\widehat{\mathbf{Mod}}_{\mathcal{B}}

and

\mathbf{QC}\left(\mathbf{Spec}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}\left(\mathcal{A}\right)\right)\simeq\widehat{\mathbf{Mod}}_{\mathcal{A}},

f^{\star}:\widehat{\mathbf{Mod}}_{\mathcal{A}}\to\widehat{\mathbf{Mod}}_{\mathcal{B}}

can be identified with the completed induced module functor (Definition
9.43
).

Corollary 9.49
.

Let
\mathcal{X}\in\mathcal{E}_{\mathfrak{Der}},
then the
\infty
-category of quasi-coherent sheaves can be presented as

\mathbf{QC}\left(\mathcal{X}\right)\simeq\underset{\mathbf{Spec}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}\left(\mathcal{A}\right)\to X}{\varprojlim\mspace{3mu}}\widehat{\mathbf{Mod}}_{\mathcal{A}},

with
\mathcal{A}
ranging over all of
\mathscr{D}.

Proof.

The sheaf
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E}_{\mathfrak{Der}}}^{*}\mathbf{QC}
on
\mathcal{E}_{\mathfrak{Der}}
is a limit preserving functor

\mathcal{E}_{\mathfrak{Der}}^{op}\to\widehat{\mathbf{Cat}}_{\infty}.

Since
\mathscr{D}
is a site for
\mathcal{E}_{\mathfrak{Der}},
it follows that

\mathcal{X}\simeq\underset{\mathbf{Spec}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}\left(\mathcal{A}\right)\to\mathcal{X}}{\underrightarrow{\mbox{colim}\vskip 0.5pt}\mspace{4mu}}\mathbf{Spec}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}\left(\mathcal{A}\right),

and by the preservation of limits we then have an identification

\displaystyle\mathbf{QC}\left(\mathcal{X}\right)

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle\underset{\mathbf{Spec}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}\left(\mathcal{A}\right)\to\mathcal{X}}{\varprojlim\mspace{3mu}}\mathbf{QC}\left(\mathbf{Spec}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}\left(\mathcal{A}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle\underset{\mathbf{Spec}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}\left(\mathcal{A}\right)\to\mathcal{X}}{\varprojlim\mspace{3mu}}\widehat{\mathbf{Mod}}_{\mathcal{A}}

∎

We now wish to show that for any
E,

\mathbf{QC}\left(E\right)
has the canonical presentation as a full subcategory of
\mathbf{Mod}_{\mathcal{O}_{E}}.
We will start with the case that
E=\mathbf{Spec}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}\left(\mathcal{A}\right)
is an affine object of
\mathscr{D}.

Lemma 9.50
.

For
\mathcal{A}
any
\mathbf{C}^{\infty}
-ring, there is pair of geometric morphisms

\Lambda:\mathcal{E}_{\mathfrak{Der}}/\mathbf{Spec}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}\left(\mathcal{A}\right)\to\operatorname{Shv}\left(\underline{\mathbf{Spec}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}\left(\mathcal{A}\right)}\right)

and

\Psi:\operatorname{Shv}\left(\underline{\mathbf{Spec}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}\left(\mathcal{A}\right)}\right)\to\mathcal{E}_{\mathfrak{Der}}/\mathbf{Spec}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}\left(\mathcal{A}\right)

such that
\Lambda_{*}\simeq\Psi^{*}
and
\Lambda^{*}
is fully faithful.

Proof.

Without loss of generality, assume that
\mathcal{A}
is complete. Denote by

j:\mathbf{Bas}\left(\mathbf{Spec}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}\left(\mathcal{A}\right)\right)\to\mathscr{D}/\mathbf{Spec}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}\left(\mathcal{A}\right)

the functor sending each basic open subset
U_{a}
to

\mathbf{Spec}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}\left(\mathcal{A}\left[a^{-1}\right]\right)\to\mathbf{Spec}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}\left(\mathcal{A}\right).

Since this functor preserves covers, there is a restriction functor

j^{*}:\mathcal{E}_{\mathfrak{Der}}/\mathbf{Spec}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}\left(\mathcal{A}\right)\to\operatorname{Shv}\left(\underline{\mathbf{Spec}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}\left(\mathcal{A}\right)}\right)

with a left adjoint
j_{!}.
Moreover, it follows from
[
7
, Proposition 5.27]
that
j_{!}
preserves finite limits, hence the pair
\left(j^{*},j_{!}\right)
constitutes a geometric morphism
\Lambda
with
\Lambda^{*}=j_{!}
and
\Lambda_{*}=j^{*}.
The functor
j^{*}
also has a natural
right
adjoint
j_{*},
and the pair
\left(j_{*},j^{*}\right)
is a geometric morphism
\Psi
with

\Psi^{*}=j^{*}

and

\Psi_{*}=j_{*}.

The fully faithfulness of
\Lambda^{*}
follows by an analogous proof to that of
[
7
, Proposition 5.27]
.
∎

Corollary 9.51
.

With the notation of the preceding theorem,

\Lambda\circ\Psi\simeq id.

Proof.

Since
\Lambda^{*}
is fully faithful, it follows that the unit

id\to\Lambda_{*}\Lambda^{*}

is an equivalence, but since
\Lambda_{*}=\Psi^{*},
we have

\displaystyle id

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle\Psi^{*}\Lambda^{*}

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle\left(\Lambda\circ\Psi\right)^{*}

It follows that
\Lambda\circ\Psi\simeq id.

∎

Corollary 9.52
.

Let
X=\mathbf{Spec}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}\left(\mathcal{A}\right)\in\mathcal{E}_{\mathfrak{Der}}.
Then there is a triple adjunction between
\mathbf{Mod}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{A}}}
and
\mathbf{Mod}_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}
given by

\Lambda^{\star}\dashv\Lambda_{\star}=\Psi^{\star}\dashv\Psi_{\star}.

Moreover,
\Lambda^{\star}
and
\Psi_{\star}
are both fully faithful.

Proof.

Firstly, notice that

\displaystyle\Lambda_{*}\mathcal{O}_{X}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle j^{*}\mathcal{O}_{X}

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}_{A}.

This canonical equivalence
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{A}}\to\Lambda_{*}\mathcal{O}_{X}
equips the geometric morphism
\Lambda
with the structure of a map of
{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}
-ringed
\infty
-topoi. The inverse equivalence, after identifying
\Psi^{*}
with
\Lambda_{*}
is a morphism

\Psi^{*}\mathcal{O}_{X}\to\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{A}}

and is adjoint to a map

\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}\to\Psi_{*}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{A}}

which equips
\Psi
with the structure of a map of
{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}
-ringed
\infty
-topoi.

The first statement of the corollary now follows immediately from the fact that
\Psi^{*}=\Lambda_{*}.
The second statement follows from the fact that the unit map

id\to\Psi^{\star}\Psi_{\star}

can be identified with the canonical equivalence
\Lambda_{\star}\Psi_{\star}\simeq id
and the counit map

\Lambda^{*}\Lambda_{*}\to id

can be identified with the canonical equivalence

\Lambda^{\star}\Psi^{\star}\simeq id.

∎

Theorem 9.53
.

For
X=\mathbf{Spec}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}\left(\mathcal{A}\right)\in\mathcal{E}_{\mathfrak{Der}},
the fully faithful functor

\Lambda^{\star}:\mathbf{Mod}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{A}}}\hookrightarrow\mathbf{Mod}_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}

identifies

\mathbf{QC}\left(\mathbf{Spec}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}\left(\mathcal{A}\right)\right)=\mathbf{Mod}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{A}}}

with the subcategory
\mathbf{Mod}_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}
spanned by those
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}
-modules of the form
\mathbf{MSpec}_{\mathcal{E}_{\mathfrak{Der}}/X}\left(M\right)
for an
\mathcal{A}
-module
M.

Proof.

Denote by

t_{\mathcal{A}}:\left(\operatorname{Shv}\left(\underline{\mathbf{Spec}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}\left(\mathcal{A}\right)}\right),\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{A}}\right)\to\left(\mathcal{S}\mskip-3.0mu\operatorname{pc},\mathcal{A}\right)

and

t_{X}:\left(\mathcal{E}_{\mathfrak{Der}}/X,\mathcal{O}_{X}\right)\to\left(\mathcal{S}\mskip-3.0mu\operatorname{pc},\mathcal{A}\right)

the canonical morphisms. Observe that the composite

\left(\operatorname{Shv}\left(\underline{\mathbf{Spec}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}\left(\mathcal{A}\right)}\right),\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{A}}\right)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\Psi}}{{\longrightarrow}}\left(\mathcal{E}_{\mathfrak{Der}}/X,\mathcal{O}_{X}\right)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle t_{X}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\left(\mathcal{S}\mskip-3.0mu\operatorname{pc},\mathcal{A}\right)

is equivalent to
t_{\mathcal{A}}
and the composition

\left(\mathcal{E}_{\mathfrak{Der}}/X,\mathcal{O}_{X}\right)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\Lambda}}{{\longrightarrow}}\left(\operatorname{Shv}\left(\underline{\mathbf{Spec}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}\left(\mathcal{A}\right)}\right),\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{A}}\right)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle t_{\mathcal{A}}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\left(\mathcal{S}\mskip-3.0mu\operatorname{pc},\mathcal{A}\right)

is equivalent to
t_{X}.

By Theorem
9.38
,
t_{\mathcal{A}}^{\star}=\mathbf{MSpec}_{\mathcal{A}}
is essentially surjective, so the essential image of
\Lambda^{\star}
is the same as the essential image of
\left(t_{\mathcal{A}}\circ\Lambda\right)^{\star}.
But this is the same as the essential image of
t_{X}^{\star}=\mathbf{MSpec}_{\mathcal{E}_{\mathfrak{Der}}/X}.

∎

Proposition 9.54
.

Let
\mathcal{X}\in\mathcal{E}_{\mathfrak{Der}}
be arbitrary and let
\mathcal{F}
be an
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}
-module. Then, for any
X=\mathbf{Spec}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}\left(\mathcal{A}\right)\in\mathscr{D}
(with
\mathcal{A}
complete) and
f:\mathbf{Spec}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}\left(\mathcal{A}\right)\to\mathcal{X},

\mathcal{F}\left(f\right)
is a complete
\mathcal{A}
-module.

Proof.

Note that
\mathcal{F}\left(f\right)
is equivalent to global sections of
f^{\star}\mathcal{F},
but this is the same as global sections of
\Lambda_{\star}f^{*}\mathcal{F}
over
\operatorname{Shv}\left(\underline{\mathbf{Spec}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}\left(\mathcal{A}\right)}\right),
and thus is contained in the essential image of

\left(t_{\mathcal{A}}\right)_{\star}:\mathbf{Mod}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{A}}}\to\mathbf{Mod}_{\mathcal{A}},

which is precisely the complete
\mathcal{A}
-modules.
∎

Corollary 9.55
.

Let
X=\mathbf{Spec}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}\left(\mathcal{A}\right).
Then if
\mathcal{F}\in\mathbf{QC}\left(X\right),
if

f:\mathbf{Spec}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}\left(\mathcal{B}\right)\to\mathbf{Spec}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}\left(\mathcal{A}\right)

is in
\mathscr{D},
then
\Lambda_{X}^{\star}\mathcal{F}\left(f\right)
is the complete
\mathcal{B}
-module

\mathcal{B}\underset{\mathcal{A}}{\widehat{\otimes}}\mathcal{F}\left(\mathbf{Spec}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}\left(\mathcal{A}\right)\right).

Proof.

By Theorem
9.53
,

\Lambda_{X}^{\star}\mathcal{F}\simeq\mathbf{MSpec}_{\mathcal{E}_{\mathfrak{Der}}/X}\left(\mathcal{F}\left(\mathbf{Spec}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}\left(\mathcal{A}\right)\right)\right).

Following the proof of Proposition
9.37
, for
M
any
\mathcal{A}
-module, we can identify
\mathbf{MSpec}_{\mathcal{E}_{\mathfrak{Der}}/X}\left(M\right)
with the sheafification over
\mathscr{D}/X
of the presheaf
\tilde{M}
that assigns each
f:\mathbf{Spec}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}\left(\mathcal{B}\right)\to X
the
\mathcal{B}
-module

\mathcal{B}\underset{\mathcal{A}}{\otimes}M.
The value of the sheafification of
\tilde{M}
on
f
can be identified with the global sections of the sheafification of the restriction of
f^{*}\tilde{M}
to the poset of open subsets of
\mathbf{Spec}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}\left(\mathcal{B}\right).
This is readily seen to be equivalent to the completed induced module

\mathcal{B}\underset{\mathcal{A}}{\widehat{\otimes}}M.

Applying this observation to
M=\mathcal{F}\left(\mathbf{Spec}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}\left(\mathcal{A}\right)\right)
yields the desired result.
∎

Proposition 9.56
.

Let
X=\mathbf{Spec}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}\left(\mathcal{A}\right),
then
\mathcal{F}\in\mathbf{Mod}_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}
is quasi-coherent if and only if for any
f:\mathbf{Spec}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}\left(\mathsf{B}\right)\to X
in
\mathscr{D},
the canonical map

\mathcal{B}\underset{\mathcal{A}}{\widehat{\otimes}}\mathcal{F}\left(X\right)\to\mathcal{F}\left(f\right)

is an equivalence.

Proof.

The module sheaf
\mathcal{F}
is quasi-coherent if and only if
\mathcal{F}\simeq\Lambda^{\star}_{X}\Lambda_{\star}*^{X}\mathcal{F}.
So, applying Corollary
9.55
to
\Lambda_{\star}\mathcal{F}
implies that if
\mathcal{F}
is quasi-coherent, the canonical map in the proposition is an equivalence.

Conversely, by Theorem
9.38
,
\rm{MSpec}_{\mathcal{A}}=t_{\mathcal{A}}^{\star}
is fully faithful, so the co-unit

t_{\mathcal{A}}^{\star}t^{\mathcal{A}}_{\star}\to id

is an equivalence. Moreover, recall from the proof of Theorem
9.53
that
t_{X}\simeq t_{\mathcal{A}}\circ\Lambda.
It follows that

\displaystyle t^{\star}_{X}t^{X}_{\star}

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle\left(t_{\mathcal{A}}\circ\Lambda\right)^{\star}\circ\left(t_{\mathcal{A}}\circ\Lambda\right)_{\star}

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle\Lambda^{\star}\circ t_{\mathcal{A}}^{\star}\circ t^{\mathcal{A}}_{\star}\circ\Lambda_{\star}

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle\Lambda^{\star}\Lambda_{\star}.

Thus,

\Lambda^{\star}_{X}\Lambda_{\star}^{X}\simeq\rm{MSpec}\left(\mathcal{F}\left(X\right)\right).

By the proof of Corollary
9.55
, for any
f:\mathbf{Spec}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}\left(\mathsf{B}\right)\to X
in
\mathscr{D},
we have

\rm{MSpec}\left(\mathcal{F}\left(X\right)\right)\left(f\right)\simeq\mathcal{B}\underset{\mathcal{A}}{\widehat{\otimes}}\mathcal{F}\left(X\right).

The result now follows.
∎

Lemma 9.57
.

Let
\left(\mathcal{X},\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}\right)
be an
{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}
-Deligne-Mumford stack and let
Y\left(\mathcal{X}\right)\in\mathcal{E}_{\mathfrak{Der}}
be its functor of points. Then

\mathbf{QC}\left(Y\left(\mathcal{X}\right)\right)\simeq\mathbf{Mod}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}}.

Proof.

By Lemma
8.17
, there exists a unique colimit preserving functor

\mathcal{E}_{\mathfrak{Der}}\to\mathfrak{Top}_{\infty}^{{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}}

which sends
Y\left(\mathcal{X}\right)
to
\left(\mathcal{X},\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}\right)
for all
{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}
-Deligne-Mumford stacks
\left(\mathcal{X},\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}\right).
For any such
\mathcal{X},

the composite

\mathcal{X}^{op}\simeq\left(\mathfrak{DM}_{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}^{\acute{e}t}/\mathcal{X}\right)^{op}\to\left(\mathfrak{DM}_{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}\right)^{op}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{E}_{\mathfrak{Der}}^{op}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathbf{Mod}}}{{-\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu-\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu-\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu-\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu-\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu-\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu\longrightarrow}}\widehat{\mathsf{Cat}_{\infty}}

preserves limits as it can be identified with
\mathbf{Mod}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}},
which is a sheaf on
\mathcal{X}.
So the assignment

\left(\mathcal{X},\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}\right)\mapsto\mathbf{Mod}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}}

is a sheaf on
\mathfrak{DM}_{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}.
By
[
7
, Lemma 3.1]
, the restriction functor

\operatorname{Shv}\left(\mathfrak{DM}_{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}\right)\to\operatorname{Shv}\left(\mathscr{D}\right)=\mathcal{E}_{\mathfrak{Der}}

is an equivalence, and the restriction of this sheaf agrees with
\mathbf{QC}
on affine
{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}
-schemes. The result now follows.
∎

By Lemma
9.45
, the functor

\mathbf{Mod}_{\mathcal{O}}:\mathcal{E}_{\mathfrak{Der}}^{op}\to\widehat{\mathsf{Cat}_{\infty}}

C sending
E\in\mathcal{E}_{\mathfrak{Der}}
to
\mathbf{Mod}_{\mathcal{O}_{E}}
is a sheaf. Moreover,
\mathbf{QC}
is a sheaf by definition. Both of these functors take values in presentable
\infty
-categories, and send morphisms to accessible colimit-preserving functors. It follows from
[
22
, Proposition 5.5.3.13]
that they both lift to sheaves with values in
\mathfrak{Pr}^{\mathsf{L}}
— the
\infty
-category of presentable
\infty
-categories and accessible colimit-preserving functors.

Proposition 9.58
.

There is a canonical morphism

\Lambda^{\star}:\mathbf{QC}\to\mathbf{Mod}_{\mathcal{O}}

in
\operatorname{Shv}\left(\mathcal{E}_{\mathfrak{Der}},\mathfrak{Pr}^{\mathsf{L}}\right)
which restricts to
\Lambda_{X}^{\star}
for all
X\in\mathscr{D}.
Moreover, for any object
E\in\mathcal{E}_{\mathfrak{Der}},

\Lambda_{E}^{\star}
is fully faithful and thus
\mathbf{QC}\left(E\right)
can be realized as a coreflective subcategory of
\mathbf{Mod}_{\mathcal{O}_{E}}
.

Proof.

By Lemma
8.18
, there is a colimit preserving functor

\mathcal{E}_{\mathfrak{Der}}\to\operatorname{Fun}\left(\Delta\left[1\right],\mathfrak{Top}_{\infty}^{{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}}\right)

which sends each affine element
X=\mathbf{Spec}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}\left(\mathcal{A}\right)\in\mathscr{D},
to

\Lambda_{X}:\left(\mathcal{E}_{\mathfrak{Der}}/X,\mathcal{O}_{X}\right)\to\left(\operatorname{Shv}\left(\underline{\mathbf{Spec}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}\left(\mathcal{A}\right)}\right),\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{A}}\right).

Composition with the functor

\mathbf{Mod}:\left(\mathfrak{Top}_{\infty}^{{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}}\right)^{op}\to\mathfrak{Pr}^{\mathsf{L}}

induces a functor

\mathcal{E}_{\mathfrak{Der}}^{op}\to\operatorname{Fun}\left(\Delta\left[1\right],\mathfrak{Pr}^{\mathsf{L}}\right),

which we can canonically identify with a functor

\mu:\mathcal{E}_{\mathfrak{Der}}^{op}\times\Delta\left[1\right]\to\mathfrak{Pr}^{\mathsf{L}},

in other words a natural transformation

\mu:\mu_{0}=\mu\left(\mspace{3mu}\cdot\mspace{3mu},0\right)\Rightarrow\mu\left(\mspace{3mu}\cdot\mspace{3mu},1\right)=\mu_{1}.

We will prove that
\mu_{0}=\mathbf{QC},

\mu_{1}=\mathbf{Mod}_{\mathcal{O}},
and that for all
X\in D,

\mu_{X}=\Lambda^{\star}_{X}.
The latter is true by construction, and in particular, for all
X\in D,

\mu_{0}\left(X\right)=\mathbf{QC}\left(X\right)
and
\mu_{1}\left(X\right)=\mathbf{Mod}_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}.

Thus, it suffices to prove that
\mu_{0}
and
\mu_{1}
are sheaves. However, this is automatic since by construction, both functors are limit preserving functors from
\mathcal{E}_{\mathfrak{Der}}^{op}
to
\mathfrak{Pr}^{\mathsf{L}}.
Thus
\Lambda^{\star}:=\mu
above is the desired morphism.

Now lets show that
\Lambda^{\star}
is component-wise fully faithful. Since both
\mathbf{QC}
and
\mathbf{Mod}
are sheaves, regarded as functors

\mathcal{E}_{\mathfrak{Der}}^{op}\to\widehat{\mathsf{Cat}_{\infty}},

they are limit preserving. Given any
E\in\mathcal{E}_{\mathfrak{Der}},
we can write

E\simeq\underset{D\to E}{\underrightarrow{\mbox{colim}\vskip 0.5pt}\mspace{4mu}}D,

with the colimit is indexed by
\mathscr{D}/E.
It follows that

\mathbf{QC}\left(E\right)\simeq\underset{E\to D}{\varprojlim\mspace{3mu}}\mathbf{QC}\left(D\right)

and

\mathbf{Mod}_{\mathcal{O}_{E}}\simeq\underset{E\to D}{\varprojlim\mspace{3mu}}\mathbf{Mod}_{\mathcal{O}_{D}}.

It is well-known that mapping spaces in limits of
\infty
-categories can be computed as limits of mapping spaces. Using this, the naturality of
\Lambda,
and the fact that
\Lambda_{D}^{\star}
is fully faithful for all
D\in\mathscr{D},
we have for any
\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G}\in\mathbf{QC}\left(E\right),
the following string of natural equivalences:

\displaystyle\mathbb{M}\mathrm{ap}_{\mathbf{QC}\left(E\right)}\left(\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G}\right)

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle\underset{D\stackrel{{\scriptstyle f}}{{\to}}E}{\varprojlim\mspace{3mu}}\mathbb{M}\mathrm{ap}_{\mathbf{QC}\left(D\right)}\left(f^{\star}\mathcal{F},f^{\star}\mathcal{G}\right)

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle\underset{D\stackrel{{\scriptstyle f}}{{\to}}E}{\varprojlim\mspace{3mu}}\mathbb{M}\mathrm{ap}_{\mathbf{Mod}_{\mathcal{O}_{D}}}\left(\Lambda_{D}^{\star}f^{\star}\mathcal{F},\Lambda_{D}^{\star}f^{\star}\mathcal{G}\right)

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle\underset{D\stackrel{{\scriptstyle f}}{{\to}}E}{\varprojlim\mspace{3mu}}\mathbb{M}\mathrm{ap}_{\mathbf{Mod}_{\mathcal{O}_{D}}}\left(f^{\star}\Lambda_{E}^{\star}\mathcal{F},f^{\star}\Lambda_{E}^{\star}\mathcal{G}\right)

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle\mathbb{M}\mathrm{ap}_{\mathbf{Mod}_{\mathcal{O}_{E}}}\left(\Lambda_{E}^{\star}\mathcal{F},\Lambda_{E}^{\star}\mathcal{G}\right).

Moreover, since
\Lambda_{E}^{\star}
is a morphism in
\mathfrak{Pr}^{\mathsf{L}},
it has a right adjoint
\Lambda^{E}_{\star},
so
\mathbf{QC}\left(E\right)
is coreflective in
\mathbf{Mod}_{\mathcal{O}_{E}}.

∎

Remark 9.59
.

Given that for any stack
\mathcal{X},
the functor

\Lambda_{\mathcal{X}}^{\star}:\mathbf{QC}\left(\mathcal{X}\right)\hookrightarrow\mathbf{Mod}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}}

is fully faithful, we will speak of an
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}
-module
\mathcal{F}
as
being
quasicoherent if it’s in the essential image of
\Lambda^{\star}_{\mathcal{X}}.

The fact that
\Lambda
is a natural transformations means that for any morphism
f:\mathcal{X}\to\mathcal{Y}
of derived stacks, there is a commutative diagram

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 30.23132pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-19.11108pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathbf{QC}\left(\mathcal{X}\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 19.11108pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@hook{1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 46.23946pt\raise 6.17209pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.17209pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\Lambda_{\mathcal{X}}^{\star}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 90.24297pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 90.24297pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathbf{Mod}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}}}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern-19.11108pt\raise-32.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathbf{QC}\left(\mathcal{Y}\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-30.23132pt\raise-16.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.75pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{QC}\left(f\right)}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-8.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 19.11108pt\raise-32.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@hook{1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 46.23946pt\raise-25.82791pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.17209pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\Lambda_{\mathcal{Y}}^{\star}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 90.24297pt\raise-32.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 90.24297pt\raise-32.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathbf{Mod}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{Y}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 110.9513pt\raise-16.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.99155pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{f^{\star}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 110.9513pt\raise-8.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces,

with the horizontal functors fully faithful. This justifies our abuse of notation in writing
\mathbf{QC}\left(f\right)
as
f^{\star}.

We warn that
f_{\star}

need not
send quasi-coherent sheaves, to quasi-coherent sheaves. Nonetheless,
\mathbf{QC}\left(f\right)
does admit a right adjoint.

Lemma 9.60
.

Let
R:\mathscr{C}\hookrightarrow\mathscr{D}
be a reflective subcategory of an
\infty
-category
\mathscr{D},
with reflector
L,
and suppose that
\tilde{R}:\widetilde{\mathscr{C}}\hookrightarrow\widetilde{\mathscr{D}}
is a reflective subcategory with reflector
\tilde{L}.
Suppose further that there is a commutative diagram of functors

\textstyle{\widetilde{\mathscr{C}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{F}
\scriptstyle{\tilde{R}}
\textstyle{\widetilde{\mathscr{D}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{G}
\textstyle{\mathscr{C}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{R}
\textstyle{\mathscr{D},}

and that
G
has a left adjoint
H
. Then the composite
\tilde{L}\circ H\circ R
is left adjoint to
F.

Proof.

Let
C
be an element of
\mathscr{C}
and
Y
an element of
\widetilde{\mathscr{C}}.
Then we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{M}\mathrm{ap}_{\widetilde{\mathscr{C}}}\left(\tilde{L}HR\left(C\right),Y\right)

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle\mathbb{M}\mathrm{ap}_{\widetilde{\mathscr{D}}}\left(HR\left(C\right),\tilde{R}\left(Y\right)\right)

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle\mathbb{M}\mathrm{ap}_{\mathscr{D}}\left(R\left(C\right),G\tilde{R}\left(Y\right)\right)

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle\mathbb{M}\mathrm{ap}_{\mathscr{D}}\left(R\left(C\right),RF\left(Y\right)\right)

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle\mathbb{M}\mathrm{ap}_{\mathscr{C}}\left(C,F\left(Y\right)\right),

with the last equivalence following since
R
is fully faithful.
∎

Remark 9.61
.

By the dual of Lemma
9.60
, we conclude that for any morphism
f:\mathcal{X}\to\mathcal{Y}
in
\mathcal{E}_{\mathfrak{Der}},
the functor

f^{\star}:\mathbf{QC}\left(\mathcal{Y}\right)\to\mathbf{QC}\left(\mathcal{X}\right)

has a right adjoint
f^{\mathbf{QC}}_{\star}.
Explicitly, it is given by
\Lambda^{\mathcal{Y}}_{\star}\circ f_{\star}\circ\Lambda_{\mathcal{X}}^{\star}.

Lemma 9.62
.

Let
\mathcal{X}\in\mathcal{E}_{\mathfrak{Der}}
and
\mathcal{F}\in\mathbf{Mod}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}}.
Then
\mathcal{F}
is quasi-coherent if and only if for all
\varphi:\mathbf{Spec}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}\left(\mathcal{A}\right)\to\mathcal{X}
with
\mathbf{Spec}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}\left(\mathcal{A}\right)\in\mathscr{D},

\varphi^{\star}\mathcal{F}
is quasi-coherent.

Proof.

Since we have equivalences

\mathbf{Mod}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}}\simeq\underset{f:D\to\mathcal{X}}{\varprojlim\mspace{3mu}}\mathbf{Mod}_{\mathcal{O}_{D}}

\mathbf{QC}\left(\mathcal{X}\right)\simeq\underset{f:D\to\mathcal{X}}{\varprojlim\mspace{3mu}}\mathbf{QC}\left(D\right),

we can think of an
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}
-module
\mathcal{G}
as a family of compatible
\mathcal{O}_{D}
-modules
\mathcal{G}_{f}
for each
f:D\to\mathcal{X},
satisfying homotopy coherence, and similarly we can think of a quasi-coherent sheaf
\mathcal{H}
over
\mathcal{X}
as a family of compatible quasi-coherent sheaves
\mathcal{H}_{f}
over
D
for each such
f.
Since we can view quasi-coherent sheaves over
D
as sitting inside
\mathcal{O}_{D}
-modules as a full subcategory, and the functor
f^{\star}
for
\mathcal{O}_{D}
-modules restricts to one between quasi-coherent sheaves, we see that when viewing
\mathcal{F}
as a family
\left(\mathcal{F}_{f}\right)_{f:D\to\mathcal{X}},

\mathcal{F}
being quasi-coherent is just the condition that each
\mathcal{F}_{f},
is. But under the equivalence

\mathbf{Mod}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}}\simeq\underset{f:D\to\mathcal{X}}{\varprojlim\mspace{3mu}}\mathbf{Mod}_{\mathcal{O}_{D}}

we have
\mathcal{F}_{f}\simeq f^{\star}\mathcal{F}.
This can be made formal by identifying
\mathbf{Mod}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}}
and
\mathbf{QC}\left(\mathcal{X}\right)
with the
\infty
-categories of Cartesian sections of the Cartesian fibrations

\int_{\mathscr{D}/\mathcal{X}}\pi^{*}_{\mathcal{X}}\mathbf{Mod}\to\mathscr{D}/\mathcal{X}

and

\int_{\mathscr{D}/\mathcal{X}}\pi^{*}_{\mathcal{X}}\mathbf{QC}\to\mathscr{D}/\mathcal{X}

respectively.

∎

Theorem 9.63
.

Let
\mathcal{X}\in\mathcal{E}_{\mathfrak{Der}}
be an arbitrary derived stack. Then the following conditions are equivalent for an
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}
-module
\mathcal{F}
:

i)

\mathcal{F}
is quasi-coherent (i.e. in the essential image of the fully-faithful functor
\Lambda^{\star}_{\mathcal{X}}
).

ii)

For all morphisms

\textstyle{\mathbf{Spec}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}\left(\mathcal{B}\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\varphi}
\scriptstyle{g}
\textstyle{\mathbf{Spec}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}\left(\mathcal{A}\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f}
\textstyle{\mathcal{X}}

in
\mathscr{D}/\mathcal{X},
the canonical map

\mathcal{B}\underset{\mathcal{A}}{\widehat{\otimes}}\mathcal{F}\left(f\right)\to\mathcal{F}\left(g\right)

is an equivalence.

(Note that by Proposition
9.54
,
\mathcal{F}
always takes values in complete modules, and above we are using the completed induced module functor
\mathcal{B}\underset{\mathcal{A}}{\widehat{\otimes}}\left(\mspace{3mu}\cdot\mspace{3mu}\right).
)

Proof.

Suppose that
\mathcal{F}=\Lambda_{\mathcal{X}}^{\star}\mathcal{G}
is a quasi-coherent sheaf on
\mathcal{X}
. Then

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}\left(g\right)

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}\left(f\circ\varphi\right)

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle\Gamma_{\mathbf{Spec}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}\left(\mathcal{B}\right)}\left(\varphi^{\star}\left(f^{\star}\mathcal{F}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle\left(t_{\mathcal{B}}\right)_{\star}\Lambda^{\mathbf{Spec}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}\left(\mathcal{B}\right)}_{\star}\left(\varphi^{\star}\left(f^{\star}\mathcal{F}\right)\right)\in\widehat{\mathbf{Mod}}_{\mathcal{B}}.

Under the identification

\widehat{\mathbf{Mod}}_{\mathcal{B}}\simeq\mathbf{QC}\left(\mathbf{Spec}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}\left(\mathcal{B}\right)\right),

the complete
\mathcal{B}
-module on the last line above corresponds to

\displaystyle\Lambda^{\mathbf{Spec}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}\left(\mathcal{B}\right)}_{\star}\left(\varphi^{\star}\left(f^{\star}\mathcal{F}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle\Lambda^{\mathbf{Spec}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}\left(\mathcal{B}\right)}_{\star}\left(\varphi^{\star}\left(f^{\star}\Lambda_{\mathcal{X}}^{\star}\mathcal{G}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle\Lambda^{\mathbf{Spec}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}\left(\mathcal{B}\right)}_{\star}\left(\varphi^{\star}\left(\Lambda_{\mathbf{Spec}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}\left(\mathcal{A}\right)}^{\star}f^{\star}\mathcal{G}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle\Lambda^{\mathbf{Spec}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}\left(\mathcal{B}\right)}_{\star}\Lambda_{\mathbf{Spec}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}\left(\mathcal{B}\right)}^{\star}\varphi^{\star}f^{\star}\mathcal{G}

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle\varphi^{\star}f^{\star}\mathcal{G}.

Switching back to complete modules, the quasi-coherent sheaf
f^{\star}\mathcal{G}
on
\mathbf{Spec}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}\left(\mathcal{A}\right)
is identified with its global sections, which is nothing but the complete
\mathcal{A}
-module
\mathcal{F}\left(f\right).
The result now follows since the operation
\varphi^{\star}
on quasi-coherent sheaves corresponds to the complete induced module construction, by Remark
9.48
.

Conversely, notice that condition
ii)
is equivalent to stating that for all
f:D\to\mathcal{X},

f^{\star}
satisfies the necessary and sufficient conditions of Proposition
9.56
for quasi-coherence. Hence, we are done by Lemma
9.62
.
∎

10.
The de Rham Stack and Formal Neighborhoods

Definition 10.1
.

Given
\mathcal{X}\in\mathcal{E}_{\mathfrak{Der}},
its
de Rham stack

\mathcal{X}_{dR}
is
k_{*}k^{*}\mathcal{X}.
Note that the unit of the adjunction
k^{*}\dashv k_{*}
furnishes a canonical map

\eta_{\mathcal{X}}:\mathcal{X}\to\mathcal{X}_{dR}.

Lets unwind this definition a bit:

\displaystyle\mathcal{X}_{dR}\left(\mathbf{Spec}_{C^{\infty}}A\right)

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle\mathbb{M}\mathrm{ap}\left(y\left(\mathbf{Spec}_{C^{\infty}}A\right),k_{*}k^{*}\mathcal{X}\right)

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle\mathbb{M}\mathrm{ap}\left(k^{*}y\left(\mathbf{Spec}_{C^{\infty}}A\right),k^{*}\mathcal{X}\right)

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle\mathbb{M}\mathrm{ap}\left(p_{!}y\left(\mathbf{Spec}_{C^{\infty}}A\right),k^{*}\mathcal{X}\right),

and since
p_{!}\simeq\operatorname{Lan}_{y}\left(y_{red}\circ p\right),
we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{X}_{dR}\left(\mathbf{Spec}_{C^{\infty}}A\right)

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle\mathbb{M}\mathrm{ap}\left(y_{red}\left(p\left(\mathbf{Spec}_{C^{\infty}}A\right)\right),k^{*}\mathcal{X}\right)

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle k^{*}\mathcal{X}\left(p\left(\mathbf{Spec}_{C^{\infty}}A\right)\right)

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle\mathcal{X}\left(kp\left(\mathbf{Spec}_{C^{\infty}}A\right)\right).

In other words, the de Rham stack
\mathcal{X}_{dR}
’s value on
\mathbf{Spec}_{C^{\infty}}
of an algebra
A
is the same as the value of
\mathcal{X}
on
\mathbf{Spec}_{C^{\infty}}
of the reduction of
A.

As a consequence, the functor

\left(\mspace{3mu}\cdot\mspace{3mu}\right)_{dR}:\mathcal{E}_{\mathfrak{Der}}\to\mathcal{E}_{\mathfrak{Der}}

is idempotent, i.e. there is a canonical equivalence

\left(\mspace{3mu}\cdot\mspace{3mu}\right)_{dR}\circ\left(\mspace{3mu}\cdot\mspace{3mu}\right)_{dR}\simeq\left(\mspace{3mu}\cdot\mspace{3mu}\right)_{dR}.

Remark 10.2
.

The functor
\left(\mspace{3mu}\cdot\mspace{3mu}\right)_{dR}:=k_{*}k^{*}
is a comonad. It moreover has both a left and right adjoint given by
k_{!}k^{*}
and
p^{*}p_{*}
respectively. Consequently, it preserves all small limits and colimits.

10.1.
Formal completions and formal neighborhoods

Definition 10.3
.

Let
f:\mathcal{Y}\to\mathcal{X}
be a map in
\mathcal{E}_{\mathfrak{Der}}.
The
formal completion of
\mathcal{Y}
along
f
,
denoted by
\widehat{\mathcal{Y}}_{f},
is the pullback

\textstyle{\widehat{\mathcal{Y}}_{f}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\hat{f}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{Y}_{dR}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f_{dR}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{X}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\eta_{\mathcal{X}}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{X}_{dR}.}

The naturality square for
\eta
yields a canonical map
\iota_{f}:\mathcal{Y}\to\widehat{\mathcal{Y}}_{f}
factorizing
f
as

\mathcal{Y}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\iota_{f}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\widehat{\mathcal{Y}}_{f}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\hat{f}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathcal{X}.

Viewing
\hat{f}
as a generalized point, the map

\hat{f}:\widehat{\mathcal{Y}}_{f}\to\mathcal{X}

is defined to be the
formal neighborhood of
\mathcal{X}
at
f
, for which we introduce another notation

\mathcal{X}_{\left(\mkern-3.0mu\left(f\right)\mkern-3.0mu\right)}=\widehat{\mathcal{Y}}_{f}.

Proposition 10.4
.

The canonical map

\left(\iota_{f}\right)_{dR}:\mathcal{Y}_{dR}\to\left(\widehat{\mathcal{Y}}_{f}\right)_{dR}

is an equivalence.

Proof.

This follows formally from the fact that the de Rham functor is idempotent and preserves limits:

\displaystyle\left(\widehat{\mathcal{Y}}_{f}\right)_{dR}

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle\left(\mathcal{X}\times_{\mathcal{X}_{dR}}\mathcal{Y}\right)_{dR}

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle\mathcal{X}_{dR}\times_{\left(\mathcal{X}_{dR}\right)_{dR}}\mathcal{Y}_{dR}

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle\mathcal{X}_{dR}\times_{\mathcal{X}_{dR}}\mathcal{Y}_{dR}

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle\mathcal{Y}_{dR}

∎

The following proposition follows by the universal property of pullback diagrams together with the fact that the de Rham functor preserves finite products:

Proposition 10.5
.

Let
f=\left(f_{0},f_{1}\right):\mathcal{Y}\to\mathcal{X}_{0}\times\mathcal{X}_{1}.
Then the canonical commutative diagram

\textstyle{\widehat{\mathcal{Y}}_{\left(f_{0},f_{1}\right)}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\widehat{\mathcal{Y}}_{f_{0}}\times\widehat{\mathcal{Y}}_{f_{1}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{Y}_{dR}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Delta}
\textstyle{\mathcal{Y}_{dR}\times\mathcal{Y}_{dR},}

is a pullback diagram.

The following corollary is a special case of Proposition
10.5
:

Corollary 10.6
.

Let
x=\left(x_{0},x_{1}\right):*\to\mathcal{X}_{0}\times\mathcal{X}_{1}
be a geometric point. Then

\left(\mathcal{X}_{0}\times\mathcal{X}_{1}\right)_{\left(\mkern-3.0mu\left(x\right)\mkern-3.0mu\right)}\simeq\left(\mathcal{X}_{0}\right)_{\left(\mkern-3.0mu\left(x_{0}\right)\mkern-3.0mu\right)}\times\left(\mathcal{X}_{1}\right)_{\left(\mkern-3.0mu\left(x_{1}\right)\mkern-3.0mu\right)}.

Definition 10.7
.

Let
k
be a non-negative integer. The
k
-dimensional formal disk
is the colimit

\mathbb{D}^{k}:=\underset{n}{\underrightarrow{\mbox{colim}\vskip 0.5pt}\mspace{4mu}}\mathbf{Spec}_{C^{\infty}}\left(C^{\infty}\left(t_{1},t_{2},\ldots,t_{k}\right)/\left(t_{1},t_{2},\ldots,t_{k}\right)^{n}\right),

where the colimit is taken in
\mathcal{E}_{\mathfrak{Der}}.

Definition 10.8
.

Let
\mathcal{A}
be an
{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}
-algebra. Let
k
be a non-negative integer. Let
N_{k}\subset\mathbf{Nil}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}\left(\pi_{0}\left(\mathcal{A}\right)\right)
be the subset of nilpotent elements of degree
k
. Define

\mathbf{Nil}_{k}\left(\mathcal{A}\right):=\mathcal{A}\times_{\pi_{0}\mathcal{A}}N_{k}

be the
space of nilpotent elements of degree
k
of
\mathcal{A}
, and let

\mathbf{Nil}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}\left(\mathcal{A}\right):=\mathcal{A}\times_{\pi_{0}\mathcal{A}}\mathbf{Nil}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}\left(\pi_{0}\mathcal{A}\right)

be the
space of locally nilpotent elements of
\mathcal{A}
. Denote by

\mathbf{Nil}_{k}:\mathcal{D}^{op}\to\mathcal{S}\mskip-3.0mu\operatorname{pc}

the functor
\mathbf{Spec}_{C^{\infty}}\left(\mathcal{A}\right)\mapsto\mathbf{Nil}_{k}\left(\mathcal{A}\right),
and by

\mathbf{Nil}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}:\mathcal{D}^{op}\to\mathcal{S}\mskip-3.0mu\operatorname{pc}

the functor
\mathbf{Spec}_{C^{\infty}}\left(\mathcal{A}\right)\mapsto\mathbf{Nil}_{\mathbf{C}}^{\infty}\left(\mathcal{A}\right).

Lemma 10.9
.

The functor

\mathbf{Nil}_{k}:\mathcal{D}^{op}\to\mathcal{S}\mskip-3.0mu\operatorname{pc}

is representable by
\mathbf{Spec}_{C^{\infty}}\left(C^{\infty}\left(t\right)/t^{k}\right).

Proof.

Define
P
to be the pushout in
\mathbf{d}{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}\mathbf{Alg}

\textstyle{C^{\infty}\left(t\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{x^{k}}
\textstyle{C^{\infty}\left(t\right)/t=\mathbb{R}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{C^{\infty}\left(x\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{P.}

Clearly,
\mathbf{Spec}_{C^{\infty}}\left(P\right)
represents
\mathbf{Nil}_{k}.
It suffices to prove that
P\simeq C^{\infty}\left(t\right)/t^{k}.
Since
\pi_{0}
preserves colimits, we have that
\pi_{0}P\simeq C^{\infty}\left(t\right)/t^{k}.
The model structure of dg-
C^{\infty}
-algebras gives a presentation for
\mathbf{d}{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}\mathbf{Alg},
consequently, we can represent
P
as the derived tensor product with respect to this model structure. Consider the Koszul algebra
K\left(t\right)
associated to the element
t
in
C^{\infty}\left(t\right)
. Since the singleton
t
is a regular sequence,
K\left(t\right)
is acyclic, and
C^{\infty}\left(t\right)\to K\left(t\right)
is a quasi-free extension, hence a cofibration. Therefore the derived tensor product is simply
P=C^{\infty}\left(x\right)\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\mkern-13.73mu\resizebox{1305270}{540744}{$\phantom{x}{}_{\phantom{.}{}^{\infty}}$}$\crcr$\displaystyle\bigcirc$\crcr}}}}{\mathbin{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\mkern-13.73mu\resizebox{1305270}{540744}{$\phantom{x}{}_{\phantom{.}{}^{\infty}}$}$\crcr$\textstyle\bigcirc$\crcr}}}}{\mathbin{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\mkern-13.73mu\resizebox{1305270}{540744}{$\phantom{x}{}_{\phantom{.}{}^{\infty}}$}$\crcr$\scriptstyle\bigcirc$\crcr}}}}{\mathbin{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-13.73mu\resizebox{1305270}{540744}{$\phantom{x}{}_{\phantom{.}{}^{\infty}}$}$\crcr$\scriptscriptstyle\bigcirc$\crcr}}}}K\left(t\right)
which can be identified with the Koszul algebra of
C^{\infty}\left(x\right)
with respect to the element
x^{k}.
Since
x^{k}
is not a zero divisor, the canonical map
P\to C^{\infty}\left(x\right)/x^{k}
is a quasi-isomorphism.
∎

Corollary 10.10
.

\mathbf{Nil}_{k}
is a sheaf for all
k.

Proposition 10.11
.

\mathbf{Nil}_{\mathbf{C}}^{\infty}
is a sheaf and

\mathbf{Nil}_{\mathbf{C}}^{\infty}\simeq\underset{k}{\underrightarrow{\mbox{colim}\vskip 0.5pt}\mspace{4mu}}\mathbf{Nil}_{k}.

Proof.

It suffices to prove that the restriction of
\mathbf{Nil}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}
to the small site of
\mathbf{Spec}_{C^{\infty}}\left(\mathcal{A}\right)
is a sheaf for all
\mathcal{A}.
As in the proof of Lemma
3.21
,
\mathbf{Nil}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}\left(A\right)
can be identified with global sections over
\mathbf{Spec}_{C^{\infty}}\left(\mathcal{A}\right)
of
\mathbf{MSpec}\left(\mathbf{Nil}\left(\mathcal{A}\right)\right),
which is a sheaf. Hence
\mathbf{Nil}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}
is a sheaf, and in fact the sheafification of the presheaf
\mathbf{Nil}
assigning the space of genuinely nilpotent elements. Since

\mathbf{Nil}\left(A\right)\simeq\underset{k}{\underrightarrow{\mbox{colim}\vskip 0.5pt}\mspace{4mu}}\mathbf{Nil}_{k}\left(A\right),

and colimits in sheaves are computed by first computing them object-wise in presheaves and then sheafifying, the result follows.
∎

Corollary 10.12
.

The sheaf
\mathbf{Nil}_{\mathbf{C}}^{\infty}
is represented by
\mathbb{D}^{1}.

Lemma 10.13
.

Let
x:*\to\mathbb{R}
be a point in
\mathbb{R}.
Then the formal neighborhood around
x
is equivalent to
\mathbb{D}^{1}

Proof.

Firstly, since there is a diffeomorphism

\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}

carrying
x
to the origin, we can assume without loss of generality that
x
is the origin.

Notice that

\mathbb{M}\mathrm{ap}\left(\mathbf{Spec}_{C^{\infty}}\left(A\right),\mathbb{R}\right)\simeq A

and

\mathbb{M}\mathrm{ap}\left(\mathbf{Spec}_{C^{\infty}}\left(A\right),\mathbb{R}_{dR}\right)\simeq\pi_{0}\left(A\right)/\mathbf{Nil}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}\left(\pi_{0}A\right),

hence

\mathbb{M}\mathrm{ap}\left(\mathbf{Spec}_{C^{\infty}}\left(A\right),\mathbb{R}_{\left(\mkern-3.0mu\left(0\right)\mkern-3.0mu\right)}\right)
can be identified with the fiber over
0
of

A\to\pi_{0}\left(A\right)/\mathbf{Nil}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}\left(\pi_{0}A\right).

By pasting pullback diagrams

\textstyle{\mathbf{Nil}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}\left(A\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{A\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathbf{Nil}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}\left(\pi_{0}A\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\pi_{0}\left(A\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\pi_{0}\left(A\right)/\mathbf{Nil}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}\left(\pi_{0}A\right),}

we conclude that

\mathbb{M}\mathrm{ap}\left(\mathbf{Spec}_{C^{\infty}}\left(A\right),\mathbb{R}_{\left(\mkern-3.0mu\left(0\right)\mkern-3.0mu\right)}\right)\simeq\mathbf{Nil}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}\left(A\right).

The result now follows from Corollary
10.12
.
∎

Proposition 10.14
.

Let
x:*\to\mathbb{R}^{n}
be a point in
\mathbb{R}^{n}.
Then the formal neighborhood around
x
is equivalent to
\mathbb{D}^{n}
.

Proof.

By Corollary
10.6
, the formal neighborhood of
x
in
\mathbb{R}^{n}
is equivalent to
\left(\mathbb{D}^{1}\right)^{n}.
It therefore suffices to prove that

\left(\mathbb{D}^{1}\right)^{n}\simeq\mathbb{D}^{n}.

By Proposition
7.4
and the fact that
\mathcal{E}_{\mathfrak{Cl}}
is coreflective in
\mathcal{E}_{\mathfrak{Der}}
and hence closed under colimits, both
\left(\mathbb{D}^{1}\right)^{n}
and
\mathbb{D}^{n}
are in
\mathcal{E}_{\mathfrak{Cl}}.
Therefore, it suffices to show that

i^{*}\left(\mathbb{D}^{1}\right)^{n}\simeq i^{*}\mathbb{D}^{n}.

Let
\mathbf{Spec}_{C^{\infty}}\left(A\right)
be in
\mathscr{D}_{0}.
Then
A
is an ordinary
C^{\infty}
-ring. Hence, on one hand,

i^{*}\left(\mathbb{D}^{1}\right)^{n}\left(\mathbf{Spec}_{C^{\infty}}\left(A\right)\right)\cong\mathbf{Nil}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}\left(A\right)^{n}.

On the other hand
i^{*}\mathbb{D}^{n}
is the sheafification of the presheaf
F

A\mapsto\underset{k}{\underrightarrow{\mbox{colim}\vskip 0.5pt}\mspace{4mu}}\mathbb{H}\mathrm{om}\left(C^{\infty}\left(t_{1},t_{2},\ldots,t_{n}\right)/\left(t_{1},t_{2},\ldots,t_{n}\right)^{k},A\right).

Since
t_{i}^{k}\in\left(t_{1},t_{2},\ldots,t_{n}\right)^{k}
for all
i,
it follows that

F\left(\mathbf{Spec}_{C^{\infty}}\left(A\right)\right)\subseteq\mathbf{Nil}\left(A\right)^{n}.

Let us now show the reverse inclusion. Let
a_{1},a_{2},\ldots a_{n}\in\mathbf{Nil}\left(A\right)^{n}.
Suppose that
a_{i}
is nilpotent of order
n_{i}.
Let
N:=\sum_{i=1}^{n}n_{i}.
The ideal
\left(t_{1},t_{2},\ldots t_{n}\right)^{N}
is generated by the binomials of the form
t_{1}^{k_{1}}t_{2}^{k_{2}}\cdots t_{m}^{k_{m}},
where
k_{1}+k_{2}+\cdots+k_{m}=N.
Fix such a monomial. For a given
j,

k_{j}=N-\sum_{i\neq j}k_{i}.

So, if each
i\neq j,

k_{i}<n_{j},
then
k_{j}=n_{j}+\sum_{i\neq j}\left(n_{i}-k_{i}\right)>n_{j},
hence
a_{j}^{k_{j}}=0,
and consequently
a_{1}^{k_{1}}a_{2}^{k_{2}}\cdots a_{m}^{k_{m}}=0.
Otherwise, there exists
i\neq j
such that
k_{i}\geq n_{i},
and again we conclude that
a_{1}^{k_{1}}a_{2}^{k_{2}}\cdots a_{m}^{k_{m}}=0
. It follows that

\mathbf{Nil}\left(A\right)^{n}\subseteq\underset{k}{\underrightarrow{\mbox{colim}\vskip 0.5pt}\mspace{4mu}}\mathbb{H}\mathrm{om}\left(C^{\infty}\left(t_{1},t_{2},\ldots,t_{n}\right)/\left(t_{1},t_{2},\ldots,t_{n}\right)^{k},A\right)=F\left(\mathbf{Spec}_{C^{\infty}}\left(A\right)\right).

Since sheafification is left exact, the result follows.
∎

Definition 10.15
.

Let
n
and
k
be non-negative integers. The
k^{th}
order
\left(0|n\right)
-dimensional formal disk
is

\mathbb{D}^{0|n}_{\left(k\right)}:=\mathbf{Spec}_{C^{\infty}}\left(C^{\infty}\left(0;\eta_{1},\eta_{2},\ldots,\eta_{n}\right)/\left(\eta_{1},\eta_{2},\ldots,\eta_{n}\right)^{k}\right).

The
\left(0,n\right)
-dimensional formal disk
is the colimit

\mathbb{D}^{0|n}:=\underset{k}{\underrightarrow{\mbox{colim}\vskip 0.5pt}\mspace{4mu}}\mathbf{Spec}_{C^{\infty}}\left(C^{\infty}\left(0;\eta_{1},\eta_{2},\ldots,\eta_{n}\right)/\left(\eta_{1},\eta_{2},\ldots,\eta_{n}\right)^{k}\right),

where the colimit is taken in
\mathcal{E}_{\mathfrak{Der}}.

Remark 10.16
.

More generally, define the
k^{th}
order
\left(m|n\right)
-dimensional formal disk
as

\mathbb{D}^{m|n}_{\left(k\right)}:=\mathbf{Spec}_{C^{\infty}}\left(C^{\infty}\left(x_{1},\ldots,x_{m};\eta_{1},\eta_{2},\ldots,\eta_{n}\right)/\left(x_{1},\ldots,x_{m}\eta_{1},\eta_{2},\ldots,\eta_{n}\right)^{k}\right).

Then we have

\mathbb{D}^{m|n}_{\left(k\right)}\simeq\mathbb{D}^{m}_{\left(k\right)}\times\mathbb{D}^{0|n}_{\left(k\right)}.

Proposition 10.17
.

There is a canonical equivalence

\mathbb{D}^{0|n}\simeq\mathbb{R}^{0|n}.

Definition 10.18
.

For
n
and
m
non-negative integers, define the
\left(n|m\right)
-dimensional formal disk
to be

\mathbb{D}^{m|n}:=\mathbb{D}^{m}\times\mathbb{D}^{0|n}.

Proof.

Let
F
be an arbitrary object of
\mathcal{E}_{\mathfrak{Der}}.
Then we have

\mathbb{M}\mathrm{ap}\left(\mathbb{D}^{0|n},F\right)\simeq\underset{k}{\varprojlim\mspace{3mu}}\mathbb{M}\mathrm{ap}\left(\mathbb{D}^{0|n}_{\left(k\right)},F\right).

Notice that for all
k\geq n,

\mathbb{D}^{0|n}_{\left(k\right)}=\mathbb{R}^{0|n}.
Thus the diagram in the above limit has
\mathbb{M}\mathrm{ap}\left(\mathbb{R}^{0|n},F\right)
as an initial object, and thus the limit is
\mathbb{M}\mathrm{ap}\left(\mathbb{R}^{0|n},F\right).
We are done by the Yoneda lemma.
∎

Proposition 10.19
.

Let
\mathcal{A}
be a (complete)
{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}
-algebra such that
\mathcal{A}_{red}=\mathbb{R}.
Then

i)

\mathbb{M}\mathrm{ap}\left(*,\mathbf{Spec}_{C^{\infty}}\left(\mathcal{A}\right)\right)\simeq*,

ii)

\mathbf{Spec}_{C^{\infty}}\left(\mathcal{A}\right)_{dR}\simeq*,

iii)

The formal completion
\mathbf{Spec}_{C^{\infty}}\left(\mathcal{A}\right)_{\left(\left(x\right)\right)}
at the unique point
x
is equivalent to
\mathbf{Spec}_{C^{\infty}}\left(\mathcal{A}\right)
itself.

Proof.

Consider the following string of natural equivalences

\displaystyle\mathbb{M}\mathrm{ap}\left(*,\mathbf{Spec}_{C^{\infty}}\left(\mathcal{A}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle\mathbb{M}\mathrm{ap}\left(\mathbf{Spec}_{C^{\infty}}\left(\mathbb{R}\right),\mathbf{Spec}_{C^{\infty}}\left(\mathcal{A}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle\mathbb{M}\mathrm{ap}\left(\mathcal{A},\mathbb{R}\right)

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle\mathbb{M}\mathrm{ap}\left(\mathcal{A}_{red},\mathbb{R}\right)

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle\mathbb{M}\mathrm{ap}\left(\mathbb{R},\mathbb{R}\right)

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle*.

This establishes
i)

Let
\mathsf{B}
be an arbitrary (complete)
{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}
-ring in
\mathcal{D}
. Then we have the following natural equivalences

\displaystyle\mathbb{M}\mathrm{ap}\left(\mathbf{Spec}_{C^{\infty}}\left(\mathsf{B}\right),\mathbf{Spec}_{C^{\infty}}\left(\mathcal{A}\right)_{dR}\right)

\displaystyle\simeq
