\displaystyle\mathbb{M}\mathrm{ap}\left(\mathbf{Spec}_{C^{\infty}}\left(\mathsf{B}_{red}\right),\mathbf{Spec}_{C^{\infty}}\left(\mathcal{A}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle\mathbb{M}\mathrm{ap}\left(\mathcal{A},\mathsf{B}_{red}\right)

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle\mathbb{M}\mathrm{ap}\left(\mathcal{A}_{red},\mathsf{B}_{red}\right)

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle\mathbb{M}\mathrm{ap}\left(\mathbb{R},\mathsf{B}_{red}\right)

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle*.

It follows that
\mathbf{Spec}_{C^{\infty}}\left(\mathcal{A}\right)_{dR}
is the terminal object, establishing
ii).

Finally, we have that

\displaystyle\mathbf{Spec}_{C^{\infty}}\left(\mathcal{A}\right)_{\left(\left(x\right)\right)}

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle*\times_{\mathbf{Spec}_{C^{\infty}}\left(\mathcal{A}\right)_{dR}}\mathbf{Spec}_{C^{\infty}}\left(\mathcal{A}\right)

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle*\times_{*}\mathbf{Spec}_{C^{\infty}}\left(\mathcal{A}\right)

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle\mathbf{Spec}_{C^{\infty}}\left(\mathcal{A}\right),

which establishes
iii).

∎

Corollary 10.20
.

Let
x:*\to\mathbb{R}^{n|m}
be a geometric point. Then its formal neighborhood around
x
is equivalent to

\mathbb{D}^{m|n}=\mathbb{D}^{n}\times\mathbb{R}^{0|m}.

Proof.

The de Rham functor preserves all limits, hence

\left(\mathbb{R}^{n|m}\right)_{dR}\simeq\left(\mathbb{R}^{n}\right)_{dR}\times*\simeq\left(\mathbb{R}^{n}\right)_{dR}.

It follows that the canonical map

\mathbb{R}^{n|m}=\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\mathbb{R}^{0|m}\to\left(\mathbb{R}^{n|m}\right)_{dR}\times\left(\mathbb{R}^{n}\right)

factors as

\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\mathbb{R}^{0|m}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle pr_{1}}}{{-\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu-\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu-\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu-\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu-\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu-\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu\longrightarrow}}\mathbb{R}^{n}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eta}}{{\longrightarrow}}\left(\mathbb{R}^{n}\right)_{dR}.

By pasting pullback diagrams we get a diagram of pullbacks

\textstyle{\mathbb{D}^{n}\times\mathbb{R}^{0|m}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\mathbb{R}^{0|m}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathbb{D}^{n}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathbb{R}^{n}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\ast\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\left(\mathbb{R}^{n}\right)_{dR}.}

The bottom square is a pullback diagram by virtue of Proposition
10.14
.
∎

10.2.
Formally étale maps

Definition 10.21
.

A morphism
\varphi:\mathcal{Z}\to\mathcal{X}
in
\mathcal{E}_{\mathfrak{Der}}
is
formally étale
if
\varphi\simeq\hat{f}
for some
f:\mathcal{Y}\to\mathcal{X}.

Proposition 10.22
.

\varphi:\mathcal{Z}\to\mathcal{X}
in
\mathcal{E}_{\mathfrak{Der}}
is formally étale if and only if the naturality square

\textstyle{\mathcal{Z}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\varphi}
\scriptstyle{\eta_{\mathcal{Z}}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{Z}_{dR}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\varphi_{dR}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{X}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\eta_{\mathcal{X}}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{X}_{dR}}

is Cartesian.

Proof.

If the above diagram is a pullback square, it implies that
\varphi\simeq\hat{\varphi},
so
\varphi
is formally étale. Conversely, suppose that
\varphi=\hat{f},
for
f:\mathcal{Y}\to\mathcal{X}.
Let
\mathcal{Z}=\widehat{\mathcal{Y}}_{f}.
Since

\textstyle{\widehat{\mathcal{Y}}_{f}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\hat{f}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{Y}_{dR}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f_{dR}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{X}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\eta_{\mathcal{X}}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{X}_{dR}}

is a pullback diagram, and
\left(\mspace{3mu}\cdot\mspace{3mu}\right)_{dR}
is idempotent and preserves pullbacks, the two right most squares in the following diagram are pullbacks:

\textstyle{\widehat{\mathcal{Y}}_{f}=\mathcal{Z}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\hat{f}}
\scriptstyle{\eta_{\mathcal{Z}}}
\textstyle{\left(\widehat{\mathcal{Y}}_{f}\right)_{dR}=\mathcal{Z}_{dR}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\left(\hat{f}\right)_{dR}}
\textstyle{\left(\mathcal{Y}_{dR}\right)_{dR}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\left(f_{dR}\right)_{dR}}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\textstyle{\mathcal{Y}_{dR}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f_{dR}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{X}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\eta_{\mathcal{X}}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{X}_{dR}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\left(\eta_{\mathcal{X}}\right)_{dR}}
\textstyle{\left(\mathcal{X}_{dR}\right)_{dR}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\textstyle{\mathcal{X}_{dR}.}

The outermost square is also a pullback, by definition, hence so is the left most square.
∎

Lemma 10.23
.

Formally étale maps are stable under pullback.

Proof.

Let
f:X\to Y
be formally étale, and let
\varphi:Z\to Y.
We wish to show that
pr_{Z}:Z\times_{Y}X\to Z
is formally étale. Since
f
is formally étale, all squares in the following diagram are pullbacks:

\textstyle{Z\times_{Y}X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\eta_{X}}
\scriptstyle{f}
\textstyle{X_{dR}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f_{dR}}
\textstyle{Z\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\varphi}
\textstyle{Y\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\eta_{Y}}
\textstyle{Y_{dR}.}

By the naturality of
\eta,
this implies that all the squares in the following diagram are pullbacks:

\textstyle{Z\times_{Y}X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{Z_{dR}\times_{Y_{dR}}X_{dR}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{X_{dR}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{Z\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\eta_{Z}}
\textstyle{Z_{dR}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\varphi_{dR}}
\textstyle{Y_{dR}.}

Since
\left(\mspace{3mu}\cdot\mspace{3mu}\right)
preserves limits, we have
\left(Z\times_{Y}X\right)_{dR}\simeq Z_{dR}\times_{Y_{dR}}X_{dR}
and the morphism

Z_{dR}\times_{Y_{dR}}X_{dR}\to Z_{dR}

in the above diagram can be identified with
\left(pr_{Z}\right)_{dR}.
Hence
pr_{Z}
is formally étale.
∎

Lemma 10.24
.

The following are equivalent for a morphism
f:\mathcal{Y}\to\mathcal{X}
:

i)

f
is formally étale

ii)

for all
\varphi:D\to\mathcal{X}
with
D\in\mathscr{D},

\mathcal{Y}\times_{\mathcal{X}}D\to D
is formally étale.

Proof.

i)\Rightarrow ii)
follows from the fact that formally étale maps are stable under pullback (Lemma
10.23
). Conversely, suppose that
f
satisfies
ii).
We wish to show that the canonical map

\textstyle{\mathcal{Y}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\eta_{\mathcal{Y}}}
\scriptstyle{f}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{X}\times_{\mathcal{X}_{dR}}\mathcal{Y}_{dR}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{pr_{\mathcal{X}}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{Y}_{dR}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f_{dR}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{X}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\eta_{\mathcal{X}}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{X}_{dR},}

denoted by a dotted arrow above, is an equivalence. For this, it suffices to prove that for any
D\in\mathscr{D}
and
g:D\to\mathcal{X},
the induced map

\operatorname{hofib}_{g}\left(\mathcal{Y}\left(D\right)\to\mathcal{X}\left(D\right)\right)\to\operatorname{hofib}_{g}\left({\mathcal{X}\times_{\mathcal{X}_{dR}}\mathcal{Y}_{dR}}\left(D\right)\to\mathcal{X}\left(D\right)\right)

is an equivalence. This is equivalent to showing that the induced map

\mathbb{M}\mathrm{ap}_{\mathcal{E}_{\mathfrak{Der}}/\mathcal{X}}\left(g,f\right)\to\mathbb{M}\mathrm{ap}_{\mathcal{E}_{\mathfrak{Der}}/\mathcal{X}}\left(g,pr_{\mathcal{X}}\right)

is an equivalence.
Notice that

\mathbb{M}\mathrm{ap}_{\mathcal{E}_{\mathfrak{Der}}/\mathcal{X}}\left(g,f\right)\simeq\Gamma_{D}\left(\alpha:\mathcal{Y}\times_{\mathcal{X}}D\to D\right).

By hypothesis, this is sections of a formally étale map. This implies that the left most square in the following diagram is is a pullback,

\textstyle{\mathcal{Y}\times_{\mathcal{X}}D\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\alpha}
\textstyle{\mathcal{Y}_{dR}\times_{\mathcal{X}_{dR}}D_{dR}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\alpha_{dR}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{Y}_{dR}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f_{dR}}
\textstyle{D\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\eta_{D}}
\textstyle{D_{dR}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{g_{dR}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{X}_{dR}.}

Therefore all of the squares above are pullback squares. By the naturality of
\eta
, it follows all the squares in the following diagram are also pullbacks

\textstyle{\mathcal{Y}\times_{\mathcal{X}}D\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{X}\times_{\mathcal{X}_{dR}}\mathcal{Y}_{dR}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{pr_{\mathcal{X}}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{Y}_{dR}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f_{dR}}
\textstyle{D\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{g}
\textstyle{\mathcal{X}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\eta_{\mathcal{X}}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{X}_{dR}.}

Hence, the space of sections of
\alpha
can be canonically identified with
\mathbb{M}\mathrm{ap}_{\mathcal{E}_{\mathfrak{Der}}/\mathcal{X}}\left(g,pr_{\mathcal{X}}\right),
completing the proof.
∎

Proposition 10.25
.

If
f:X\to Y
is an étale map (a.k.a local diffeomorphism) of
{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}
-schemes, then
f
is formally étale.

Proof.

We want to show that the canonical map

X\to Y\times_{Y_{dR}}X_{dR}

is an equivalence. Let
Z
be any
{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}
-scheme. Then a morphism to the above fibered product is the same as a pair of morphisms making a commutative square

\textstyle{Z_{red}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{g}
\scriptstyle{\pi}
\textstyle{X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f}
\textstyle{Z\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{h}
\textstyle{Y.}

Since
f
is étale, we have that
f^{*}\mathcal{O}_{Y}\simeq\mathcal{O}_{X},
where here we mean the structure sheaf of the
{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}
-schemes as ringed spaces. Notice that
\pi
is the identity morphism on the underlying spaces of
Z
and
Z_{red}.
In particular, the spatial component of the map
h:Z\to Y
is fixed by the fact that we must have
\underline{h}=\underline{f}\circ\underline{g},
where the underline denotes the forgetful functor to the category of topological spaces. Since
f
is étale, this means that
h^{*}\mathcal{O}_{Y}\simeq g^{*}f^{*}\mathcal{O}_{Y}\simeq g^{*}\mathcal{O}_{X},
hence the morphism
h=\left(\underline{h},\alpha\right)
with
\alpha:h^{*}\mathcal{O}_{Y}\to\mathcal{O}_{Z},
gives rise to a morphism

g^{\prime}:=\left(g,\alpha\right):Z\to X

such that
f\circ g\simeq h
and
g^{\prime}\circ\pi\simeq g.
So,
g^{\prime}
determines the pair
\left(g,h\right)
up to a contractible space of choices. It follows that the induced map

\mathbb{M}\mathrm{ap}\left(Z,X\right)\to\mathbb{M}\mathrm{ap}\left(Y\times_{Y_{dR}}X_{dR}\right)

is an equivalence.
∎

Corollary 10.26
.

Any representable étale map is formally étale.

Proof.

Let
f:\mathcal{X}\to\mathcal{Y}
be a representable étale map. Then, for any
D\in\mathscr{D}
and
\varphi:D\to\mathcal{Y},
we have that
\mathcal{X}\times_{\mathcal{Y}}D
is an étale map of
{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}
-schemes, hence formally étale by Proposition
10.25
. The result now follows from Lemma
10.24
.
∎

Proposition 10.27
.

A morphism
f:\mathcal{M}\to\mathcal{N}
of supermanifolds is formally étale if and only if it is a local diffeomorphism.

Proof.

If
f
is a local diffeomorphism, then it is étale, and hence formally étale by Proposition
10.25
. Conversely, suppose that
f:\mathcal{M}\to\mathcal{N}
is a formally étale map of supermanifolds. To show that
f
is a local diffeomorphism, it suffices to show that for each point
x\in\mathcal{M},
the tangent map

Tf_{x}:T_{x}\mathcal{M}\to T_{x}\mathcal{N}

is an isomorphism of super vector spaces. Let
D
be either
\mathbb{D}_{\left(1\right)}
or
\mathbb{D}^{0|1}.
In either case, for any
\mathcal{X},

\mathcal{X}_{dR}\left(D\right)\simeq\mathcal{X}\left(*\right).

Since
f
is formally étale, there is a pullback diagram

\textstyle{\mathcal{M}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f}
\scriptstyle{\eta_{\mathcal{M}}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{M}_{dR}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f_{dR}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{N}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\eta_{\mathcal{N}}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{N}_{dR}.}

Then, for
x\in\mathcal{M}
of the induced map between the fiber over
x
of the map

\mathbb{M}\mathrm{ap}\left(D,\mathcal{M}\right)\to\mathbb{M}\mathrm{ap}\left(*,\mathcal{M}\right)

with the fiber over
f\left(x\right)
of the map

\mathbb{M}\mathrm{ap}\left(D,\mathcal{N}\right)\to\mathbb{M}\mathrm{ap}\left(*,\mathcal{N}\right)

is a bijection. But these maps can be identified with the even/odd components of
Tf_{x}
when
D=\mathbb{D}_{\left(1\right)}
and
D=\mathbb{D}^{0|1}
respectively.
∎

10.3.
Formal smoothness

Definition 10.28
.

An object
\mathcal{X}\in\mathcal{E}_{\mathfrak{Der}}
is
formally smooth
if the unit map

\eta_{\mathcal{X}}:\mathcal{X}\to\mathcal{X}_{dR}

to the de Rham stack is an epimorphism.

Lemma 10.29
.

Suppose that
\left(X_{\alpha}\right)_{\alpha}
is a collection of formally smooth objects. Then the coproduct
\underset{\alpha}{\coprod}X_{\alpha}
is also formally smooth.

Proof.

Recall from Definition
10.1
and Section
7.2
that

\left(\mspace{3mu}\cdot\mspace{3mu}\right)_{dR}=k_{*}k^{*}\simeq\left(kp\right)^{*}.

It follows that the de Rham functor is a left adjoint, and hence preserves coproducts. The result now follows.
∎

Lemma 10.30
.

If
p:\mathcal{Y}\to\mathcal{X}
is an epimorphism and
\mathcal{Y}
is formally smooth, then so is
\mathcal{X}.

Proof.

Since the de Rham functor is a left adjoint, it preserves epimorphisms. Consider the naturality square for
p

\textstyle{\mathcal{Y}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\eta_{\mathcal{Y}}}
\scriptstyle{p}
\textstyle{\mathcal{Y}_{dR}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{p_{dR}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{X}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\eta_{\mathcal{X}}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{X}_{dR}.}

It follows from
[
22
, Corollary 6.2.3.12]
that
\eta_{\mathcal{X}}
is also an epimorphism.
∎

Proposition 10.31
.

For all
\left(n,m\right)

\mathbb{R}^{\left(n,m\right)}
is formally smooth.

Proof.

The map
\eta:\mathbb{R}^{\left(n,m\right)}\to\mathbb{R}^{\left(n,m\right)}_{dR}
is an epimorphism if and only if any map
f:D\to\mathbb{R}^{\left(n,m\right)}_{dR}
with
D\in\mathscr{D}
locally factors through
\eta.
We will prove something stronger, namely that any such maps globally factors through
\eta.
Let
D=\mathbf{Spec}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}\left(\mathcal{A}\right).
The map
f
corresponds to a homomorphism

f:{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}\left(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n};\zeta_{1},\ldots,\zeta_{m}\right)\to\mathcal{A}_{red}.

As the algebra
{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}\left(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n};\zeta_{1},\ldots,\zeta_{m}\right)
is free, this corresponds to picking
n
even elements of
\mathcal{A}_{red}
and
m
odd ones, but
\mathcal{A}_{red}
is purely even so this is the same as picking
n
elements. We want a lift of this map to
\mathcal{A},
but this is easy— just pick
n
even elements of
\mathcal{A}
whose class in
\mathcal{A}_{red}
agree with the chosen ones, and pick
m
arbitrary odd elements of
\mathcal{A}.

∎

Corollary 10.32
.

If
\mathcal{M}
is a supermanifold, then it is formally smooth.

11.
Group objects

Here, we briefly recall the notion of a group object in an
\infty
-topos as introduced in
[
36
]
. We claim no originality for the ideas.

Definition 11.1
.

A
classifying stack for a group object
in an
\infty
-topos
\mathcal{X}
is an object
G
together with an effective epimorphism

\pi_{X}:*\to X

from the terminal object
*.
The
group object
associated to this classifying stack is
G:=\Omega_{*}\left(X\right).

Remark 11.2
.

If
H
is a Lie group, then
\pi:*\to\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu\to\mathcal{B}H
is an effective epimorphism in
\mathcal{E}_{\mathfrak{Der}},
making it a classifying stack for a group object. Notice that

\Omega_{*}\left(\mathcal{B}H\right)\simeq H.

Moreover, we claim that the group structure on
H
is completely encoded by the fact that
H
can be identified with the based loop object on
\mathcal{B}H.
Indeed, the Čech nerve of
\pi
is a simplicial object

\mbox{\v{C}}_{\pi}:\Delta^{op}\to\mathcal{E}_{\mathfrak{Der}}

with
\mbox{\v{C}}_{n}=H^{n},
and
d_{1}:H\times H\to H
can be identified with group multiplication.

More generally, for any group object
G,
the Čech nerve of
\pi_{G}
produces a simplicial object

\mbox{\v{C}}_{\pi_{G}}:\Delta^{op}\to\mathcal{X}

with
\left(\mbox{\v{C}}_{\pi_{X}}\right)_{0}=*.
This is the usual definition of a group object given in
[
22
, Definition 7.2.2.1]
. Conversely, the if

\mathcal{G}:\Delta^{op}\to\mathcal{X}

is a group object in Lurie’s sense,
\mathcal{G}
is equivalent to the Čech nerve of
*=\mathcal{G}_{0}\to\underrightarrow{\mbox{colim}\vskip 0.5pt}\mspace{4mu}\mathcal{G},
so the two definitions agree.

Notice since
\pi_{G}:*\to\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu\to X
is an effective epimorphism,
X
is equivalent to the colimit of the Čech nerve of
\pi_{G}.
Since
\Omega_{*}\left(X\right)=G,
we have

X\simeq\underset{n\in\Delta^{op}}{\underrightarrow{\mbox{colim}\vskip 0.5pt}\mspace{4mu}}\pi_{X}=\underrightarrow{\mbox{colim}\vskip 0.5pt}\mspace{4mu}\left(\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 5.5pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-5.5pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ast}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 29.5pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces G}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 5.50002pt\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 5.50002pt\raise-2.15277pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 67.36249pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{G\times G\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 43.36249pt\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 43.36249pt\raise-2.15277pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 43.36249pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 125.3096pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces G\times G\times G\cdots}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 101.3096pt\raise 3.01385pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 101.3096pt\raise 1.07639pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 101.3096pt\raise-1.07639pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 101.3096pt\raise-3.01385pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\right)

I.e.
X\simeq\mathcal{B}G
— the classifying stack of principal
G
-bundles.

Moreover, notice that, just as for a Lie group, the structure of the simplicial object
\mbox{\v{C}}_{\pi_{G}}
encodes the group structure. E.g., the face map

d_{1}:G\times G\to G
is the group multiplication, and the other simplicial maps encode higher coherency information.

Definition 11.3
.

Given a group object
G,
denote by
N\left(G\right)
the simplicial object
\mbox{\v{C}}_{*\to\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu\to\mathcal{B}G}.

Definition 11.4
.

A
principal
G
-bundle
or
G
-torsor
over an object
S
is a pullback square of the form

\textstyle{P\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\ast\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi_{G}}
\textstyle{S\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{B}G.}

P
is called the
total space
of the bundle.

Definition 11.5
.

An
action
of a group object
G
on an object
V
in
\mathcal{X}
is a pullback square

\textstyle{V\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi_{\rho}}
\textstyle{\ast\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi_{G}}
\textstyle{S\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{B}G}

exhibiting
V
as the total space of a principal bundle over some object
S.
In this case the object
S
is called the
quotient stack
of
V
by
G
and is denoted by
{V}\mskip-3.0mu{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{155.0}{$|\mskip-3.0mu|$}}\mskip-3.0mu{G}.

Remark 11.6
.

Notice that, as a pullback of an effective epimorphism,

\pi_{\rho}:V\to{V}\mskip-3.0mu{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{155.0}{$|\mskip-3.0mu|$}}\mskip-3.0mu{G}

is an effective epimorphism, and hence

{V}\mskip-3.0mu{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{155.0}{$|\mskip-3.0mu|$}}\mskip-3.0mu{G}\simeq\underrightarrow{\mbox{colim}\vskip 0.5pt}\mspace{4mu}\mbox{\v{C}}_{\pi_{\rho}}.

By stacking pullback diagrams

\displaystyle V\times_{{V}\mskip-3.0mu{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{155.0}{$|\mskip-3.0mu|$}}\mskip-3.0mu{G}}V

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle*\times_{\mathcal{B}G}V

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle\left(*\times_{\mathcal{B}G}*\right)\times_{*}V

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle G\times V,

and more generally, the
\left(n+1\right)^{st}
-fold fiber product of
V
with itself over
{V}\mskip-3.0mu{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{155.0}{$|\mskip-3.0mu|$}}\mskip-3.0mu{G}
is equivalent to
G^{n}\times V.

So

{V}\mskip-3.0mu{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{155.0}{$|\mskip-3.0mu|$}}\mskip-3.0mu{G}\simeq\underrightarrow{\mbox{colim}\vskip 0.5pt}\mspace{4mu}\left(\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 7.02777pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-7.02777pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{V}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 31.02777pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces G\times V}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 7.02779pt\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 7.02779pt\raise-2.15277pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 89.16794pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{G\times G\times V\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 65.16795pt\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 65.16795pt\raise-2.15277pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 65.16795pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 167.39273pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces G\times G\times G\times V\cdots}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 143.39275pt\raise 3.01385pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 143.39275pt\raise 1.07639pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 143.39275pt\raise-1.07639pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 143.39275pt\raise-3.01385pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\right)

The map

\rho:=d_{0}:G\times V\to V

exhibits the action map of
G
on
V,
and the rest of the simplicial structure encodes the higher coherencies. Denote the above groupoid object by
N\left(G\ltimes V\right).
In particular, for an ordinary smooth action of a Lie group
G
on a manifold
V,
this agrees with the actual nerve of the action groupoid.

In particular, we proved the following proposition:

Proposition 11.7
.

For
G
any group object with an action on an object
V,
we have a canonical identification:

V\times_{{V}\mskip-3.0mu{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{155.0}{$|\mskip-3.0mu|$}}\mskip-3.0mu{G}}V\simeq G\times V.

12.
Jet Bundles and Differential Operators

12.1.
Jet bundles and disk bundles

Definition 12.1
.

Let
\pi:E\to M
be an object of
\mathcal{E}_{\mathfrak{Der}}/M.
Then the
formal disk bundle of
\pi
,
\mathbf{T}^{\infty}_{M}\left(\pi\right)\to M
is

\eta^{*}\eta_{!}\left(\pi\right),

where
\eta:M\to M_{dR}
is the unit map to the de Rham stack.

More concretely, there is a pullback diagram

\textstyle{\mathbf{T}^{\infty}_{M}\left(\pi\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{E\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi}
\textstyle{M\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\eta}
\textstyle{M\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\eta}
\textstyle{M_{dR}.}

Definition 12.2
.

Given
M\in\mathcal{E}_{\mathfrak{Der}},
the
formal disk bundle of
M
,
denoted by

\mathbf{T}^{\infty}M\to M

is the formal disk bundle of
id_{M},
i.e. the pullback
M\times_{M_{dR}}M\to M.

The following proposition follows immediately from the fact that the de Rham functor
\left(\mspace{3mu}\cdot\mspace{3mu}\right)_{dR}
preserves finite products:

Proposition 12.3
.

The formal disk bundle of
M
is the formal completion of
M
along the diagonal

\Delta:M\to M\times M,

that is
\mathbf{T}^{\infty}M\simeq\widehat{M}_{\Delta}.

Remark 12.4
.

For any
\pi:E\to M,
there is a factorization

\textstyle{\mathbf{T}^{\infty}_{M}\left(\pi\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi_{E}}
\scriptstyle{\mathbf{ev}_{E}}
\textstyle{E\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi}
\textstyle{\mathbf{T}^{\infty}M\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi_{M}}
\scriptstyle{\mathbf{ev}}
\textstyle{M\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\eta}
\textstyle{M\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\eta}
\textstyle{M_{dR}.}

The following proposition is immediate:

Proposition 12.5
.

Given any
F\to M
in
\mathcal{E}_{\mathfrak{Der}}/M,
and
D\in\mathcal{E}_{\mathfrak{Der}},
then
\mathbf{T}^{\infty}_{M}
applied to

F\times D\stackrel{{\scriptstyle pr}}{{\longrightarrow}}F\to M

is equivalent to

\mathbf{T}^{\infty}_{M}\left(F\right)\times D\stackrel{{\scriptstyle pr}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathbf{T}^{\infty}_{M}\left(F\right)\to M.

Lemma 12.6
.

Let
G
be a formally smooth group object. Then there is a canonical equivalence

G_{dR}\simeq{G}\mskip-3.0mu{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{155.0}{$|\mskip-3.0mu|$}}\mskip-3.0mu{\widehat{G}_{e}},

where
\widehat{G}_{e}
is the formal completion of the identity.

Proof.

Since the de Rham functor preserves limits and effective epimorphisms,

*\simeq*_{dR}\to\left(\mathcal{B}G\right)_{dR}

is an effective epimorphism. Due again to the preservation of limits, the fibered product
*\times_{\left(\mathcal{B}G\right)_{dR}}*\simeq G_{dR}.
It follows that
G_{dR}
is a group object and

\left(\mathcal{B}G\right)_{dR}\simeq\mathcal{B}\left(G_{dR}\right).

Denote by
P
the fibered product

\textstyle{P\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{B}G\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\eta}
\textstyle{\ast\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\left(\mathcal{B}G\right)_{dR}.}

Since
G
is formally smooth,

\eta_{G}:G\to G_{dR}

is an effective epimorphism, and hence

\pi_{0}\left(G\right)\to\pi_{0}\left(G_{dR}\right)

is as well. We can identify the latter map with the induced map

\pi_{1}\left(\mathcal{B}G\right)\to\pi_{1}\left(\left(\mathcal{B}G\right)_{dR}\right).

Analyzing the long exact sequence in homotopy sheaves from the fiber sequence

P\to\mathcal{B}G\to\left(\mathcal{B}G\right)_{dR},

we conclude that
\pi_{0}\left(P\right)\simeq*.
Thus,
P
is the classifying stack of a group object, and this group object is
\Omega_{*}P.
Consider diagram of pullback squares

\textstyle{\Omega_{\ast}P\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\ast\simeq\ast_{dR}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{G\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\eta_{G}}
\textstyle{G_{dR}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\ast\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\ast\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{P\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{B}G\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\ast\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{B}\left(G_{dR}\right).}

It follows that there is a canonical identification

\Omega_{*}P\simeq\widehat{G}_{e},

and thus

P\simeq\mathcal{B}\left(\widehat{G}_{e}\right).

Therefore there is a canonically induced action of
\widehat{G}_{e}
on
G,
and we can identify

G_{dR}\simeq{G}\mskip-3.0mu{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{155.0}{$|\mskip-3.0mu|$}}\mskip-3.0mu{\widehat{G}_{e}}.

∎

Corollary 12.7
.

Let
G
be a formally smooth group object. Denote by
\widehat{G}_{e}
the formal completion of
G
at the identity. Then there is a canonical equivalence

T^{\infty}\left(G\right)\simeq G\times\widehat{G}_{e}.

Proof.

By Lemma
12.6
, we can identify
\eta_{G}
with the quotient map

G\to{G}\mskip-3.0mu{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{155.0}{$|\mskip-3.0mu|$}}\mskip-3.0mu{\widehat{G}_{e}}.

The result now follows from Proposition
11.7
.
∎

Corollary 12.8
.

There is a canonical equivalence

\mathbf{T}^{\infty}\left(\mathbb{R}^{\left(m,n\right)}\right)\cong\mathbb{R}^{\left(m,n\right)}\times\mathbb{D}^{\left(m,n\right)}.

Proof.

For all
n
and
m,

\mathbb{R}^{\left(m,n\right)}
is an abelian group object, so by Corollary
12.7
,

\mathbf{T}^{\infty}\left(\mathbb{R}^{\left(m,n\right)}\right)\cong\mathbb{R}^{\left(m,n\right)}\times\widehat{\mathbb{R}^{\left(m,n\right)}}_{0}.

The result now follows from Corollary
10.20
.
∎

Definition 12.9
.

Let
\mathcal{A}
be a classical
{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}
-algebra. Consider the codiagonal map

\nabla:\mathcal{A}\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\mkern-13.73mu\resizebox{1305270}{540744}{$\phantom{x}{}_{\phantom{.}{}^{\infty}}$}$\crcr$\displaystyle\bigcirc$\crcr}}}}{\mathbin{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\mkern-13.73mu\resizebox{1305270}{540744}{$\phantom{x}{}_{\phantom{.}{}^{\infty}}$}$\crcr$\textstyle\bigcirc$\crcr}}}}{\mathbin{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\mkern-13.73mu\resizebox{1305270}{540744}{$\phantom{x}{}_{\phantom{.}{}^{\infty}}$}$\crcr$\scriptstyle\bigcirc$\crcr}}}}{\mathbin{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-13.73mu\resizebox{1305270}{540744}{$\phantom{x}{}_{\phantom{.}{}^{\infty}}$}$\crcr$\scriptscriptstyle\bigcirc$\crcr}}}}\mathcal{A}\to\mathcal{A},

and let
\mathcal{I}
be its kernel. Then the
k^{th}
-order infinitesimal neighborhood of the diagonal of
\mathbf{Spec}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}\left(\mathcal{A}\right)
is
\mathbf{Spec}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}}\left(\left(\mathcal{A}\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\mkern-13.73mu\resizebox{1305270}{540744}{$\phantom{x}{}_{\phantom{.}{}^{\infty}}$}$\crcr$\displaystyle\bigcirc$\crcr}}}}{\mathbin{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\mkern-13.73mu\resizebox{1305270}{540744}{$\phantom{x}{}_{\phantom{.}{}^{\infty}}$}$\crcr$\textstyle\bigcirc$\crcr}}}}{\mathbin{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\mkern-13.73mu\resizebox{1305270}{540744}{$\phantom{x}{}_{\phantom{.}{}^{\infty}}$}$\crcr$\scriptstyle\bigcirc$\crcr}}}}{\mathbin{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-13.73mu\resizebox{1305270}{540744}{$\phantom{x}{}_{\phantom{.}{}^{\infty}}$}$\crcr$\scriptscriptstyle\bigcirc$\crcr}}}}\mathcal{A}\right)/\mathcal{I}^{k+1}\right).
For
X
an affine
{\mathbf{SC}^{\infty}}
-scheme, we denote this by
X_{\left(k\right)},
and denote the canonical maps
X_{\left(k\right)}\to X
coming from first and second projection respectively by
\alpha^{k}
and
\beta^{k}.

Lemma 12.10
.

For any non-negative integers
n
an
m
and
k,

\left(\mathbb{R}^{n|m}\right)_{\left(k\right)}\simeq\mathbb{R}^{n|m}\times\mathbb{D}_{\left(k\right)}^{n|m}.

Proof.

Notice that the codiagonal map

\mathbf{C}^{\infty}\left(x_{1},x_{2},\ldots,x_{n}|\eta_{1},\ldots,\eta_{m}\right)\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\mkern-13.73mu\resizebox{1305270}{540744}{$\phantom{x}{}_{\phantom{.}{}^{\infty}}$}$\crcr$\displaystyle\bigcirc$\crcr}}}}{\mathbin{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\mkern-13.73mu\resizebox{1305270}{540744}{$\phantom{x}{}_{\phantom{.}{}^{\infty}}$}$\crcr$\textstyle\bigcirc$\crcr}}}}{\mathbin{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\mkern-13.73mu\resizebox{1305270}{540744}{$\phantom{x}{}_{\phantom{.}{}^{\infty}}$}$\crcr$\scriptstyle\bigcirc$\crcr}}}}{\mathbin{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-13.73mu\resizebox{1305270}{540744}{$\phantom{x}{}_{\phantom{.}{}^{\infty}}$}$\crcr$\scriptscriptstyle\bigcirc$\crcr}}}}\mathbf{C}^{\infty}\left(y_{1},y_{2},\ldots,y_{n}|\xi_{1},\ldots,\xi_{m}\right)\to\mathbf{C}^{\infty}\left(t_{1},t_{2},\ldots,t_{n}|\theta_{1},\ldots,\theta_{m}\right)

sends
x_{i}
and
y_{i}
to
t_{i},
and
\eta_{j}
and
\xi_{j}
to
\theta_{j}.
It follows that the homogeneous ideal
\mathcal{I}=\ker\left(\nabla\right)
is generated by
\left(x_{1}-y_{1},\ldots,x_{n}-y_{n}|\eta_{1}-\xi_{1},\ldots,\eta_{m}-\xi_{m}\right).
Define

z_{i}:=x_{i}-y_{i}

and

\theta_{i}:=\eta_{i}-\xi_{i}.

Then we have the following string of natural equivalences:

\left(\mathbf{C}^{\infty}\left(\mathbf{x}|\mathbf{\eta}\right)\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\mkern-13.73mu\resizebox{1305270}{540744}{$\phantom{x}{}_{\phantom{.}{}^{\infty}}$}$\crcr$\displaystyle\bigcirc$\crcr}}}}{\mathbin{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\mkern-13.73mu\resizebox{1305270}{540744}{$\phantom{x}{}_{\phantom{.}{}^{\infty}}$}$\crcr$\textstyle\bigcirc$\crcr}}}}{\mathbin{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\mkern-13.73mu\resizebox{1305270}{540744}{$\phantom{x}{}_{\phantom{.}{}^{\infty}}$}$\crcr$\scriptstyle\bigcirc$\crcr}}}}{\mathbin{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-13.73mu\resizebox{1305270}{540744}{$\phantom{x}{}_{\phantom{.}{}^{\infty}}$}$\crcr$\scriptscriptstyle\bigcirc$\crcr}}}}\mathbf{C}^{\infty}\left(\mathbf{y}|\mathbf{\xi}\right)\right)/\mathcal{I}^{k}

\displaystyle\cong

\mathbf{C}^{\infty}\left(\mathbf{x}|\mathbf{\eta}\right)\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\mkern-13.73mu\resizebox{1305270}{540744}{$\phantom{x}{}_{\phantom{.}{}^{\infty}}$}$\crcr$\displaystyle\bigcirc$\crcr}}}}{\mathbin{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\mkern-13.73mu\resizebox{1305270}{540744}{$\phantom{x}{}_{\phantom{.}{}^{\infty}}$}$\crcr$\textstyle\bigcirc$\crcr}}}}{\mathbin{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\mkern-13.73mu\resizebox{1305270}{540744}{$\phantom{x}{}_{\phantom{.}{}^{\infty}}$}$\crcr$\scriptstyle\bigcirc$\crcr}}}}{\mathbin{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-13.73mu\resizebox{1305270}{540744}{$\phantom{x}{}_{\phantom{.}{}^{\infty}}$}$\crcr$\scriptscriptstyle\bigcirc$\crcr}}}}\mathbf{C}^{\infty}\left(\mathbf{y}|\mathbf{\xi}\right)/\left(x_{1}-y_{1},\ldots,x_{n}-y_{n}|\eta_{1}-\xi_{1},\ldots,\eta_{m}-\xi_{m}\right)^{k}

\displaystyle\cong

\left(\mathbf{C}^{\infty}\left(z_{1},\ldots,z_{n}|\theta_{1},\ldots,\theta_{m}\right)\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\mkern-13.73mu\resizebox{1305270}{540744}{$\phantom{x}{}_{\phantom{.}{}^{\infty}}$}$\crcr$\displaystyle\bigcirc$\crcr}}}}{\mathbin{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\mkern-13.73mu\resizebox{1305270}{540744}{$\phantom{x}{}_{\phantom{.}{}^{\infty}}$}$\crcr$\textstyle\bigcirc$\crcr}}}}{\mathbin{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\mkern-13.73mu\resizebox{1305270}{540744}{$\phantom{x}{}_{\phantom{.}{}^{\infty}}$}$\crcr$\scriptstyle\bigcirc$\crcr}}}}{\mathbin{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-13.73mu\resizebox{1305270}{540744}{$\phantom{x}{}_{\phantom{.}{}^{\infty}}$}$\crcr$\scriptscriptstyle\bigcirc$\crcr}}}}\mathbf{C}^{\infty}\left(\mathbf{y}|\mathbf{\xi}\right)\right)/\left(z_{1},\ldots,z_{n},\theta_{1},\ldots,\theta_{m}\right)^{k}

\displaystyle\cong

\left(\mathbf{C}^{\infty}\left(z_{1},\ldots,z_{n}|\theta_{1},\ldots,\theta_{m}\right)/\left(z_{1},\ldots,z_{n},\theta_{1},\ldots,\theta_{m}\right)^{k}\right)\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\mkern-13.73mu\resizebox{1305270}{540744}{$\phantom{x}{}_{\phantom{.}{}^{\infty}}$}$\crcr$\displaystyle\bigcirc$\crcr}}}}{\mathbin{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\mkern-13.73mu\resizebox{1305270}{540744}{$\phantom{x}{}_{\phantom{.}{}^{\infty}}$}$\crcr$\textstyle\bigcirc$\crcr}}}}{\mathbin{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\mkern-13.73mu\resizebox{1305270}{540744}{$\phantom{x}{}_{\phantom{.}{}^{\infty}}$}$\crcr$\scriptstyle\bigcirc$\crcr}}}}{\mathbin{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-13.73mu\resizebox{1305270}{540744}{$\phantom{x}{}_{\phantom{.}{}^{\infty}}$}$\crcr$\scriptscriptstyle\bigcirc$\crcr}}}}\mathbf{C}^{\infty}\left(\mathbf{y}|\mathbf{\xi}\right)

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle\mathbf{C}^{\infty}\left(\mathbb{D}_{\left(k\right)}^{n|m}\times\mathbb{R}^{n|m}\right)

∎

Proposition 12.11
.

Let
\mathcal{M}
be a finite dimensional supermanifold. Then

\mathbf{T}^{\infty}\left(\mathcal{M}\right)=\underset{k}{\underrightarrow{\mbox{colim}\vskip 0.5pt}\mspace{4mu}}\mathcal{M}_{\left(k\right)}.

Proof.

Write
\mathcal{M}\simeq\underset{\alpha}{\underrightarrow{\mbox{colim}\vskip 0.5pt}\mspace{4mu}}U_{\alpha}
in
\mathcal{E}_{\mathfrak{Der}}.
with each
U_{\alpha}
diffeomorphic to
\mathbb{R}^{n|m}.
It follows from Proposition
12.13
and Lemma
12.10
that

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{\left(k\right)}

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle\underset{\alpha}{\underrightarrow{\mbox{colim}\vskip 0.5pt}\mspace{4mu}}\left(U_{\alpha}\right)_{\left(k\right)}

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle\underset{\alpha}{\underrightarrow{\mbox{colim}\vskip 0.5pt}\mspace{4mu}}U_{\alpha}\times\mathbb{D}_{\left(k\right)}^{n|m}.

Hence

\displaystyle\underset{k}{\underrightarrow{\mbox{colim}\vskip 0.5pt}\mspace{4mu}}\mathcal{M}_{\left(k\right)}

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle\underset{k}{\underrightarrow{\mbox{colim}\vskip 0.5pt}\mspace{4mu}}\underset{\alpha}{\underrightarrow{\mbox{colim}\vskip 0.5pt}\mspace{4mu}}\left(U_{\alpha}\times\mathbb{D}_{\left(k\right)}^{n|m}\right)

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle\underset{\alpha}{\underrightarrow{\mbox{colim}\vskip 0.5pt}\mspace{4mu}}\underset{k}{\underrightarrow{\mbox{colim}\vskip 0.5pt}\mspace{4mu}}\left(U_{\alpha}\times\mathbb{D}_{\left(k\right)}^{n|m}\right)

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle\underset{\alpha}{\underrightarrow{\mbox{colim}\vskip 0.5pt}\mspace{4mu}}\mathbb{T}^{\infty}\left(U_{\alpha}\right)\

Note however, that since each open inclusion
U_{\alpha}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{M}
is formally étale, it follows that we have two pullback squares

\textstyle{\mathbf{T}^{\infty}U_{\alpha}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{U_{\alpha}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{U_{\alpha}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{U_{dR}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{M}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{M}_{dR},}

and hence

\mathbf{T}^{\infty}U_{\alpha}\simeq\mathbf{T}^{\infty}_{\mathcal{M}}U_{\alpha}\simeq\mathbf{T}^{\infty}\mathcal{M}\times_{\mathcal{M}}U_{\alpha}.

It follows thus that

\displaystyle\underset{k}{\underrightarrow{\mbox{colim}\vskip 0.5pt}\mspace{4mu}}\mathcal{M}_{\left(k\right)}

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle\underset{\alpha}{\underrightarrow{\mbox{colim}\vskip 0.5pt}\mspace{4mu}}\mathbf{T}^{\infty}\mathcal{M}\times_{\mathcal{M}}U_{\alpha}

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle\mathbf{T}^{\infty}\c{M}\times_{\mathcal{M}}\mathcal{M}

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle\mathbf{T}^{\infty}\mathcal{M}.

∎

Lemma 12.12
.

If
\mathcal{M}
is a supermanifold, then there is an isomorphism of presheaves on
\mathcal{M}_{0}

U\mapsto\Gamma_{\mathcal{M}}\left(\mathcal{J}^{k}\left(U,\mathbb{R}\right)\right)\cong\mathbf{C}^{\infty}\left(U_{\left(k\right)}\right).

Proof.

For simplicity, we will prove this for an even manifold
M
.
Fix
U\subseteq M
an open subset. Define

\displaystyle\psi_{U}:\mathbf{C}^{\infty}\left(U_{\left(k\right)}\right)

\displaystyle\to

\displaystyle\Gamma_{\mathcal{M}}\left(\mathcal{J}^{k}\left(U,\mathbb{R}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\left[f\right]

\displaystyle\mapsto

\displaystyle\left(x\mapsto j^{1}_{\left(x,x\right)}\left(f|_{\left\{x\right\}\times X}\right)\right),

where

\displaystyle\mathbf{C}^{\infty}\left(U\times U\right)

\displaystyle\to

\displaystyle\mathbf{C}^{\infty}\left(U\times U\right)/\mathcal{I}^{k+1}

\displaystyle f

\displaystyle\mapsto

\displaystyle\left[f\right].

This clearly defines a natural transformation of presheaves. We claim that
\psi_{U}
is an isomorphism. We will construct a map in the opposite direction. Fix a locally finite atlas
\left(V_{i}\hookrightarrow U\right),
with
\varphi_{i}:V_{i}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\cong}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathbb{R}^{n}
and a partition of unity
\lambda
subordinate to it. Note that each coordinate patch
\varphi_{i}
induces a trivialization

\mathcal{J}^{k}\left(U,\mathbb{R}\right)|_{V_{i}}\cong V_{i}\times\mathcal{J}^{k}_{0}\left(\mathbb{R}^{n}\right),

where
\mathcal{J}^{k}_{0}\left(\mathbb{R}^{n}\right)
is the set of
k
-jets of smooth functions on
\mathbb{R}^{n}
at the origin. I.e., these are induced jet coordinates on
\mathcal{J}^{k}\left(U,\mathbb{R}\right),

\left(x^{1},\ldots,x^{n},u^{I}\right),
with
u^{I}
ranging over all multi-indices with
|I|\leq k.
By multiplying with
1=\underset{i}{\sum}\lambda_{i},
we get that

\sigma=\underset{i}{\sum}\sigma^{i},

with each
\sigma^{i}
a section supported on
V_{i}.
Hence, in local coordinates we can write

\sigma^{i}=\underset{|I|\leq k}{\sum}\sigma^{i}_{I}u^{I},

with each
\sigma^{i}_{I}
a smooth function on
V_{i}.
Define
f^{i}_{\sigma}:V_{i}\times V_{i}\to\mathbb{R}
by

f^{i}_{\sigma}\left(x,y\right)=\underset{|I|\leq k}{\sum}\sigma^{i}_{I}\left(x\right)\left(x-y\right)^{I}.

Then
\underset{i}{\sum}f^{i}_{\sigma}\left(x,y\right)
is a smooth function defined on some neighborhood
W
of the diagonal in
U\times U,
independent of
\sigma.
Let
\beta
be a bump function supported in
W.
Then

f_{\sigma}:=\beta\cdot\underset{i}{\sum}f^{i}_{\sigma}\left(x,y\right)

is a global function on
U\times U.
Fixing the choice of coordinate charts and W, define
\theta\left(\sigma\right)=\left[f_{\sigma}\right].
Notice that
\psi_{U}\left(\theta\left(\sigma\right)\right)=\sigma
and
\theta\left(\psi_{U}\left(\left[f\right]\right)\right)=\left[f\right].
It follows that
\psi_{U}
is an isomorphism.
∎

Proposition 12.13
.

The assignment
U\mapsto U_{\left(k\right)}
is a cosheaf on
\mathcal{M}_{0}
with values in
\mathcal{E}_{\mathfrak{Der}}.

Proof.

The assignment
U\mapsto\mathbf{C}^{\infty}\left(U_{\left(k\right)}\right)
can be identified the sheaf of sections of
\mathcal{J}^{k}\left(\mathcal{M},\mathbb{R}\right),
since for all
U,

\mathcal{J}^{k}\left(\mathcal{M},\mathbb{R}\right)|_{U}\cong\mathcal{J}^{k}\left(U,\mathbb{R}\right).

Applying
\mathbf{Spec}_{\mathbf{C}^{\infty}},
the result now follows.
∎

More generally, there is the following result:

Proposition 12.14
.

[
18
, Chapter 7]

Let
\pi:E\to M
be a surjective submersion between finite dimensional manifolds. Then
\mathcal{J}^{k}\left(M,E\right)\to M
can be identified with
\beta^{k}_{*}\alpha^{*}_{k}\left(\pi\right)
in
\mathcal{E}_{\mathfrak{Der}}/M.

Corollary 12.15
.

Let
\pi:E\to M
be a surjective submersion between finite dimensional manifolds. Then the following are equivalent:

1.

S_{\mathsf{Conv}}\left(\mathcal{J}^{\infty}\left(\pi\right)\right)\to M,
where
\mathcal{J}^{\infty}\left(\pi\right)
is given its usual Frechét manifold structure.

2.

The limit
\underset{k}{\varprojlim\mspace{3mu}}\beta^{k}_{*}\alpha^{*}_{k}\left(\pi\right).

Proof.

This follows immediately from Corollary
6.44
and Proposition
12.14
.
∎

Proposition 12.16
.

Let

\textstyle{P\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{b}
\scriptstyle{a}
\textstyle{Z\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{g}
\textstyle{X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f}
\textstyle{Y}

be a pullback diagram in an infinity topos
\mathcal{X}.
Then there is a canonical equivalence of endofunctors of
\mathcal{X}/Z

b_{*}a^{*}\simeq g^{*}f_{*}.

Proof.

Let
w:W\to Z
be an object of
\mathcal{X}/Z.

Consider the diagram of pullback squares

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 20.54062pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-20.54062pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{P\times_{Z}W\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 25.25105pt\raise 5.00694pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.00694pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\pi_{2}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 44.54062pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-14.57915pt\raise-15.5pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.00694pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\pi_{1}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-24.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 44.54062pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{W\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 52.95729pt\raise-14.75pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.50694pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{w}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 52.95729pt\raise-24.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern-6.90451pt\raise-31.33331pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{P\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 21.78716pt\raise-25.90276pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.43056pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{b}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 46.18646pt\raise-31.33331pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-10.33765pt\raise-46.08331pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.50694pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{a}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-55.33331pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 46.18646pt\raise-31.33331pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{Z\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 52.95729pt\raise-46.08331pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-0.8264pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{g}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 52.95729pt\raise-55.33331pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern-7.53471pt\raise-62.66663pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 21.39702pt\raise-68.77773pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.75pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{f}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 45.9434pt\raise-62.66663pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 45.9434pt\raise-62.66663pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{Y}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces.

On one hand,
b^{*}w
is
\pi_{1}:P\times_{Z}W\to P\in\mathcal{X}/P,
and hence

a_{!}b^{*}w
is just
a\circ\pi_{1}\in\mathcal{X}/X.
On the other hand,
g_{!}w
is
g\circ w\in\mathcal{X}/Y,
and, by pasting pullback diagrams, we see that
f^{*}g_{!}w
is thus

X\times_{Y}W\to X.
However, this is just the left vertical map in the outer pullback square (formed by composition), and hence is canonically equivalent to
a\circ\pi_{1}
also. Hence, there is a canonical equivalence of functors

a_{!}b^{*}w\simeq f^{*}g_{!}.

Both of these functors are left adjoints, so there corresponding right adjoint must also be equivalent, and these are
b_{*}a^{*}
and
g^{*}f_{*}
respectively.
∎

Lemma 12.17
.

Suppose that

\textstyle{P\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{b}
\scriptstyle{a}
\textstyle{Z\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{g}
\textstyle{X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f}
\textstyle{Y}

is a pullback diagram in an
\infty
-topos
\mathcal{X}
and

P\simeq\underset{k\in{\mathcal{K}}_{0}}{\underrightarrow{\mbox{colim}\vskip 0.5pt}\mspace{4mu}}P_{k}

is an expression of
P
as a colimit over some small
\infty
-category
\mathcal{K}.
For each
k\in\mathcal{K}_{0},
consider the induced maps

b_{k}:P_{k}\to Z

and

a_{k}:P_{k}\to X.

Notice that for each such
k,
there is a commutative square

\textstyle{P_{k}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{b_{k}}
\scriptstyle{a_{k}}
\textstyle{Z\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{g}
\textstyle{X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f}
\textstyle{Y}

(which is no longer necessarily a pullback diagram). Then there is a canonical equivalence of endofunctors of
\mathcal{X}/X

\underset{k\in\mathcal{K}_{0}}{\varprojlim\mspace{3mu}}b^{k}_{*}a^{*}_{k}\simeq b^{*}a_{*}.

Proof.

Let
w:W\to Z\in\mathcal{X}/Z
be arbitrary. By the universality of colimits, we have a pullback diagram

\textstyle{{\underset{k\in\mathcal{K}_{0}}{\underrightarrow{\mbox{colim}\vskip 0.5pt}\mspace{4mu}}\left(P_{k}\times_{Z}W\right)}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{W\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{w}
\textstyle{P\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{b}
\textstyle{Z.}

Let
\mu
be a colimiting cocone with components
\mu_{k}.
Then we have

b^{*}w\simeq\underset{k\in\mathcal{K}_{0}}{\underrightarrow{\mbox{colim}\vskip 0.5pt}\mspace{4mu}}\left(\mu_{k}\right)_{!}\left(b_{k}^{*}w\right).

Since
a_{!}
preserves colimits,

\displaystyle a_{!}b^{*}w\simeq\underset{k\in\mathcal{K}_{0}}{\underrightarrow{\mbox{colim}\vskip 0.5pt}\mspace{4mu}}a_{!}\left(\mu_{k}\right)_{!}\left(b_{k}^{*}w\right)

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle\underset{k\in\mathcal{K}_{0}}{\underrightarrow{\mbox{colim}\vskip 0.5pt}\mspace{4mu}}\left(a\circ\mu_{k}\right)_{!}\left(b_{k}^{*}w\right)

So, for any
w
in
\mathcal{X}/W
and
q:Q\to X
in
\mathcal{X}/X,

\displaystyle\mathbb{M}\mathrm{ap}_{\mathcal{X}/X}\left(a_{!}b^{*}w,q\right)

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle\mathbb{M}\mathrm{ap}_{\mathcal{X}/X}\left(\underset{k\in\mathcal{K}_{0}}{\underrightarrow{\mbox{colim}\vskip 0.5pt}\mspace{4mu}}\left(a_{k}\right)_{!}\left(b_{k}^{*}w\right),q\right)

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle\underset{k\in\mathcal{K}_{0}}{\varprojlim\mspace{3mu}}\mathbb{M}\mathrm{ap}_{\mathcal{X}/X}\left(\left(a_{k}\right)_{!}\left(b_{k}^{*}w\right),q\right)

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle\underset{k\in\mathcal{K}_{0}}{\varprojlim\mspace{3mu}}\mathbb{M}\mathrm{ap}_{\mathcal{X}/W}\left(w,b^{k}_{*}a_{k}^{*}q\right)

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle\mathbb{M}\mathrm{ap}_{\mathcal{X}/W}\left(w,\underset{k\in\mathcal{K}_{0}}{\varprojlim\mspace{3mu}}b^{k}_{*}a_{k}^{*}q\right).

The result now follows by the uniqueness of adjoints.
∎

Corollary 12.18
.

Let
\pi:E\to M
be a surjective submersion between finite dimensional manifolds. Then there is a canonical identification

\eta_{M}^{*}\eta^{M}_{*}\left(\pi\right)\simeq S_{\mathsf{Conv}}\left(\mathcal{J}^{\infty}\left(\pi\right)\right)\to M,

where
\mathcal{J}^{\infty}\left(\pi\right)
is the infinite jet bundle regarded as a Frechét manifold.

Proof.

By definition of
T^{\infty}M,
we have a pullback diagram

\textstyle{T^{\infty}M\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{M\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\eta_{M}}
\textstyle{M\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\eta_{M}}
\textstyle{M_{dR}.}

By Proposition
12.11
, we have

T^{\infty}M\simeq\underset{k}{\underrightarrow{\mbox{colim}\vskip 0.5pt}\mspace{4mu}}M_{(k)},

so we are in the situation of Lemma
12.17
.
Therefore

\eta^{*}\eta_{*}\left(\pi\right)\simeq\underset{k}{\varprojlim\mspace{3mu}}b^{k}_{*}a_{k}^{*}\left(\pi\right).

So we are done by Corollary
12.15
.
∎

This justifies the following definition in general:

Definition 12.19
.

Let
\pi:E\to M
be an object of
\mathcal{E}_{\mathfrak{Der}}/M,
and consider the unit map

\eta:M\to M_{dR}.

Then
the infinite jet bundle of
\pi
is the object
\eta^{*}\eta_{*}\left(\pi\right)
in
\mathcal{E}_{\mathfrak{Der}}/M.

Remark 12.20
.

Observe that we have an adjunction:

\mathbf{T}^{\infty}_{M}\dashv\mathcal{J}^{\infty}.

12.1.1.
Jet prolongations

Given a fiber bundle
\pi:E\to M,
and a section
\sigma,
for any
0\leq k\leq\infty,
there is its
k^{th}
-jet prolongation
j^{k}\left(\sigma\right)
which is a section of
\mathcal{J}^{k}\left(\pi\right).
This construction leads to a set-theoretic map

j^{k}:\Gamma\left(\pi\right)\to\Gamma\left(\mathcal{J}^{k}\left(\pi\right)\right)

and one way wonder if this map is in a suitable sense smooth, or better, in topos-theoretic language, whether or not this is just the evaluation on the point of a map

j^{k}:\mathbf{\Gamma}_{M}\left(\mathcal{J}^{k}\left(\pi\right)\right)\to\mathbf{\Gamma}_{M}\left(\mathcal{J}^{k}\left(\pi\right)\right)

in
\mathcal{E}_{\mathfrak{Der}}.
We shall show that this is indeed the case.

We will firstly dispense with the case
k=\infty,
which will hold for an arbitrary
\pi:E\to M
in
\mathcal{E}_{\mathfrak{Der}}.

Lemma 12.21
.

There is a canonical natural transformation

J^{\infty}:\mathbf{\Gamma}_{M}\left(\mspace{3mu}\cdot\mspace{3mu}\right)\times M\Rightarrow\mathcal{J}^{\infty}.

Proof.

Consider the diagram

\textstyle{E\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi}
\textstyle{M\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{t}
\scriptstyle{\eta}
\textstyle{M_{dR}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{s}
\textstyle{\ast}

Notice that
t=s\eta,
so on one hand we have

\displaystyle\mathbf{\Gamma}_{M}\left(\mspace{3mu}\cdot\mspace{3mu}\right)\times M

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle t^{*}t_{*}

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle\eta^{*}s^{*}s_{*}\eta_{*}.

On the other hand, we have that
\mathcal{J}^{\infty}=\eta^{*}\eta_{*},
so the counit

s^{*}s_{*}\Rightarrow id_{\mathcal{E}_{\mathfrak{Der}}/M_{dR}}

induces a canonical natural transformation

J^{\infty}:\mathbf{\Gamma}_{M}\left(\mspace{3mu}\cdot\mspace{3mu}\right)\times M\Rightarrow\mathcal{J}^{\infty}.

∎

Now for any
E\to M
in
\mathcal{E}_{\mathfrak{Der}}/M,

\mathbf{\Gamma}_{M}\left(E\right)=t_{*}\left(E\right),
and there is a canonical map

t_{*}\left(E\right)\to t^{*}t_{*}t^{*}\left(E\right)

induced by the unit map

id\Rightarrow t_{*}t^{*}.

We can now consider the composite

t_{*}\left(\pi\right)\to t_{*}t^{*}t_{*}\left(\pi\right)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle t_{*}J^{\infty}}}{{-\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu-\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu-\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu-\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu-\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu-\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu\longrightarrow}}t_{*}\eta^{*}\eta_{*}\left(E\right),

which we denote by
j^{\infty}\left(E\right).
Clearly, these maps assemble into a natural transformation

j^{\infty}:\mathbf{\Gamma}_{M}\Rightarrow\mathbf{\Gamma}_{M}\circ\mathcal{J}^{\infty}.

Definition 12.22
.

The above transformation
j^{\infty}
is called
the
\infty
-jet prolongation

Now, let
M
be a manifold, but allow
E
to be arbitrary. Consider the diagram

\textstyle{E\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi}
\textstyle{M_{\left(k\right)}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\beta}
\scriptstyle{\alpha}
\textstyle{M\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{t}
\textstyle{\ast.}

Notice that since
\ast
is terminal,
t\circ\alpha\simeq t\circ\beta.
Consequently

\displaystyle\Gamma_{M}\left(\mathcal{J}^{k}\left(\pi\right)\right)

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle t_{*}\beta_{*}\alpha^{*}\left(\pi\right)

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle\left(t\beta\right)_{*}\alpha^{*}\left(\pi\right)

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle\left(t\alpha\right)_{*}\alpha^{*}\left(\pi\right)

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle t_{*}\alpha_{*}\alpha^{*}\left(\pi\right).

Denote by
\lambda
the unit of the adjunction
\alpha^{*}\dashv\alpha_{*}.
Then we have the canonical map

t_{*}\left(\lambda_{\pi}\right):t_{*}\left(\pi\right)\to t_{*}\alpha_{*}\alpha^{*}\left(\pi\right),

which can be identified with a map

\mathbf{\Gamma}_{M}\left(\pi\right)\to\mathbf{\Gamma}_{M}\left(\mathcal{J}^{k}\left(\pi\right)\right),

which can easily be checked to be a natural transformation

j^{k}:\mathbf{\Gamma}_{M}\Rightarrow\mathbf{\Gamma}_{M}\circ\mathcal{J}^{k}.

Definition 12.23
.

The above transformation
j^{k}
is called
the
k
-jet prolongation
.

12.2.
Differential operators

Notice that if
D:\mathcal{J}^{\infty}E\to E
is a morphism in
\mathcal{E}_{\mathfrak{Der}}/M,
there is an induced map
\widehat{D}
of stacks of sections, defined as the composite

\mathbf{\Gamma}_{M}\left(E\right)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle j^{\infty}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathbf{\Gamma}_{M}\left(\mathcal{J}^{\infty}E\right)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathbf{\Gamma}_{M}\left(D\right)}}{{-\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu-\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu-\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu-\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu-\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu-\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu\longrightarrow}}\mathbf{\Gamma}_{M}\left(F\right).

Definition 12.24
.

Given
E,F\in\mathcal{E}_{\mathfrak{Der}}/M,
the
space of (non-linear) differential operators
from
\mathbf{\Gamma}_{M}\left(E\right)
to
\mathbf{\Gamma}_{M}\left(F\right)
is the essential image of

\mathbb{M}\mathrm{ap}_{\mathcal{E}_{\mathfrak{Der}}/M}\left(\mathcal{J}^{\infty}E,F\right)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathbf{\Gamma}_{M}}}{{-\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu-\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu-\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu-\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu-\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu-\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu\longrightarrow}}\mathbb{M}\mathrm{ap}_{\mathcal{E}_{\mathfrak{Der}}}\left(\mathbf{\Gamma}_{M}\left(\mathcal{J}^{\infty}E\right),\mathbf{\Gamma}_{M}\left(F\right)\right)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\left(j^{\infty}\right)^{*}}}{{-\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu-\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu-\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu-\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu-\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu-\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu\longrightarrow}}\mathbb{M}\mathrm{ap}_{\mathcal{E}_{\mathfrak{Der}}}\left(\mathbf{\Gamma}_{M}\left(E\right),\mathbf{\Gamma}_{M}\left(F\right)\right).

The space of
formal differential operators
is simply the space
\mathbb{M}\mathrm{ap}_{\mathcal{E}_{\mathfrak{Der}}/M}\left(\mathcal{J}^{\infty}E,F\right).

A
\widehat{D}
is a differential operator
of order
k
if there is a factorization of
D
as

\mathcal{J}^{\infty}\left(E\right)\to\mathcal{J}^{k}\left(E\right)\to\operatorname{F}.

Note that since
\mathcal{J}^{\infty}=\eta^{*}\eta_{*},
and
\eta^{*}\dashv\eta_{*},

\mathcal{J}^{\infty}
inherits the structure of a comonad. Consider the comonadic adjunction

\textstyle{\mathcal{E}_{\mathfrak{Der}}/M\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{C}
\textstyle{\mathbf{CoAlg}_{\mathcal{J}}^{\infty}\left(\mathcal{E}_{\mathfrak{Der}}/M\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{U}

between the
\infty
-category of Eilenberg-Moore coalgebras for
\mathcal{J}^{\infty}
and
\mathcal{E}_{\mathfrak{Der}}/M,
where
U
is the forgetful functor, and
C
is the cofree coalgebra functor, with
C\vdash U.
Notice that for
X,Y\in\mathcal{E}_{\mathfrak{Der}}/M,

\displaystyle\mathbb{M}\mathrm{ap}\left(CX,CY\right)

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle\mathbb{M}\mathrm{ap}\left(UCX,Y\right)

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle\mathbb{M}\mathrm{ap}\left(\mathcal{J}^{\infty}X,Y\right),

that is the space of
\mathcal{J}^{\infty}
-coalgebra maps from
CX
to
CY
is the same as the space of formal differential operators from
X
to
Y
.

Definition 12.25
.

The
\infty
-category of formal differential operators on M,

\mathsf{FDiff_{M}},
is the essential image of
C
above.

Lemma 12.26
.

The composition of two differential operators is a differential operator.

Proof.

The follows by using the composition in the
\infty
-category
\mathbf{CoAlg}_{\mathcal{J}}^{\infty}\left(\mathcal{E}_{\mathfrak{Der}}/M\right).
Suppose that
D:\mathcal{J}^{\infty}\left(E\right)\to F
and
D^{\prime}:\mathcal{J}^{\infty}\left(F\right)\to G
are formal differential operators. Define
D^{\prime}\star D
to be the following composition

\mathcal{J}^{\infty}\left(E\right)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mu_{E}}}{{-\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu-\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu-\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu-\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu-\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu-\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu\longrightarrow}}\mathcal{J}^{\infty}\mathcal{J}^{\infty}\left(E\right)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathcal{J}^{\infty}\left(D\right)}}{{-\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu-\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu-\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu-\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu-\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu-\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu\mskip-3.0mu\longrightarrow}}\mathcal{J}^{\infty}\left(F\right)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle D^{\prime}}}{{\longrightarrow}}G,

where
\mu
comes from the comonad structure on
\mathcal{J}^{\infty}=\eta^{*}\eta_{*}.
A careful unwinding of the definitions shows that the induced differential operator

\mathbf{\Gamma}_{M}\left(E\right)\to\mathbf{\Gamma}_{M}\left(G\right)

is equivalent the composite
\hat{D^{\prime}}\circ\hat{D}.

∎

Often, but certainly not always, a given differential operator
B
has a unique formal differential operator
D
inducing it. For example, suppose that
M
is a manifold, and
E\to M
is a surjective submersions onto
M,
then this is a classical fact.

Proposition 12.27
.

Let
E\to M
be in
\mathcal{E}_{\mathfrak{Der}}/M
. All differential operator from
\mathbf{\Gamma}_{M}\left(E\right)
to
\mathbf{\Gamma}_{M}\left(F\right),
for all
F\to M
in
\mathcal{E}_{\mathfrak{Der}}/M,
uniquely determine a corresponding formal differential operator inducing them, (up to equivalence) if and only if

J^{\infty}_{M}:M\times\mathbf{\Gamma}_{M}\left(E\right)\to\mathcal{J}^{\infty}E

is an epimorphism.

Proof.

Suppose that

D,D^{\prime}:\mathcal{J}^{\infty}E\to F

are two formal differential operators that
\widehat{D}\simeq\widehat{D^{\prime}}.
Notice that

\widehat{D}\simeq\mathbf{\Gamma}_{M}\left(D\circ\phi\right),

and similarly for
D^{\prime}.
Moreover, we have a commutative diagram

\textstyle{M\times\mathbf{\Gamma}_{M}\left(E\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{J^{\infty}_{M}}
\scriptstyle{id_{M}\times j^{\infty}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{J}^{\infty}E\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{D}
\textstyle{F}
\textstyle{M\times\mathbf{\Gamma}_{M}\left(\mathcal{J}^{\infty}E\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\epsilon_{\mathcal{J}^{\infty}E}}
\scriptstyle{\widehat{D}}
\textstyle{M\times\mathbf{\Gamma}_{M}\left(F\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces,}
\scriptstyle{\epsilon_{F}}

where
\epsilon
is the counit of the adjunction
t^{*}\dashv t_{*}=\mathbf{\Gamma}_{M}
(and similarly for
D^{\prime}
). It follows that
D\circ J^{\infty}_{M}
is completely determined by
\widehat{D}.
This completely determines
D
for all differential operators
D
if and only if
J^{\infty}_{M}
is an epimorphism (by definition).
∎

Proposition 12.28
.

If
E\to M
is a surjective submersion of manifolds, and let
F\to M
be such that
F
is a concrete sheaf (aka diffeological space), then any differential operator

B:\mathbf{\Gamma}_{M}\left(E\right)\to\mathbf{\Gamma}_{M}\left(F\right)

is induced by a unique formal differential operator

D:\mathcal{J}^{\infty}E\to F.

Proof.

Each point of
\mathcal{J}^{\infty}E
is an infinite jet class of a section of
E
at a point, so it follows that
\phi
is surjective on points, or, more precisely, that
J^{\infty}_{M}\left(\ast\right)
is surjective. However, both
\mathcal{J}^{\infty}E
and
F,
are concrete sheaves, so any differential operator (i.e. any map)
D:\mathcal{J}^{\infty}E\to F
is uniquely determined by

D\left(\ast\right):\mathcal{J}^{\infty}E\left(\ast\right)\to F\left(\ast\right),

hence
D=D^{\prime}
if and only if

D\left(\ast\right)=D^{\prime}\left(\ast\right).

Now suppose that
D\circ J^{\infty}_{M}=D^{\prime}\circ J^{\infty}_{M},
then

D\left(\ast\right)\circ J^{\infty}_{M}\left(\ast\right)=D^{\prime}\left(\ast\right)\circ J^{\infty}_{M}\left(\ast\right),

and since
J^{\infty}_{M}\left(\ast\right)
is an epimorphism of sets, this is implies that
D\left(\ast\right)=D^{\prime}\left(\ast\right),
and hence
D=D^{\prime}.

∎

Corollary 12.29
.

If
E\to M
is a surjective submersion of manifolds, and let
F\to M
be such that
F
is a concrete sheaf (aka diffeological space), then any differential operator

B:\mathbf{\Gamma}_{M}\left(E\right)\to\mathbf{\Gamma}_{M}\left(F\right)

is uniquely determined by its value on the point, i.e. the map of sets

B\left(\ast\right):\Gamma_{M}\left(E\right)\to\Gamma_{M}\left(F\right).

Proof.

This follows immediately from the proof of Proposition
12.28
since
B\left(\ast\right)
uniquely determines
D\left(\ast\right)
.
∎

13.
Monadic and comonadic descent

Let
\mathscr{C}
be an
\infty
-category. Recall that a
monad
on
\mathscr{C}
is a monoid object in the monoidal
\infty
-category
\operatorname{Fun}\left(\mathscr{C},\mathscr{C}\right),
with monoidal product given by composition.

Recall that given an adjunction

\textstyle{\mathscr{D}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{R}
\textstyle{\mathscr{C}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces,}
\scriptstyle{L}

it produces a monad
T:=RL
on the category
\mathscr{C},
and a comonad
C:=LR
on
\mathscr{D}.
Given any object
X\in\mathscr{D},

RX
carries the canonical structure of
T
-algebra. This produces a canonical functor

k:\mathscr{D}\to\mathbf{Alg}_{T}\left(\mathscr{C}\right)

which commutes over
\mathscr{C}.
The adjunction is called
monadic
if
k
is an equivalence of
\infty
-categories. Dually, given an object
Y\in\mathscr{C},
the object
LY
carries the canonical structure of a
C
-coalgebra, and the adjunction is called
comonadic
if the induced functor

\mathscr{C}\to\mathbf{CoAlg}_{C}\left(\mathscr{D}\right)

is an equivalence.

We have the following theorem characterizing when this happens:

Theorem 13.1

(
\infty
-Categorical Barr-Beck Theorem)
.

[
20
, Theorem 4.7.3.5]

An adjunction

\textstyle{\mathscr{C}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{G}
\textstyle{\mathscr{D}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{F}

is monadic, if and only if

i)

The functor
G:\mathscr{D}\to\mathscr{C}
is conservative

ii)

If

V^{\bullet}:\Delta^{op}\to\mathscr{D}

is a simplicial object of
\mathscr{D}
which is
G
-split, then a colimit for
V^{\bullet}
exists and
G
preserves it.

Definition 13.2
.

For a monad
T
on an
\infty
-category
\mathscr{C},
the
Kleisli category
of
T
is the full subcategory
\mathbf{Kl}\left(T\right)
of
\mathbf{Alg}_{T}\left(\mathscr{C}\right)
on the free
T
-algebras, i.e. the essential image of the left adjoint
F
to the forgetful functor

U:\mathbf{Alg}_{T}\left(\mathscr{C}\right)\to\mathscr{C}.

The objects of
\mathbf{Kl}\left(T\right)
may identified with the objects of
\mathscr{C},
and under this identification the morphisms
X\to Y
correspond to morphism
X\to TY
in
\mathscr{C}.
The composition of two such morphisms

f:X\to TY

and

g:Y\to TZ

is given as the composition

X\stackrel{{\scriptstyle f}}{{\longrightarrow}}TY\stackrel{{\scriptstyle Tg}}{{\longrightarrow}}T^{2}Z\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mu_{Z}}}{{\longrightarrow}}TZ,

where

\mu:T^{2}\to T

is the monad product.

Note that since the essential image of
F
factors through
\mathbf{Kl}\left(T\right),
there is an induced adjunction

\textstyle{\mathbf{Kl}\left(T\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{U_{\mathbf{Kl}}}
\textstyle{\mathscr{C}.\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{F_{\mathbf{Kl}}}

We make an analogous (dual) definition of
\mathbf{Kl}\left(C\right)
is the case of a comonad, where the morphisms
X\to Y
correspond to morphism
CX\to Y
in the underlying
\infty
-category.

The following lemma gives as useful description of the
\infty
-category of algebras for a monad in terms of its Kleisli category:

Lemma 13.3
.

Given a monad
T
on an
\infty
-category
\mathscr{C},
there is a pullback diagram of
\infty
-categories

\textstyle{\mathbf{Alg}_{T}\left(\mathscr{C}\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Psh\left(\mathbf{Kl}\left(T\right)\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\left(F_{\mathbf{Kl}}\right)^{\ast}}
\textstyle{\mathscr{C}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{y}
\textstyle{\Psh\left(\mathscr{C}\right).}

Proof.

Since
\mathbf{Kl}\left(T\right)
is locally small, the above pullback remains unchanged under change to a larger universe. Make this change if necessary, so that
\mathbf{Kl}\left(T\right)
is essentially small with respect to this universe. Consider the functor

\left(F_{\mathbf{Kl}}\right)^{*}:\Psh\left(\mathbf{Kl}\left(T\right)\right)\to\Psh\left(\mathscr{C}\right).

It has a left adjoint

\left(F_{\mathbf{Kl}}\right)_{!}=\operatorname{Lan}_{F_{\mathbf{Kl}}}\left(\mspace{3mu}\cdot\mspace{3mu}\right)

and a right adjoint

\left(F_{\mathbf{Kl}}\right)_{*}=\operatorname{Ran}_{F_{\mathbf{Kl}}}\left(\mspace{3mu}\cdot\mspace{3mu}\right)

given by global left and right Kan extension respectively. Similarly, there is a sequence of adjunctions

\left(U_{\mathbf{Kl}}\right)_{!}\dashv\left(U_{\mathbf{Kl}}\right)^{*}\dashv\left(U_{\mathbf{Kl}}\right)_{*}.

Since
F_{\mathbf{Kl}}\dashv U_{\mathbf{Kl}},
it readily follows that

\left(F_{\mathbf{Kl}}\right)_{!}\simeq\left(U_{\mathbf{Kl}}\right)^{*}

and

\left(F_{\mathbf{Kl}}\right)^{*}\simeq\left(U_{\mathbf{Kl}}\right)_{!}.

Since

\left(F_{\mathbf{Kl}}\right)_{!}\dashv\left(F_{\mathbf{Kl}}\right)^{*}\simeq\left(U_{\mathbf{Kl}}\right)_{!},

it follows that the induced monad from the above adjunction can be identified with

\left(U_{\mathbf{Kl}}\right)_{!}\circ\left(F_{\mathbf{Kl}}\right)_{!}\simeq T_{!}.

We also have the description

T_{!}\simeq\operatorname{Lan}_{y}\left(y\circ T\right),

and so
T_{!}
is the unique colimit preserving endofunctor of
\Psh\left(\mathscr{C}\right)
which satisfies

T_{!}y\left(C\right)\simeq y\left(TC\right)

for all
